It compares the class with the golden file `OpsOperations.java`, checks the registry entries, code index and auth schemes, regenerates with model files in another order (identical output), and compiles everything with `javac --release 8` against the runtime module: loading `OpsOperations` loads no partition class, `inputSchema()` then loads it, schemas are the `<Svc>ServiceSchemas` and `SchemaIndex` instances, `Unit` resolves to `PreludeSchemas.UNIT`, `resolveCode` follows first-wins and `:` suffixes, aliases and non-closure errors are absent, and each lazy supplier returns the error's own schema (the registry checks the ID before failing on the missing shape class). An alias whose owner does not own its class fails. `PrototypeErrorRegistryTest` builds SQS, Route 53, SNS, CloudWatch and DynamoDB through the Codegen_Pipeline and checks that the registry holds exactly the intermediate model's exception shapes and that every legacy error code (the intermediate model's `errorCode`, and for SQS, SNS and CloudWatch every C2J `error.code` a legacy awsQuery client would register, keeping the last declared shape per class) resolves, code index first and shape name second, to the shape of the legacy class; for CloudWatch it also compiles the generated classes and checks `resolveCode("ResourceNotFound")` is `DashboardNotFoundError` and `LimitExceeded` is `LimitExceededFault`. `SchemaGeneratorTasksTest` checks the file is written. The seven prototype modules regenerate and compile, and `LegacyRouteParityTest` passes.

### Schema serde members of generated model classes (task 3.7)

**Sources.** Design A.6 and OD-1, OD-14. smithy-java 1.7.0 `codegen/codegen-core` (`StructureGenerator`, `SerializerMemberGenerator`, `DeserializerGenerator`, `SharedSerdeGenerator`, `EnumGenerator`) for the shape of the generated members; the classes themselves are the SDK's existing generated classes with members added, so nothing is ported line by line. Racecar was read for reference only (OD-16).

**Target.** `codegen`: `poet.schema.SchemaSerdeModel` (which classes get the members and how their members map to the Root_Schema), `poet.schema.SharedSerdeSpec` (`<Svc>SharedSerde`), `poet.schema.ValueKind` (write and read calls per Java type), package-private `poet.model.SchemaSerdeClassSpec` (the class and builder members), plus changes to `AwsServiceModel`, `ModelBuilderSpecs`, `ModelMethodOverrides`, `EnumClass`/`AbstractEnumClass`, `SchemasGenerator`, `SchemaCodegen`, `OperationsClassSpec`, `GeneratorTaskParams`, `ModelClassGeneratorTasks` and `SchemaGeneratorTasks`. `core/schema`: `SchemaUtils` (`@SdkProtectedApi`) and a `ToStringSerializer` change. `sdk-core`: `software.amazon.awssdk.core.schema.SdkFieldsFromSchema` and `SchemaSerdeSupport` (both `@SdkProtectedApi`, because generated code in service modules calls them) and `core.schema.internal.SdkFieldsExtension` (`@SdkInternalApi`, registered in `META-INF/services/software.amazon.awssdk.schema.SchemaExtensionProvider`). Generated per schema-enabled service: `<Svc>SharedSerde` (package-private, model package).

| Design / task | Implementation | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| Every structure, union and modeled exception class implements `SerializableStruct` | Every class whose Smithy shape, members and reachable structures schema serde supports (`SchemaSerdeModel`). Excluded, keeping the previous generated code and a schema without builder supplier: event streams and events, classes with streaming, document, `BigInteger` or non-string-key map members, members with the `ignoreDataTypeConversionFailures`, `attachPayloadTraitToMember` or `modelMarshallerDefaultValueSupplier` customizations, a class whose members differ from its Smithy shape's, a Smithy shape that two classes represent, and any class that reaches an excluded structure. In the prototypes only DynamoDB's `Enable`/`DisableKinesisStreamingDestinationRequest`/`Response` are excluded (both operations use the shapes `KinesisStreamingDestinationInput`/`Output`); every other structure, union, exception and enum of the seven services has the members. DynamoDB Streams' own classes, generated from a model without schemas, are unchanged | Documents (Legacy_Document adapters, B.4), streams (A.13) and `BigInteger` have no runtime support yet. The excluded customizations put `SdkField` traits on a member that the Schema cannot carry, so `SdkFieldsFromSchema` could not keep the legacy behavior. One Root_Schema can only name one `shapeClass` |
| Request and response classes for a `smithy.api#Unit` input or output | Get a `SCHEMA` of their own: `Schema.structureBuilder(ShapeId.fromParts(<service namespace>, "<ClassName>"), UnitTypeTrait.INSTANCE)` with their builder supplier and class, in the class (not in a partition) | Task 1.27 left them without a schema. `PreludeSchemas.UNIT` cannot name the class, and two classes would share it. `<Svc>Operations` uses the class's `SCHEMA` |
| Union members that target `smithy.api#Unit` (fix after task 3.33 found that `smithy-protocol-tests` no longer generated) | The IntermediateModel's synthesized `Unit` class gets a `SCHEMA` of its own, `Schema.structureBuilder(ShapeId.fromParts("smithy.api", "Unit"), UnitTypeTrait.INSTANCE)` with its builder supplier and class, and member schemas that target `smithy.api#Unit` reference `Unit.SCHEMA` instead of `PreludeSchemas.UNIT` when that class has schema serde members. The union member reads and writes like any structure member (`writeStruct` with the empty `Unit`, `Unit.newShapeBuilder().deserializeMember(...)`); the union's public API (for a member `Nothing`: `nothing()`, `fromNothing(Unit)`, `Type.NOTHING`) is unchanged | In task 3.7 the `Unit` class looked up a partition field, which the prelude shape does not have, so codegen failed with `Shape smithy.api#Unit has no schema` (restJson1 `RestJson` unions). `PreludeSchemas.UNIT` has no builder, so the derived `SdkField` constructor the legacy unmarshallers call and `assertMemberTargetIs` would fail on it. smithy-java does the same: unit members target `Unit.SCHEMA` and write `Unit.getInstance()`. Referencing `Unit.SCHEMA` from a partition initializes the `Unit` class, whose static initializer touches no partition |
| `sdkSchema()` (OD-1); member constants `M_<NAME>` | Same; constants are `private static final Schema M_<SCHEMA_FIELD_NAME> = SCHEMA.member("<Name>")`, suffixed `_2`, … on a clash. `sdkSchema`, `serialize`, `serializeMembers`, `getMemberValue` and `newShapeBuilder` are reserved POJO method names in `DefaultSmithyNamingStrategy` | Requirement 16.1 (CloudWatch `InsightRule.schema()` stays) |
| `newShapeBuilder()` returns `BuilderImpl` | `static ShapeBuilder<X> newShapeBuilder()` (package-private) | Same entry point for `Schema.builderSupplier(X::newShapeBuilder)`, the `<Svc>SharedSerde` consumers and nested-structure reads; `BuilderImpl` stays out of the signature |
| Builder fields private | Package-private in schema serde builders | The builder's nested `InnerDeserializer` and the class constructor read and write them without synthetic accessors. `BuilderImpl` itself stays package-private, so no API change |
| `InnerDeserializer` assigns fields directly | Same, including the unmodifiable collections that `<Svc>SharedSerde` builds; except unions, whose fluent setters track the set variant, and the `message` of an exception, which goes through `AwsServiceException.Builder.message` | A union's `type` is maintained by its setters. Unknown members never reach the consumer; a union that saw only unknown members builds `UNKNOWN_TO_SDK_VERSION` (the builder's initial type), Requirement 6.11 |
| `SharedSerde` with static list and map writers | One nested class per list or map shape reached from a schema serde member: a static `INSTANCE` that is both the `BiConsumer` writer and the `ListMemberConsumer`/`MapMemberConsumer`, a static `read(Schema, ShapeDeserializer)` that pre-sizes through `SchemaSerdeSupport.newList`/`newMap` (`containerSize()` capped at `containerPreAllocationLimit()`, insertion-ordered maps) and returns an unmodifiable collection, and for a map with list or map values a static `ValueWriter`. Null elements and values are written with `writeNull` and read back as null | Requirement 6.7 and design D.3: no capturing lambda per member. Sparse collections round-trip |
| `serializeMembers` guards collections with `instanceof SdkAutoConstructList`/`Map` | Same; blobs are written as `asByteBuffer()`, enums as their wire string, an exception's `message` from `rawMessage()` | Requirements 6.5, 6.6. `rawMessage()` is the modeled message without the details suffix of `getMessage()` |
| `getMemberValue`/`setMemberValue` throw for a non-member | `switch (memberIndex())` with `SchemaUtils.validateSameMember(expected, actual, value)`, which accepts the same instance or a member with the same ShapeId and member index; the default case throws `SchemaUtils.notAMember(SCHEMA, member)` (`IllegalArgumentException`). `setMemberValue` calls the builder's fluent setter (the defensive copier runs) | Requirement 6.10; a member of a Schema built by another copy of the class (another class loader) is still accepted. `ShapeBuilder.setMemberValue`'s default would also throw, but the generated default names the shape |
| `toString()` delegates to `ToStringSerializer` | Same for classes that had a generated `toString` (exceptions keep `Throwable.toString`), except a class with a sensitive list or map member, which keeps the previous generated `toString`. `ToStringSerializer` writes a schema with a `shapeClass` with the class's simple name and member names whose first character is upper-cased | The previous `toString` wrote the redaction placeholder for a sensitive collection even when it was never set, which `serializeMembers` does not write; keeping the old body keeps the output identical (in the prototypes only SNS `ListPhoneNumbersOptedOutResponse`, whose `phoneNumbers` list is sensitive). The class name and capitalized member names are what `ToString.builder("<ClassName>").add("<MemberName>", …)` wrote |
| `sdkFields()`/`sdkFieldNameToField()` from `SdkFieldsFromSchema.fields(SCHEMA)` (OD-14) | `<Svc>SharedSerde.SDK_FIELDS.fields(SCHEMA)`: `SdkFieldsFromSchema` is an immutable settings object (`WireNaming` `MEMBER`/`JSON`/`XML`/`EC2` from the protocol, `HttpBindingMode` `NONE`/`REQUEST`/`RESPONSE`, `requiredTraits` from `requiredTraitValidationEnabled`); `<Svc>SharedSerde` holds one instance per binding mode, and REST protocols use the request and response modes for operation inputs and outputs/errors. Derived fields are cached on the schema through `SdkFieldsExtension` (first settings win; other settings derive uncached). Location names: `jsonName`/`xmlName`, else the HTTP binding name, else the member name; EC2 marshalling names capitalize. Error `message` members and streaming members are left out | OD-14 says the location names come from the protocol settings; the legacy generator also applied HTTP bindings only to operation inputs and outputs, so nested shapes ignore them. The settings object keeps the static API out of the protocol switch |
| Response metadata and `sdkHttpResponse` are set on the builder before `build()` | `SchemaSerdeSupport.buildResponse(ShapeBuilder<T>, SdkHttpResponse)` sets `sdkHttpResponse` on a builder that is an `SdkResponse.Builder` and builds; the caller sets `AwsResponse.Builder.responseMetadata` on the same builder first. Generated response builders are both | `sdk-core` cannot see `AwsResponse` (`aws-core`). Nothing calls it before the new path's protocol steps (tasks 3.21 and 3.24); the CloudWatch module test checks both values reach the built response |
| — | `SchemaSerdeSupport.toSdkBytes(ByteBuffer)` wraps a heap buffer that spans its whole array without copying and copies any other buffer | Design A.6 "Blobs" |
| Schemas reference `X::newShapeBuilder` and `X.class` | `SchemasGenerator` emits `.builderSupplier(X::newShapeBuilder).shapeClass(X.class)` for each Root_Schema with a schema serde class, and passes the enum class to `createEnum`; enums with a generated class implement `SmithyEnum` with `public static final Schema SCHEMA`, `getValue()` (null for `UNKNOWN_TO_SDK_VERSION`) and `sdkSchema()`. An enum with a constant named `SCHEMA` is left as it is | Initializing a partition initializes no model class; the class literal and the method reference do not either. No prototype service has an int enum; an int enum's class (if the IntermediateModel gives it one) is left as it is, so nothing implements `SmithyIntEnum` yet (Requirement 6.9's int enum half) |
| `<Svc>Operations` typed with the model classes | `ApiOperation<GetQueueUrlRequest, GetQueueUrlResponse>` with `() -> GetQueueUrlRequest.SCHEMA` suppliers when both classes have schema serde members, else `ApiOperation<SerializableStruct, SerializableStruct>` with partition fields as before (DynamoDB's Kinesis streaming destination operations). The class stays in the model package | Task 3.15's hand-off. The supplier still initializes the class only when the schema is requested |
| — | The Schema_Generator is prepared once per service in `GeneratorTaskParams.getSchemaCodegen()` and shared by the schema and model class tasks. A service that shares the model package of a schema-enabled service (DynamoDB Streams) does not generate the classes that service generates (`CodeGenerator` passes `shareModelsTarget` to `GeneratorTaskParams`) | The model classes read which of them get the members from the prepared generator. Previously both services wrote the same shared classes; the sharer's model has no schemas, so its copy would overwrite the schema serde one |
| — | `LegacyRouteParity` (codegen-maven-plugin tests) re-renders the pipeline's model classes that have schema serde members as the generator renders them without those members, from the same IntermediateModel and in generation order, before comparing, and ignores the pipeline-only `*/model/<X>SharedSerde.java`. The expected-difference files are unchanged | Only the pipeline route generates the members, so comparing them with the legacy route would list every model class (about 220,000 lines for the seven modules). The test's purpose, that the pipeline's IntermediateModel produces the legacy route's code, is unchanged; the members themselves are covered below |

**Tests.** `SchemaSerdeModelClassTest` (`codegen`) generates a restJson1 model with a request (label, header, query, `jsonName`, map of lists, list, enum, timestamp, blob, nested structure), a recursive structure with a sensitive member, a union with a `smithy.api#Unit` member (golden `unit.java`; the compiled test checks the member targets `Unit.SCHEMA`, builds, writes and reads through `setMemberValue`/`serializeMembers`, and the derived `SdkField` constructor builds a `Unit`), an exception with `message`, a `Unit` output and an enum, and compares the classes, `DemoSharedSerde` and `DemoOperations` with golden files under `poet/{model,common,schema}/schemaserde/`; existing golden files are unchanged. It compiles the whole generated model package with `javac --release 8` (`ModelPackageCompiler`) and checks `SCHEMA`/`shapeClass`, builders through `setMemberValue`, `getMemberValue` and its failure, `serializeMembers` order and unset-collection skipping, `toString`, derived `sdkFields` locations, the kept `toString` for a sensitive collection, and `SmithyEnum`. `PrototypeErrorRegistryTest` now compiles the CloudWatch model package and checks that `ErrorRegistry.getSchema`/`createBuilder` return the exception class's schema and builder. `SchemaUtilsTest`, the `ToStringSerializerTest` case for generated-class names, `SdkFieldsFromSchemaTest` (member order, caching, binding modes, list and map traits, scalar traits, EC2 names, message member, getter and setter) and `SchemaSerdeSupportTest` cover the runtime parts. `services/cloudwatch` gains `model.SchemaSerdeModelClassTest` (with `cbor-codec` as a test dependency): `serializeMembers` order, null and unset skipping, an explicitly empty list and an undefined enum value; `getMemberValue`/`setMemberValue`; CBOR round trips of `PutMetricDataRequest` (nested structures, double lists with extreme values, millisecond timestamps), `MetricAlarm`, a blob response and an exception; `deserialize` keeping preset members; an unknown member skipped; `toString` in the previous format; derived `sdkFields` (names, locations, marshalling types, constructor, getters and setters, `equalsBySdkFields`); and `buildResponse`. Outside the test suite, every model class of the seven modules was compared with the class of the build before this change (same `sdkFields` and their traits, location names, constructors, `sdkFieldNameToField`, values read back through the fields after setting every field, `toString` with and without values, `equals`/`hashCode` after `toBuilder`): 1,036 classes, no difference. `LegacyRouteParityTest` passes with unchanged expected differences, and the CloudWatch, SQS and DynamoDB module tests pass on the legacy pipeline.

## `core/schema` (Runtime_Schema_Module, tasks 1.4, 1.7, 1.8, 1.11, 1.14, 1.17, 3.1 and 3.14)

**Sources.** smithy-java 1.7.0 (`/Users/alexwoo/java/smithy-java`, commit `142eecf4d`), module `core`. smithy-java has no trait classes of its own; it stores smithy-model traits in schemas. The trait ports therefore follow smithy-model 1.74.0 (`/Users/alexwoo/java/smithy`, commit `ec88e02aa1`): `smithy-model` (`software.amazon.smithy.model.traits`, `ShapeId`, `ShapeType`), `smithy-aws-traits` and `smithy-protocol-traits`.

**Target.** Module `schema`, packages `software.amazon.awssdk.schema` (`ShapeId`, `ShapeType`, `TraitKey`, `TraitMap`), `software.amazon.awssdk.schema.traits`, `software.amazon.awssdk.schema.serde` (`SerializationException`, the serde interfaces and, from task 3.1, the serde helpers) and `software.amazon.awssdk.schema.serde.document`.

### `ShapeId`, `ShapeIdSyntaxException`, `ShapeType` (task 1.4)

Checked against smithy-model's `ShapeId.java` in task 1.7: `compareTo` (case-insensitive order with a case-sensitive tie-break) and the identifier and namespace grammar (`(1*"_" (ALPHA / DIGIT) / ALPHA) *(ALPHA / DIGIT / "_")`, dot-separated namespaces, no trailing `.`) match smithy-model. No change was needed.

| Source | Port | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| Design A.1 describes the grammar as `[A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_]*` per segment | Smithy IDL 2.0 grammar, as smithy-model implements it: a leading `_` must be followed by a letter or digit, so `_` and `__` alone are rejected | Requirement 2.8 ("characters outside the Smithy identifier grammar"); the design's regex is a simplification |
| `fromOptionalNamespace(String, String)` | Ported, same behavior (absolute IDs parse as-is, relative IDs resolve against the default namespace, null namespace means the ID must be absolute) | Needed for relative `__type` discriminators (Requirement 20.3) and error codes (Requirement 23.3). The design's Porting_Map row said it was not ported; that row is out of date |
| `ShapeId implements ToShapeId, Comparable<ShapeId>` | `implements Comparable<ShapeId>, Serializable`, with `readResolve` that re-validates the parts and rebuilds the cached string and hash | SDK addition: SDK model and exception types are `Serializable`, and a `ShapeId` held by them must serialize. `ToShapeId` is a smithy-model type (Requirement 1.6) |
| `toShapeId()`, `fromRelative`, `fromNode`, public static `isValidIdentifier`/`isValidNamespace`, `getMember(): Optional`, `withMember`, `withoutMember` | `toShapeId`, `fromRelative`, `fromNode` and the public validators not ported; the others kept | Runtime subset; validators stay private |
| Striped LRU parse cache (`ShapeIdFactory`) | Not ported; each `from` call parses | Generated code uses `fromParts` with constants, so parsing is off the hot path; a cache can be added if a benchmark shows a need |
| `ShapeType.SET` | Not ported | Sets are `LIST` with `uniqueItems` in IDL 2.0 (design A.1) |

### `Document` core (task 1.7)

Task 1.7 ports the schema-free core of smithy-java `Document`, `Documents` and `DocumentEqualsFlags`, because the trait factories take their values as documents. The full port (design B.1–B.3, phase 7) adds schemas, structures, discriminators, `Document.of(SerializableShape)`, `asShape`, `serialize`/`serializeContents`, `ofNumber` and `ofObject` as default methods and new factories, so it extends this code instead of replacing it.

| Source | Port | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| `serializeContents`, `discriminator`, `expectDiscriminator`, `createDeserializer`, `deserializeInto`, `asShape`, `asDataStream`, `asEventStream`, `of(Schema, DataStream)`, `of(Schema, EventStream)`, `of(SerializableShape)`, `ofNumber`, `ofObject`, `compare` | Not ported yet (`Document extends SerializableShape` and `serialize` were added in task 1.17; see below) | Their types (`Schema`, `ShapeSerializer`, `ShapeBuilder`, `DataStream`, `EventStream`) land in tasks 1.11, 1.17, 3.1 and A.13; `ofNumber` waits for the Requirement 18.5 promotion rule (below). Phase 7 adds them |
| `asStringMap()` | `asMap()` | Document SEP name used by Requirements 18.2 and 18.6 (design B.1) |
| `Documents` records holding a `Schema` | Package-private final classes without a schema; `IntegralDocument` (byte/short/integer/long held as a `long`) and `FloatingPointDocument` (float/double held as a `double`) replace the six primitive records | No `Schema` type yet; records are Java 16+ (Requirement 1.4). Widening to `long`/`double` is exact, so every coercion returns the same value as from the original type |
| Record `equals`/`hashCode` (component-based, including the schema) on most documents; `Document.equals` only on some | Every implementation uses `Document.equals(this, o)` and the new `Document.hash(Document)` | smithy-java's `hashCode` is inconsistent with its `equals`. `Document.hash` is public so other document implementations (JSON and CBOR documents, phase 7) can stay consistent |
| `FLOAT`/`DOUBLE` equality with `==` | `Double.compare(...) == 0`: `NaN` equals `NaN`, `0.0` does not equal `-0.0` | Consistent with `Double.hashCode`; protocol tests treat `NaN` as equal to `NaN` (Requirement 32.5) |
| Map equality looks up each key with `get` | Also checks `containsKey` | A null value no longer equals an absent key |
| `asBigDecimal()` on byte/short/integer/long: `BigDecimal.valueOf((double) v)`; on bigInteger: `valueOf(doubleValue())`; `asBigInteger()` on bigDecimal: `valueOf(longValue())` | `BigDecimal.valueOf(long)`, `new BigDecimal(BigInteger)`, `BigDecimal.toBigInteger()` | The source loses precision for longs above 2^53 and for big values. Floats and doubles keep the source conversions (`BigDecimal.valueOf(double)`, `BigInteger.valueOf((long) v)`) |
| `asBigDecimal()` on NaN or infinity throws `NumberFormatException` | Throws `SerializationException` naming the type | Coercion failures are `SerializationException` (Requirement 18.7) |
| `getMemberNames()` returns `Set.copyOf(keySet())` (unordered) | The map's unmodifiable key set, in entry order | `Set.copyOf` is Java 10+; Requirement 18.8 requires source order |
| Factories store the given list, map and buffer as-is; `asBlob` returns the stored buffer | Lists and maps are wrapped once in unmodifiable views (not copied); the blob document keeps a duplicate of the buffer and `asBlob()` returns a fresh duplicate; object-valued factories reject null with `NullPointerException` | Callers cannot mutate a document through its accessors, or move the shared buffer's position, at no copy cost |
| `of(boolean)` allocates | Two shared instances | No behavior change |
| `DocumentEqualsFlags` | Same name, with a checkstyle suppression for the plural-name rule | Keeps the smithy-java (and design B.2) name |

`SerializationException` is ported unchanged (`RuntimeException`, three constructors), earlier than task 3.1, because document coercions and trait factories throw it.

### Runtime traits and the trait factory registry (task 1.7)

All classes are in `software.amazon.awssdk.schema.traits` and are `@SdkPublicApi` except `TraitFactories` (`@SdkProtectedApi`, design A.2) and the package-private helpers (`TraitFactoryRegistry`, `BuiltInTraitFactories`, `TraitValues`, `HttpProtocolTrait`, `@SdkInternalApi`).

Typed traits (48), each with a `public static final ShapeId ID` and a built-in factory:

- Annotation traits, each a singleton `INSTANCE`: `RequiredTrait`, `SensitiveTrait`, `SparseTrait`, `IdempotencyTokenTrait`, `StreamingTrait`, `RequiresLengthTrait`, `EventHeaderTrait`, `EventPayloadTrait`, `HostLabelTrait`, `HttpChecksumRequiredTrait`, `XmlAttributeTrait`, `XmlFlattenedTrait`, `HttpLabelTrait`, `HttpQueryParamsTrait`, `HttpPayloadTrait`, `HttpResponseCodeTrait`, `UnitTypeTrait`, `OptionalAuthTrait`, `AwsQueryCompatibleTrait`, `AwsQueryTrait`, `Ec2QueryTrait`, `UnsignedPayloadTrait`.
- String traits: `ErrorTrait`, `MediaTypeTrait`, `XmlNameTrait`, `JsonNameTrait`, `TimestampFormatTrait`, `HttpHeaderTrait`, `HttpQueryTrait`, `HttpPrefixHeadersTrait`, `Ec2QueryNameTrait`.
- Structured traits: `DefaultTrait`, `HttpErrorTrait`, `RetryableTrait`, `EndpointTrait`, `HttpTrait`, `RequestCompressionTrait`, `PaginatedTrait`, `XmlNamespaceTrait`, `AuthTrait`, `HttpChecksumTrait`, `AwsQueryErrorTrait`, `SigV4Trait`.
- Protocol traits: `AwsJson1_0Trait`, `AwsJson1_1Trait`, `RestJson1Trait`, `RestXmlTrait` (base `AwsProtocolTrait`), `Rpcv2CborTrait` (base `Rpcv2ProtocolTrait`).

| Source | Port | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| `software.amazon.smithy.model.traits.Trait` (`toShapeId()`, `toNode()`, `getSourceLocation()`) | `Trait` with `traitId()` and `toDocument()` | No `smithy-model` at runtime (Requirement 1.6); method names from design A.2. Source locations are not kept at runtime |
| `DynamicTrait` | `GenericTrait(ShapeId, Document)` with `value()`; a null value represents a Smithy `null` | Design A.2 and Requirement 2.5 |
| `TraitService` providers discovered through `META-INF/services/software.amazon.smithy.model.traits.TraitService`; duplicates silently resolved | `TraitFactory<T>` (`traitId`, `traitClass`, `create(Document)`), discovered through `ServiceLoader.load(TraitFactory.class, TraitFactory.class.getClassLoader())`. SDK factories are built in, not service-loaded. A second factory for any ID, including an SDK one, fails registry construction with `IllegalStateException` naming the ID and both factory classes | Requirements 2.6, 2.7, 2.10. The registry is built on first use and a failed build is not cached, so every call reports the same error instead of a `NoClassDefFoundError` |
| A provider throws `ExpectationNotMetException` or `SourceException` for a bad value | `SerializationException` whose message names the trait ID. `TraitFactories.create` adds the ID when a third-party factory's message lacks it | Requirement 2.10 |
| Unknown trait ID: smithy-model creates a `DynamicTrait` | `TraitFactories.create` returns a `GenericTrait` | Requirement 2.9 |
| `AnnotationTrait` keeps its `ObjectNode`; equality compares nodes | Singleton subclasses; `toDocument()` is always the empty map; any map value is accepted and its contents dropped; a non-map value is rejected | Design A.2 (generated code allocates nothing). No SDK annotation trait has properties |
| Getters such as `getValue()`, `getCode()`, `getMethod()`, `getHttp()`, `getThrottling()`, `isNoErrorWrapping()` | Same names, return types and `Optional` use | Ported runtime code from smithy-java calls these names |
| `HttpTrait.getUri(): UriPattern`, `EndpointTrait.getHostPrefix(): SmithyPattern` | `String` | Pattern parsing belongs to Http_Binding_Serde (task 7.8) and the host-prefix runtime feature; the pattern types are smithy-model classes |
| Builders (`HttpTrait.builder()`, `PaginatedTrait.builder()`, ...) and `toBuilder()` | Public constructors (`new HttpTrait("GET", "/", 200)`, `new PaginatedTrait(in, out, items, pageSize)` with nulls for absent properties) | Compact generated trait initializers (design A.16); traits are immutable value objects |
| Value equality from `toShapeId()` plus `toNode()` (`AbstractTrait`) | Field-based `equals`/`hashCode` per class; equal values of different classes are not equal | Same result for SDK traits without building a document |
| Protocol traits extend `AwsProtocolTrait` / `Rpcv2ProtocolTrait` | Same public bases, sharing the package-private `HttpProtocolTrait` | Avoids duplicating the `http`/`eventStreamHttp` handling |
| `AwsJson1_0Trait`, `AwsJson1_1Trait` | Same names, with a checkstyle suppression for the type-name rule | Design A.2 and smithy-model names |
| `ErrorTrait` rejects values other than `client` and `server` | Same: the constructor throws `IllegalArgumentException`, the factory `SerializationException` | Matches smithy-model |
| `TimestampFormatTrait.Format` keeps unknown values as `UNKNOWN` | Same | Forward compatibility, as in smithy-model |
| `AuthTrait` keeps scheme IDs as a `LinkedHashSet` | Same, exposed through `getValueSet()` and `getValues()` | — |
| Integer-valued properties (`httpError`, `http.code`, `awsQueryError.httpResponseCode`) read with `intValue()` | Accept any numeric document whose value is an integer in `int` range; reject others | A truncated status code is a model error (Requirement 2.10) |
| `RequestCompressionTrait.SUPPORTED_COMPRESSION_ALGORITHMS`, `HttpChecksumTrait.getChecksumLocationName`, `PaginatedTrait.resolvePath`/`merge` | Not ported | Model-time helpers, some of which need `smithy-model` shapes; runtime components that need them add their own |

Traits outside design A.2's list (for example `aws.auth#sigv4a`, `smithy.api#httpBearerAuth`) have no typed class yet and are created as `GenericTrait`s.

Not ported in task 1.7: `TraitKey` (task 1.8; nothing here precludes it — every typed trait is a distinct class).

### `TraitKey` and `TraitMap` (task 1.8)

`TraitKey` is `@SdkPublicApi` in `software.amazon.awssdk.schema`. `TraitMap` is package-private (`@SdkInternalApi`) in the same package, as in smithy-java, so `Schema` (task 1.11) can hold it and read `TraitKey.id` directly.

| Source | Port | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| `TraitKey<T extends software.amazon.smithy.model.traits.Trait>` | `TraitKey<T extends software.amazon.awssdk.schema.traits.Trait>` | Requirement 1.6 |
| `ClassValue` pool whose `computeValue` takes the next value of an `AtomicInteger` | Same `ClassValue` pool, but the index comes from a per-class index map (`WeakHashMap<Class<?>, Integer>` under a lock) that `computeValue` consults | `ClassValue` may call `computeValue` more than once when threads race on a class's first lookup and keeps one result. In smithy-java each discarded result consumes an index, leaving gaps. Requirement 4.1 requires contiguous indexes. The map holds only the index, so it does not keep a trait class loader reachable; it is consulted only on a class's first lookup. The design's "single `AtomicInteger`" is replaced by an `int` guarded by the same lock |
| Pool fields declared before the constants | Pool fields in a private nested holder class | Checkstyle `DeclarationOrder` puts public constants first; the holder initializes before the first constant is created, whatever the textual order |
| Constants for the smithy-model traits smithy-java reads (`REQUIRED_TRAIT`, ..., `IDEMPOTENCY_TOKEN`, `READ_ONLY_TRAIT`, `DOCUMENTATION_TRAIT`, `CORS_TRAIT`, ...) | One `public static final` constant per SDK typed trait, 48 in all, each named `<TRAIT>_TRAIT` (so `IDEMPOTENCY_TOKEN_TRAIT`). No constants for `documentation`, `uniqueItems`, `length`, `range`, `pattern`, `enum`, `readonly`, `idempotent` and `cors` | Requirement 4.5. The SDK has no runtime classes for those traits: the Trait_Inclusion_Policy excludes them (Requirements 9.3, 9.4, OD-13) |
| Constants in an arbitrary order | Declared from member traits to service traits | A schema's trait array is sized by the largest key index it holds, so common member traits (`required` = 0, `default`, `sensitive`, `jsonName`, ...) get the smallest indexes |
| `get(Class)` accepts any trait class | `get(null)` throws `NullPointerException`; `get(GenericTrait.class)` throws `IllegalArgumentException` | All generic traits share one class, so a class key cannot tell them apart (see the next row) |
| — | `toString()` naming the class and index | Debugging aid |
| Untyped traits are `DynamicTrait`s keyed by class, so two on one shape collide (DC-26) | Generic traits are stored apart from typed traits and looked up by trait shape ID through `TraitMap.getGeneric(ShapeId)`, with no `TraitKey`. `Schema` exposes this lookup in task 1.11 | Design A.3 proposed `TraitKey.generic(ShapeId)` with its own pooled index per trait ID. That pool would grow with every distinct trait ID of every model a Dynamic_Client loads and never shrink, and each generic trait would widen the trait array of every schema holding it. Tasks.md 1.8 specifies the `TraitKey`-free path; it still makes each generic trait independently retrievable (DC-26) and keeps `TraitKey` class-keyed, as Requirement 4.1 states. Generic lookups are a linear scan of the schema's few generic traits with no allocation; they are off the serde hot path because every default-included trait is typed |
| `TraitMap.create(Trait...)`: last trait of a class wins | Same for typed traits; for generic traits, the last one with a trait ID wins. A null array or element throws `NullPointerException` naming the index | — |
| `TraitMap.withMemberTraits` merges the typed arrays | Same, and member generic traits replace target generic traits with the same ID | Member traits win over target traits (Requirement 3.7) for generic traits too |
| No trait collection view | `TraitMap.traits()`: an unmodifiable `Collection<Trait>` with each trait once, typed traits in key index order and then generic traits in first-seen order, built on first use and cached | Requirement 4.6; `Schema.traits()` (task 1.11) returns it. There are no per-trait getters (Requirement 4.7) |

### Schema hierarchy, `SchemaBuilder`, `PreludeSchemas`, `SchemaIndex` (task 1.11)

All in `software.amazon.awssdk.schema`. Public: `Schema` (abstract, package-private constructors), `SchemaBuilder`, `PreludeSchemas`, `SchemaIndex`, `MemberLookup` (`@SdkPublicApi`). Package-private (`@SdkInternalApi`): `RootSchema`, `MemberSchema`, `DeferredRootSchema`, `DeferredMemberSchema`, `ResolvedRootSchema`, `MemberSchemaBuilder`, and `SchemaBuilder.Members` (the built member array, list, name map and required-member summary that root schemas share).

| Source | Port | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| `abstract sealed class Schema permits ...` | Abstract class with package-private constructors and a package-private abstract `memberData()`, so only same-package subclasses exist | Sealed types are Java 17 (Requirement 1.4; design porting conventions) |
| `SchemaBuilder.sortMembers` moves required-without-default members first; `memberIndex` follows the sorted order | No sorting: `members()` and `memberIndex()` follow declaration (insertion) order, and the index is fixed when `putMember` is called | Requirement 3.3, OD-2 |
| `requiredStructureMemberBitfield` (0 when more than 64 members are required) and per-member `requiredByValidationBitmask` | `@SdkInternalApi public long requiredMemberMask()`: bit `i` set for a required-without-default structure member at index `i < 64`; package-private `requiredMemberCount()` counts all of them, and the member's package-private `isRequiredByValidation` flag identifies the rest. A member schema returns its target's mask. Non-structures return 0 | With declaration order a required member can sit at any index, so "more than 64 required members" is no longer the only overflow case. The presence tracker (later task, same package) falls back to member flags when `requiredMemberCount() > Long.bitCount(requiredMemberMask())` instead of a `BitSet` (design A.4) |
| Root `memberIndex()` returns 0 | Returns −1; `memberIndex()` and `member(int)` are `@SdkInternalApi` | Requirements 3.2, 3.11 |
| `member(int)` throws `IndexOutOfBoundsException` (via `List.get`); `MemberSchema`/`DeferredMemberSchema` do not override it, so it returns null on members | Returns null for an out-of-range index; member schemas delegate to the target | Matches the source Javadoc ("null if this schema has no such index") and Requirement 3.6 |
| `DeferredMemberSchema` does not delegate `stringEnumValues`/`intEnumValues` | Delegates | Requirement 3.6 |
| `DeferredMemberSchema.memberTarget()` calls `targetBuilder.build()`, building the target on access | Throws `IllegalStateException` naming the target ShapeId and the member when the target's builder has not been built; reads the builder's built (or resolved) schema otherwise | Requirement 3.13 |
| `DeferredRootSchema.resolveInternal` publishes members through a volatile with a benign race (two threads can build two sets of member schemas) | Double-checked locking: member schemas are built once, so every caller sees the same member instances | Generated code compares member schemas with `==` (`SchemaUtils.validateSameMember`); duplicate member instances would break it |
| `DeferredRootSchema.resolve()` creates a new `ResolvedRootSchema` on each call and throws `IllegalStateException` on the second | Idempotent: `SchemaBuilder.resolve` (synchronized) replaces the deferred schema once and every later call, including `build()`, returns the same resolved instance | Safer for the Schema_Converter, which may resolve from more than one path |
| `SchemaBuilder` errors: `IllegalArgumentException` for a wrong list/map member name; `IllegalStateException` for a missing list member, missing map key/value, or memberless union at `build()`; duplicate member names not detected | `IllegalArgumentException` for every invalid definition: duplicate name, wrong list/map member name, missing `member`/`key`/`value` (message names the missing member ID), memberless union, non-aggregate type, a member-qualified builder ID, and a member target that is itself a member. Each message names the shape ID and the member. Changing the builder after `build()` stays `IllegalStateException` | Requirement 3.12, design A.4 |
| `builderSupplier`/`shapeClass` after `build()` silently have no effect | Throw `IllegalStateException` | A frozen builder; the change would otherwise be lost |
| Map key and value members found at positions 0 and 1 | Found by name, once, and cached | With declaration order a builder may add `value` before `key` |
| `builderSupplier(Supplier<ShapeBuilder<?>>)`, `ShapeBuilder<? extends SerializableShape> shapeBuilder()` (throws when absent) | `builderSupplier(Supplier<? extends ShapeBuilder<?>>)`, `ShapeBuilder<? extends SerializableShape> shapeBuilder()` (returns null when absent, and for every member schema) | Narrowed in task 1.17 from the task 1.11 placeholders `Supplier<?>` and `Object`. The `? extends` wildcard also accepts a `Supplier<ConcreteBuilder>` variable; generated method references (`X::newShapeBuilder`) compile either way. Null instead of a throw: Requirement 3.5 |
| Validation state fields (`minLengthConstraint`, `stringPattern`, `uniqueItemsConstraint`, ...) and `ValidationState` | Not ported | No client-side constraint validation (Requirement 9.3, OD-13) |
| `RootSchema` reads string enum values from the legacy `@enum` trait | Not ported | `enum` is excluded from schemas; enum values come from `createEnum` (design A.16) |
| `createEnum`/`createIntEnum` wrap the caller's set | Copy into an unmodifiable `LinkedHashSet` (iteration order kept); null sets and IDs throw `NullPointerException` | Requirement 3.3: a caller's later change must not change the schema |
| `members()` returns the mutable internal `ArrayList` | Unmodifiable list over the member array | Requirement 3.3 |
| `equals` compares type, ID, `TraitMap` (identity, since `TraitMap` has no `equals`) and `members()`; `hashCode` precomputed | Identity `equals`/`hashCode` (not overridden) | The source is identity in practice, and comparing `members()` recurses without end on recursive shapes. Each shape has one schema instance per generated client or conversion |
| No direct lookup for untyped traits | `getGenericTrait(ShapeId)` and `getDirectGenericTrait(ShapeId)` | Task 1.8 gives generic traits no `TraitKey` (DC-26); these are the shape-ID path, with the same member-over-target precedence (Requirements 3.7, 3.8) |
| `getDirectTrait` overridden by member schemas | Final in `Schema`, reading a `directTraits` map that root schemas share with `traits` | Same behavior, no virtual call |
| `traits()` view | Added; returns `TraitMap.traits()` of the merged traits | Requirement 4.6 |
| `getExtension`, extension array and provider holder | Not ported in this task | Ported in task 1.14 (next subsection) |
| `Unit.SCHEMA` (on the `Unit` struct) | Also `PreludeSchemas.UNIT`: an empty structure `smithy.api#Unit` with `UnitTypeTrait` | Design A.4 lists `UNIT` in `PreludeSchemas`; the `Unit` struct (after task 1.17) can reuse it |
| `PreludeSchemas` primitive defaults built from smithy-model `Node`s | `DefaultTrait(Document.of(...))` | Requirement 1.6 |
| `PreludeSchemas` class name | Same, with a checkstyle suppression for the plural-name rule | Keeps the smithy-java and design A.4 name |
| `SchemaIndex` discovers indexes in its static initializer; the combined index's `getSchema` throws `IllegalArgumentException` for an unknown ID; `compose` uses `List.of` | Discovery in a lazy holder on the first `getCombinedSchemaIndex()` call; `getSchema` returns null for unknown IDs; `compose` copies the array and rejects null elements | Loading a generated `<Svc>SchemaIndex` subclass no longer triggers `ServiceLoader`; Requirement 10.10; `List.of` is Java 9 |
| `SchemaBuilder` member list sized by a `switch` expression; `createMembers` uses `Map.of`/`Map.ofEntries`; `Map2` entry set uses `Set.of` | Classic `switch`, `Collections.emptyMap`/`singletonMap`, a sized `HashMap` wrapped unmodifiable, and an unmodifiable `LinkedHashSet` | Java 8 (Requirement 1.4) |

### Schema extensions (task 1.14)

`SchemaExtensionKey` and `SchemaExtensionProvider` are `@SdkPublicApi` in `software.amazon.awssdk.schema`; `Schema.getExtension(SchemaExtensionKey)` is public and final. Discovery lives in the package-private `SchemaExtensionRegistry` (`@SdkInternalApi`). Providers are registered in `META-INF/services/software.amazon.awssdk.schema.SchemaExtensionProvider`.

| Source | Port | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| `SchemaExtensionKey`: `final int id` from a static `AtomicInteger`, public no-arg constructor, package-private `count()` | Same, without `count()`; `toString()` returns `SchemaExtensionKey{id=<n>}` | The registry sizes itself from the provider keys' IDs, so `count()` is unused. Keys have no name, so `toString` identifies a key in the duplicate-provider error (Requirement 5.11) |
| `SchemaExtensionProvider` Javadoc: plain array publication, benign race, "all invocations must produce equivalent results" | Same interface (`key()`, `provide(Schema)`). Javadoc states the publish-once contract below, that concurrent first calls may each invoke the provider, and that `provide` must not rely on locks held by callers | Requirements 5.1, 5.5 |
| Providers discovered in a holder class's static initializer, through `ServiceLoader.load(SchemaExtensionProvider.class, SchemaExtensionProvider.class.getClassLoader())` | Same class loader, but discovered on the first `getExtension` call that misses the cache, once, under a lock (`SchemaExtensionRegistry.instance()`). A failed discovery is not cached, so every later call throws the same `IllegalStateException` instead of a `NoClassDefFoundError` | Requirement 5.2; same pattern as `TraitFactories` (task 1.7) |
| A second provider for a key silently replaces the first | Discovery fails with `IllegalStateException("More than one SchemaExtensionProvider is registered for SchemaExtensionKey{id=n}: <class> and <class>")`. A provider whose `key()` returns null also fails discovery, naming the class | Requirement 5.11 (design A.5) |
| Every schema allocates an `Object[]` of all registered keys in its constructor, prefilled with `NOT_COMPUTED` | One `volatile AtomicReferenceArray<Object>` field per schema, null until the schema's first extension value is published, then allocated once (double-checked under the schema's monitor) with the registry's size. A null element means "not computed"; a private sentinel stands for a cached null | A schema whose extensions are never read costs one reference field and no allocation. Generated clients hold thousands of schemas, and most member schemas are never asked for an extension |
| Plain array reads and writes with a benign race: two threads may compute a slot and each publish its own value, and different callers can receive different instances | Fast path: one volatile field read, a bounds check and one volatile element read (plain loads on x86). On a miss the provider runs outside any lock, then the value is published with `compareAndSet(id, null, value)`; the loser of a race discards its value and returns the winner's. A slot is written once, so every caller and every later call get one instance | Requirements 5.4 and 5.5 (design A.5 "publish-once"). Design A.5 proposed a plain-read fast path with publication under `synchronized`; a plain `Object[]` behind a volatile field is flagged by SpotBugs (`VO_VOLATILE_REFERENCE_TO_ARRAY`), and `AtomicReferenceArray` gives safe publication of the value without relying on providers returning objects with only final fields. The schema's monitor is taken only to allocate the array, once per schema |
| Provider invoked inside `computeExtension` with no lock | Same: invoked outside any lock | A provider can read extensions of other schemas, such as member targets in a recursive graph; computing under the schema's monitor could deadlock two threads walking a cycle in opposite directions. As a result, concurrent first calls can invoke a provider more than once (each call for a schema and key made alone invokes it exactly once, Requirement 5.3) |
| Exception from `provide` | Propagates unchanged; nothing is cached, so the next call invokes the provider again | Requirement 5.10; same as the source, now stated |
| Key with no provider, or with an ID beyond the provider array | Returns null, without caching or locking | Requirement 5.9; same as the source |
| `getExtension(null)` throws an implicit `NullPointerException` | `NullPointerException` naming `key` (`Validate.paramNotNull`) | SDK convention |
| One cache per `Schema` instance | Same. A member schema and its target are different instances with separate caches. `DeferredRootSchema` and the `ResolvedRootSchema` that `resolve()` returns are different instances, so values computed on the deferred schema are not carried over; the member schemas are shared between the two, so member values are kept | Requirements 5.3 and 5.6 are per schema instance. Copying values from the deferred to the resolved schema would hand the resolved schema values computed for another instance (a provider may capture the schema it was given). Generated code and the Schema_Converter use the resolved schema once it exists |

Requirement 5.7 (codecs and Http_Binding_Serde read their metadata through extensions) is met by the codec and protocol tasks that define those extensions (design A.5 table). Schemas from the Schema_Converter (phase 8) go through the same `Schema` constructors, so they share this implementation (Requirement 5.6).

### Serde interfaces (task 1.17)

Runtime interfaces only; no generated class implements them until task 3.7. In `software.amazon.awssdk.schema` (`@SdkPublicApi`): `SerializableShape`, `SerializableStruct`, `ShapeBuilder`, `SmithyEnum`, `SmithyIntEnum`. In `software.amazon.awssdk.schema.serde` (`@SdkPublicApi`): `ShapeSerializer`, `ShapeDeserializer` with its nested `StructMemberConsumer`, `ListMemberConsumer` and `MapMemberConsumer`, and `MapSerializer` (needed by `ShapeSerializer.writeMap`); package-private `NullSerializer` (`@SdkInternalApi`, needed by `ShapeSerializer.nullSerializer()`). Method names, parameter types and return types otherwise match smithy-java 1.7.0 (Requirement 6.1).

| Source | Port | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| `SerializableStruct.schema()`, `ShapeBuilder.schema()` | `sdkSchema()`. `SerializableStruct.serialize` calls `writeStruct(sdkSchema(), this)`; `ShapeBuilder.setMemberValue`'s default message reads `sdkSchema().id()` | OD-1, Requirement 16.1: generated SDK model classes already expose public `schema()` getters for modeled members named `Schema` (CloudWatch `InsightRule.schema()` returns `String`), and Java cannot overload by return type. Deviates from the literal method name in Requirements 6.1 and 6.3 |
| `SmithyEnum` (`getValue()` only), `SmithyIntEnum` (`getValue()` only) | Add abstract `Schema sdkSchema()` | Requirement 6.9 (design A.6): the enum exposes its schema. Named `sdkSchema()` for consistency with OD-1 |
| `ShapeSerializer extends Flushable, AutoCloseable`; `ShapeDeserializer extends AutoCloseable` | `extends Flushable, SdkAutoCloseable` and `extends SdkAutoCloseable` | SDK checkstyle rule: types extend `SdkAutoCloseable` (an `AutoCloseable` whose `close()` throws no checked exception). The source's `close()` overrides already throw nothing, so callers see the same API; Requirement 7.4 still holds |
| `ShapeSerializer.writeDataStream(Schema, DataStream)`, `writeEventStream(Schema, EventStream)`; `ShapeDeserializer.readDataStream(Schema)`, `readEventStream(Schema)` | Not ported yet | `DataStream` and `EventStream` are design A.13 types that do not exist yet. They are default methods in the source (no-op writes, throwing reads), so adding them with A.13 (phase 4, tasks 7.19/7.20) does not break implementations written against this interface |
| `CONTAINER_PRE_ALLOCATION_LIMIT = Integer.parseInt(System.getProperty("smithy.java.serde.container-pre-allocation-limit", "10000"))` | `Integer.getInteger("aws.serde.containerPreAllocationLimit", 10_000)` | SDK system-property naming. `Integer.getInteger` falls back to the default for an unparseable value instead of failing the interface's initialization with `NumberFormatException` |
| `NullSerializer` (package-private, implicit public constructor) | Package-private, `@SdkInternalApi`, private constructor | Singleton; one SDK visibility annotation per type |
| `Document extends SerializableShape` with `default serialize(s) { s.writeDocument(PreludeSchemas.DOCUMENT, this); }` and abstract `serializeContents(ShapeSerializer)` | `Document extends SerializableShape` with the same `serialize` default. `serializeContents` is not added yet | `serialize` is complete as ported (Requirement 18.1: exactly one `writeDocument` call). `serializeContents` would be abstract, and its implementations walk structure documents that do not exist yet; it lands with the full document port (task 11.1/11.5). Within this module only the package-private `Documents` classes implement `Document`, and the interface is unreleased, so phase 7 can add it as abstract (as in the source) |
| Interface Javadoc | Rewritten; `ShapeDeserializer.containerPreAllocationLimit` Javadoc shortened | No behavior change |

Not in this task: `SpecificShapeSerializer`, `SpecificShapeDeserializer`, `InterceptingSerializer`, `ListSerializer`, `ToStringSerializer`, `TimestampFormatter`, `ShapeTranscoder` (task 3.1) and `Codec` (task 3.5).

### Ported smithy-java tests (task 1.18)

**Sources.** smithy-java 1.7.0 `core/src/test/java/software/amazon/smithy/java/core/schema/` (7 files) and, because smithy-java uses smithy-model's `ShapeId` and `ShapeType`, smithy-model 1.74.0 `smithy-model/src/test/java/software/amazon/smithy/model/shapes/ShapeIdTest.java` and `ShapeTypeTest.java`. smithy-java has no unit tests of its own for `TraitKey`, `SchemaBuilder`, `MemberSchema`, `PreludeSchemas`, `SchemaIndex` or schema extensions. Those types are covered only by the SDK tests written with each task (`TraitKeyTest`, `SchemaBuilderTest`, `SchemaTest`, `PreludeSchemasTest`, `SchemaIndexTest`, `SchemaExtensionTest`). smithy-java's only extension assertions are in `core.serde.ShapeTranscoderTest`, which is ported with `ShapeTranscoder` (task 3.1).

**Targets.** `core/schema/src/test/java/software/amazon/awssdk/schema/`: `PortedShapeIdTest`, `PortedShapeTypeTest`, `PortedSchemaTest` and `PortedTraitMapTest`. They live in package `software.amazon.awssdk.schema` because `TraitMap`, `requiredMemberCount()` and `isRequiredByValidation` are package-private. The tests use JUnit 5 with AssertJ in place of Hamcrest. Methods keep their source names; the one exception is `ShapeIdValidationsTest`, which is renamed `shapeIdValidations`.

**Counts.** 91 source test methods: 24 ported, 7 adapted, 60 skipped. Every ported or adapted test passed against the existing port, so no `core/schema` main code changed.

Common adaptations, applied throughout and not repeated in the table:

- `Set.of` becomes `LinkedHashSet`.
- `new XTrait()` for annotation traits becomes `XTrait.INSTANCE`.
- `DefaultTrait(Node)` becomes `DefaultTrait(Document)`.
- `equalTo` on schemas becomes `isSameAs`, because schema equality is identity.

| Source class | Method | Status | Ported as / reason |
|---|---|---|---|
