SDK additions: `JsonCodecTest` (preset values and output, default settings, `toBuilder`/`updateBuilder`/codec `settings` copy and override, provider override, null arguments, extension registration, number literal read into a string member (including a value a double cannot hold) and a malformed number, type-mismatch and range messages naming the ShapeId, trailing content rejected by `deserializeShape`, union `__type` and `forbidUnknownUnionMembers`, pretty-print layout through both paths and read-back, the wire-format table above, documents by type, document number typing, one codec used by 8 threads, flush/close without closing the sink), `JsonPrettyPrinterTest` (layouts, escaped backslash before a quote, and a jqwik property: pretty output reads back to the same document for any write chunking), `ByteArraysTest` (views against `ByteBuffer`, bounds, `rangeEquals`, `unsignedMultiplyHigh` against `BigInteger`). The JSON round-trip and malformed-input properties are tasks 7.2 and 7.3.

**For the protocol tasks (7.11 awsJson, 7.13 restJson1, 7.17 documents).** Use `JsonSettings.AWS_JSON`/`REST_JSON_1` (`JsonCodec.builder().settings(...)`); for an empty response body, `readStruct` returns with no members (as smithy-java). `JsonCodec.deserializeShape` rejects trailing content; a protocol reading a body through `createDeserializer` should call `close()` for the same check. There is no `MemberSubsetCodec`: HTTP-binding body serde (7.9) must write the body members itself. The `bigDecimal` and timestamp spellings differ from the legacy SDK (table above); if a service needs the legacy string form of `bigDecimal`, that is a protocol-level choice, not a codec setting. Document members (7.17): documents are schema-free task 1.7 documents; a JSON string read into a document stays a string (no base64 or timestamp conversion), structure documents cannot be written, and `__type`/`defaultNamespace` handling arrives with `JsonDocuments` in task 11.5. `NullDocumentProtocolTest` (`{"documentValue": null}`) relies on a null member being treated as absent, which `readStruct` does.

## `core/codecs/xml-codec` (task 7.4)

**Sources.** smithy-java 1.7.0 (`/Users/alexwoo/java/smithy-java`, commit `142eecf4d`), module `codecs/xml-codec`, package `software.amazon.smithy.java.xml`.

**Target.** In `software.amazon.awssdk.codecs.xml` (`@SdkPublicApi`): `XmlCodec`, `XmlUtil` (ports) and `XmlSettings` (SDK addition, extends `CodecSettings`). In `software.amazon.awssdk.codecs.xml.internal` (`@SdkInternalApi`): `XmlSchemaExtensions` (public, registered in `META-INF/services/software.amazon.awssdk.schema.SchemaExtensionProvider`), `XmlReader` and the SDK addition `XmlSerdeFactory` (public), and package-private `SmithyXmlSerializer`, `SmithyXmlDeserializer` (the native byte-level implementation, the default), `XmlSerializer`, `XmlDeserializer` (the StAX implementation), `LazyXmlSerializer`, `EmptyXmlDeserializer`, `XmlInfo`, `XmlMemberLookup`, `XmlWriteUtils` and `XmlErrorCodeParser`. The module depends on `annotations`, `schema`, `codec-commons` and `utils`; the task 1.2 `maven.javadoc.skip`/`mdep.analyze.skip` are removed. The XML API is the JDK's StAX (`javax.xml.stream`, `javax.xml.namespace`), as in smithy-java (no Woodstox or Aalto), so there is no new third-party dependency; the SDK checkstyle `legalPackages` allows `javax.xml.namespace`, `javax.xml.stream` and `javax.xml.stream.events` in `software.amazon.awssdk.codecs.xml`, following the `aws-query-protocol` entry. `xml-codec` was already in `PortComplianceTest.PORTED_MODULES`; the compliance, `InternalApiBoundaryTest` and `PackageContainmentTest` checks pass with the new classes.

| Source | Port | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| Everything public or package-private in `xml`; `XmlSchemaExtensions` and its records public | All but `XmlCodec`, `XmlSettings` and `XmlUtil` move to `xml.internal`. The serializer/deserializer choice moves from `XmlCodec` into `XmlSerdeFactory`; `XmlUtil.parseErrorCodeName` delegates to it | `PackageContainmentTest`, as `CborSchemaExtensions` and `JsonSchemaExtensions` (tasks 3.6, 7.1) |
| `XmlCodec.Builder`: `defaultNamespace`, `wrapperElements`, `strictRootElement`, package-private `useNative`; `xmlName` and `timestampFormat` always honored, `date-time` default | Same, plus `settings(XmlSettings)`, `useXmlName`, `useTimestampFormat`, `defaultTimestampFormat`, `forbidUnknownUnionMembers`; `XmlCodec.settings()`, `wrapperElements()`, `strictRootElement()`. `XmlSettings` presets `REST_XML` and `AWS_QUERY_RESPONSE` (both `xmlName` on, `timestampFormat` on, `date-time` default; the protocol supplies the namespace and wrapper elements). `defaultNamespacePrefix` without a `defaultNamespace` throws `IllegalArgumentException` | Requirement 8.3 and design A.8; same settings shape as `JsonSettings`/`CborSettings`. The defaults reproduce smithy-java's fixed behavior |
| Implementation chosen by the `smithy-java.xml-provider` system property (`stax` selects StAX) | Native always, unless the package-private `useNative(false)` builder method (tests) selects StAX. No provider discovery: smithy-java has no XML serde-provider SPI to port | SDK checkstyle forbids `System.getProperty` |
| `XMLInputFactory` with `SUPPORT_DTD`, `javax.xml.stream.isSupportingExternalEntities`, `IS_REPLACING_ENTITY_REFERENCES` and `IS_COALESCING` off | Same four properties (`XmlSerdeFactory.newHardenedInputFactory`), the first two being the legacy `XmlDomParser` settings (`aws-query-protocol`, used by the restXml and query unmarshallers). The native reader is unchanged: a `<!DOCTYPE>` is skipped without being read or resolved, an internal subset is rejected, and only the five predefined entities and character references are resolved; any other entity reference throws | XXE and entity expansion (below) |
| `record`s (`AcquireContext`, `XmlSchemaExtensions.MemberExtension`/`StructExtension`/`ListExtension`/`MapExtension`), `sealed` `XmlReader` and `XmlExtension`, switch expressions, `var` | Final classes with accessors; `XmlReader` abstract with a package-private constructor and `SdkAutoCloseable`; statements | Java 8 |
| `XmlWriteUtils` `CompactStringAccess` Latin-1 path | The `charAt` loop only | Design "Porting conventions" (no `Unsafe`); same output |
| `Arrays.equals(byte[], int, int, byte[], int, int)` in `XmlMemberLookup`; `ConcurrentHashMap.computeIfAbsent` per lookup in `XmlInfo` | `ByteArrays.rangeEquals`; `get` first, then `computeIfAbsent` | Java 9 API; Java 8's `computeIfAbsent` locks the bin even on a hit |
| `XmlSchemaExtensions.resolveMemberXmlName` and `resolveXmlName` (identical) | `resolveXmlName` only | Duplicate code |
| Text escapes `&`, `<`, `>`; attributes also `"` and `'`. A carriage return in text, and a tab or line feed in an attribute, are written raw | Also `&#xD;` for CR in text (both implementations) and `&#x9;`/`&#xA;` in attributes | Behavior fix: XML parsers normalize these characters (XML 1.0 sections 2.11, 3.3.3), so the raw form did not read back as itself. Needed for `SdkRestXmlEscapedCharacters` (`rest-xml-input.json#3`) |
| Value errors: reader messages without the target | Typed reads name the target ShapeId; malformed numbers, blobs and attribute values throw `SerializationException` | Requirement 7.7, same extent as the CBOR and JSON ports |
| `LazyXmlSerializer.close()` releases the pooled serializer on every call | Releases once; a second `close()` does nothing | Releasing twice put one serializer in the pool twice |
| `XmlUtil.parseErrorCodeName` casts to `XmlErrorCodeParser` (`ClassCastException` for an empty body's `EmptyXmlDeserializer`) | `SerializationException` naming the deserializer type; the deserializer is still closed | Codec errors are `SerializationException` |
| `XmlCodec.deserializeShape` (from `Codec`) | Null checks naming the parameter; closes the deserializer after building, so trailing content is rejected; a close failure is added as suppressed to the original exception | Requirement 39.5; as `JsonCodec` |

Unchanged from smithy-java: documents are not supported (`writeDocument` writes nothing, `readDocument` returns `null`); an empty body reads as a structure with no members; unknown elements are skipped and reported through `unknownMember`; root `<ErrorResponse>`, `<Response>` and `<Error>` elements are unwrapped for errors; flattened list and map items may be interleaved with other members (both readers buffer and replay them); no XML declaration is written.

**XXE hardening.** `XmlSecurityTest` runs each case against both implementations: an external entity declared in an internal subset (`<!DOCTYPE Doc [<!ENTITY xxe SYSTEM "file:...">]>`) and an entity declared in an external DTD are rejected with `SerializationException`, and the exception does not contain the referenced file's content; an external DTD reference alone is ignored (the DTD file ends in garbage, so the read would fail if it were loaded); a billion-laughs style internal entity expansion and an undeclared entity (`&nbsp;`) are rejected; the predefined entities and decimal and hex character references are resolved; and the hardened `XMLInputFactory` reports the four properties as off.

**Wire format compared with the legacy SDK** (`XmlProtocolMarshaller`/`XmlGenerator`/`XmlWriter`, `ValueToStringConverter`, `DateUtils`). Unchanged: element names (`locationName` ↔ `xmlName`), wrapped lists (`<member>` or the list member's name) and maps (`<entry><key><value>`), flattened lists and maps, `date-time` (`ISO_INSTANT` spelling, e.g. `2015-01-25T08:00:00.123Z`; fraction in groups of three, sub-millisecond digits kept), `http-date` (`Tue, 29 Apr 2014 18:30:38 GMT`), base64 blobs with padding, `bigDecimal`/`bigInteger` as `toString()` (`1E+3`), integers and booleans, `xmlns` of the operation's namespace on the root element only, a member's `xmlNamespace` declared on that member's element, and an empty structure as a start and an end tag (`<simpleStruct></simpleStruct>`). Intentional differences (the Smithy restXml protocol tests compare bodies with XMLUnit, so the escaping and declaration rows compare equal there; not checked against the generated XML protocol tests yet, tasks 7.14, 7.15 and 7.21 run them):

| Value | Legacy SDK | XML codec |
|---|---|---|
| XML declaration | `<?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8"?>` before the root element | None |
| Text escaping | `&`, `"`, `'`, `<`, `>`, CR, LF as `&amp;` `&quot;` `&apos;` `&lt;` `&gt;` `&#x0D;` `&#x0A;`, after first unescaping any of those entities already in the value (so `&amp;` is written as `&amp;` and reads back as `&`) | `&amp;` `&lt;` `&gt;` and `&#xD;`; quotes, apostrophes, tabs and LF raw; the value is never unescaped first (`&amp;` is written as `&amp;amp;`) |
| Attribute values (`xmlAttribute`) | Written raw, unescaped | Escaped: `&amp;` `&lt;` `&gt;` `&quot;` `&apos;` `&#xD;` `&#xA;` `&#x9;` |
| `double`/`float` | `Double.toString`/`Float.toString`: `1.0`, `1.0E10` | Native: integral values in `long` range without a fraction (`1`, `10000000000`, `-0.0` → `0`), others in shortest round-trip form (Zmij, `1.5E-7`, a `float` of `1e10` → `1.0E10`); `NaN`, `Infinity`, `-Infinity`. StAX (tests only): `Double.toString`/`Float.toString` as the legacy SDK |
| `epoch-seconds` timestamp | Millisecond `BigDecimal`, always three fraction digits: `1422172800.000`, `1422172800.100` | `TimestampFormatter.EPOCH_SECONDS.writeString`, the same text as the JSON codec: up to nine fraction digits, trailing zeros dropped (`1422172800`, `1422172800.1`, `1422172800.001`, `1422172800.0000015`); sub-millisecond precision is kept. Reading parses the decimal exactly. The smithy-java formatter's `double` fraction test, which wrote 1 ms after `1422172800` as `1422172800`, was fixed in codec-commons (`fix(codecs): Keep epoch-seconds fractions exact`) |

**Tests.** Sources: smithy-java `codecs/xml-codec/src/test` `XmlCodecTest` (10 methods), `GeneratedModelSerdeTest` (63), `XmlReaderTest` (9) and the helpers `ProviderTestBase` and `PerProvider`. All 82 methods are ported under the same names (`XmlReaderTest` in `xml.internal`), and run against both implementations and, where smithy-java does, every writer/reader pairing (`crossProviders`): 16, 155 and 9 runs. Adaptations, in addition to `var`, `List.of`/`Set.of`/`Map.of`/`String.repeat` (`TestCollections`), text blocks and switch expressions replaced by Java 8 code, Hamcrest → AssertJ and `schema()` → `sdkSchema()`: `GeneratedModelSerdeTest` runs on `XmlTestModel` schemas (the shapes of smithy-java's `model/test.smithy`) with `SchemaStruct` values (enums as strings) instead of generated classes; `XmlCodecTest`'s `TestPojo`/`MapPojo` are hand-written `SerializableStruct`s as in the source.

| Source test | Status | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| `XmlCodecTest`, `GeneratedModelSerdeTest`, `XmlReaderTest` (all) | Ported | — |
| fuzz `DeserializationFuzzTest`, `DifferentialXmlFuzzTest` and the dictionary | Not ported | No Jazzer source set (as tasks 3.5, 3.6, 7.1) |
| `model/test.smithy` codegen (`smithyBuild`) | Not ported | Replaced by `XmlTestModel` schemas; the SDK codecs do not generate test classes |

SDK additions: `XmlSecurityTest` (above, 13 runs); `XmlSettingsTest` (defaults, presets, `toBuilder`/`updateBuilder`, value equality, the prefix-without-namespace check, codec `settings` copy and override); `XmlCodecWireFormatTest` (45 runs: the legacy-disposition rows the codec must honor, `rest-xml-input.json#3` escaped characters including the legacy entity-escaped body read back, `#7`/`#18` empty structures, `#16`/`#17` millisecond `date-time` timestamps, `rest-xml-output.json#8` and `ec2-output.json#7` single-element flattened lists, `ec2-output.json#8` flattened list of structures interleaved with another member; the wire-format table above; no declaration; default, prefixed and member namespaces; escaped and renamed attributes; `useXmlName(false)` and `useTimestampFormat(false)`; unknown members; union `forbidUnknownUnionMembers`; error messages naming the ShapeId; query wrapper elements; `parseErrorCodeName` on an empty or non-error body; source buffer position; `serialize` byte-identical to the serializer path without closing the sink; null arguments; extension registration; one codec used by 8 threads). The XML round-trip and malformed-input properties are tasks 7.6 and 7.7.

**For the protocol tasks (7.5 QueryFormSerializer, 7.14 restXml, 7.15 awsQuery/ec2Query).**

- 7.14 restXml: `XmlCodec.builder().settings(XmlSettings.REST_XML).defaultNamespace(<service xmlNamespace>)`, with `strictRootElement(false)` for responses (S3 returns root names that differ from the output shape, such as `<CopyObjectResult>`). The codec writes no XML declaration; if a service needs one, the protocol writes it. Error codes: `XmlUtil.parseErrorCodeName(codec.createDeserializer(body))` (it closes the deserializer); guard an empty body first, which throws. Header, query and label timestamps are protocol-level (`http-date`/`date-time` per binding), not codec settings; `TimestampFormatter.Prelude` has the formatters.
- 7.15 awsQuery/ec2Query responses: `XmlSettings.AWS_QUERY_RESPONSE` and `wrapperElements(Arrays.asList(op + "Response", op + "Result"))` (awsQuery) or `op + "Response"` (ec2Query; no `Result` wrapper). Matching is exact and top-level only. `parseErrorCodeName` handles `<ErrorResponse><Error>` (awsQuery) and `<Response><Errors><Error>` (ec2Query). `ec2-output.json#7` (single-element flattened list) is covered by the codec; the protocol test still has to run end to end.
- 7.5 QueryFormSerializer: does not depend on this module (requests are form-encoded, and the XML implementation classes are package-private). Its naming rules (`xmlName`, `xmlFlattened`, ec2Query's `ec2QueryName` and capitalized `xmlName`) are its own. Epoch-seconds timestamps through `TimestampFormatter.EPOCH_SECONDS` get the exact fraction (the formatter defect is fixed); query timestamps default to `date-time`, so only modeled `epoch-seconds` members use it.

## `core/protocols/aws-query-client-protocol` QueryFormSerializer (task 7.5)

In `software.amazon.awssdk.protocols.client.awsquery.internal` (both `@SdkInternalApi`): `QueryFormSerializer` (a `ShapeSerializer`) and `AwsQuerySchemaExtensions` with its `QueryMemberBinding`, registered in `META-INF/services/software.amazon.awssdk.schema.SchemaExtensionProvider`. Ported from smithy-java `aws/client/aws-client-awsquery` (commit 142eecf4d). The client protocols are task 7.15.

**Module.** Declares `annotations`, `utils`, `schema` and `codec-commons`. The task 1.2 javadoc and dependency-analysis skips are removed, and so are the skeleton's `http-client-protocol` and `xml-codec` dependencies, which the serializer does not use; task 7.15 adds them back with the protocols.

**API for task 7.15.** `QueryFormSerializer.forAwsQuery(action, version)` or `forEc2Query(action, version)` writes `Action=<action>&Version=<version>`; then `input.serializeMembers(serializer)` (or `serializer.writeStruct(inputSchema, input)`); then `finish()` returns the body as an array-backed `ByteBuffer` starting at 0 with `arrayOffset() == 0`, without a copy. `finish()` may be called once; a write after it throws `IllegalStateException`. The protocol sets `Content-Type: application/x-www-form-urlencoded` (the legacy `QueryParametersToBodyStage` sends `; charset=utf-8`), and fills unset idempotency tokens before serializing: the serializer writes only the values it is given (Smithy `QueryProtocolIdempotencyTokenAutoFill`, `Ec2ProtocolIdempotencyTokenAutoFill`).

| Source | Port | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| `QueryFormSerializer` package-private in the protocol package (design D.14 Porting_Map: package-private) | Public `@SdkInternalApi` in `.internal`; `AwsQuerySchemaExtensions` also moves to `.internal` | `PackageContainmentTest` requires public `@SdkInternalApi` types in an `internal` package; the 7.15 protocols in the parent package call the factories |
| `acquire(QueryVariant, action, version)` from a `StripedPool`; `finish()` copies a small buffer and releases the serializer to the pool | `forAwsQuery`/`forEc2Query` create a new serializer; `finish()` returns the buffer without copying | `StripedPool`, `NumberCodec` and `TimestampCodec` are `@SdkInternalApi` in `software.amazon.awssdk.codecs.commons.internal`, and `InternalApiBoundaryTest` forbids internal APIs across root packages (`protocols` here). Without a pool there is nothing to alias, so no copy is needed |
| Three serializers (top level, list item, map value) with the value methods written out in each | One abstract `ValueSerializer` with the value methods; the subclasses only write keys and push prefixes | Same keys; removes the triplicated code |
| `writeUrlEncoded` reads the Latin-1 array through `CompactStringAccess`; `Action`/`Version` copied unencoded with `String.getBytes(int, int, byte[], int)` | A `charAt` loop with the ASCII fast path; `Action` and `Version` are percent-encoded like values | No compact-string access on Java 8. Operation names and versions are unreserved characters, so the output is the same |
| An unpaired surrogate is encoded as a three-byte sequence (`%ED%A0%BD`) | Encoded as `?` (`%3F`) | Matches the legacy SDK (`URLEncoder` over `String.getBytes(UTF_8)`) and keeps the body valid UTF-8 |
| A list nested in a list, or a map in a list, pushes the element's member name again: `ListOfLists.member.1.member.member.1`, `ListOfMaps.member.1.member.entry.1.key`; in ec2Query `OuterList.1.Member.1` | `ListOfLists.member.1.member.1`, `ListOfMaps.member.1.entry.1.key`, `OuterList.1.1` | Source bug (its test documents the doubled segment). The port matches the legacy `ListQueryMarshaller` paths. No upstream awsQuery or ec2Query request test has a list of lists |
| ec2Query `writeMap` throws `SerializationException` | Maps use the awsQuery map rules in ec2Query | The legacy SDK registers `MapQueryMarshaller` for both protocols, so a model with an ec2Query map keeps working |
| Member names, `xmlFlattened` and list/map member `xmlName` read with `getTrait` (member and target traits merged) | `getDirectTrait` on the member; `timestampFormat` still includes the target's | `xmlName` on a target structure is an XML element name, not a query key; the legacy SDK used the member's `locationName` only |
| `float`/`double` through `NumberCodec` (integral values without a fraction, `1`, `10000000000`; others shortest) | `Float.toString`/`Double.toString` (`1.0`, `1.0E10`, `-0.0`, `NaN`, `Infinity`) | Matches the legacy `ValueToStringConverter`; `NumberCodec` is internal to `codecs` (above) |
| `date-time` through `TimestampCodec.writeIso8601` (fraction with trailing zeros dropped, `08:00:00.1Z`) | `TimestampFormatter.DATE_TIME.writeString` (`Instant.toString`, fraction in groups of three: `08:00:00.100Z`) | Matches the legacy `DateUtils.formatIso8601Date` and the XML codec |
| `epoch-seconds` and `http-date` through `TimestampCodec`; an unknown format through `TimestampFormatter.of` | `TimestampFormatter.of(format).writeString`, resolved once in the extension; an unknown format throws `SerializationException` when a value is written | Same text (epoch-seconds after the codec-commons fix above); public API only |
| `writeDocument` message `Query protocols do not support document types` | The same message plus the schema ShapeId | Errors name the schema |
| — | A value written directly on the serializer with a non-member schema throws `SerializationException` (`must be a structure member`) | The source writes a `null` key and fails with `NullPointerException` |

**Wire format compared with the legacy SDK** (`QueryProtocolMarshaller`, `ListQueryMarshaller`, `MapQueryMarshaller`, `SimpleTypeQueryMarshaller`, then `QueryParametersToBodyStage`, which writes the parameters with `SdkHttpUtils.encodeAndFlattenFormData`). Unchanged: `Action` and `Version` first, then members in member order; nested structure keys joined with `.`; awsQuery lists `Name.member.N` (list member `xmlName` replaces `member`), flattened `Name.N`, an explicitly empty list `Name=`; ec2Query lists always `Name.N` and empty lists omitted; maps `Name.entry.N.key`/`.value` with key/value `xmlName`, flattened `Name.N.key`; ec2Query names (`ec2QueryName`, else capitalized `xmlName` or member name); sparse null list elements skip their index and null map values write only the key; booleans, integers, `Float`/`Double.toString`, padded base64 blobs, `date-time` and `http-date` spellings; UTF-8 percent-encoding with uppercase hex. The Smithy protocol tests compare form bodies as decoded pairs (`FormUrlEncodedBodyComparator`), so the encoding rows compare equal there; the epoch-seconds row is what `QueryTimestampsInput` and `Ec2TimestampsInput` expect.

| Value | Legacy SDK | QueryFormSerializer |
|---|---|---|
| Space | `+` (`encodeAndFlattenFormData` uses `formDataEncode`, plain `URLEncoder`, not `SdkHttpUtils.urlEncode`) | `%20` |
| `*` and `~` | `*` unencoded, `~` as `%7E` | `%2A` and `~` (RFC 3986 unreserved set, as smithy-java and `SdkHttpUtils.urlEncode`) |
| `epoch-seconds` timestamp | `DateUtils.formatUnixTimestampInstant`: three fraction digits, sub-millisecond digits dropped (`1422172800.000`) | Up to nine fraction digits, trailing zeros dropped (`1422172800`, `1422172800.001`) |
| `bigDecimal`, `byte` members | No marshaller registered (the marshalling fails) | `toPlainString()` (`1E+3` → `1000`); `byte` in decimal |
| List of lists, map in a list | `L.member.1.member.1`, `L.member.1.entry.1.key` | Same (smithy-java differs, above) |

**Tests** (`core/protocols/aws-query-client-protocol/src/test/java/.../awsquery/internal/`, 52 runs, with a write-only `SchemaStruct` copied from the codec tests and `QueryTestModel` schemas shaped like the Smithy `AwsQuery`/`AwsEc2` input shapes): `QueryFormSerializerTest` asserts exact bodies for the request cases `QuerySimpleInputParams*`, `AwsQuerySupports*FloatInputs`, `QueryTimestampsInput`, `NestedStructures`, `QueryLists`, `EmptyQueryLists`, `FlattenedQueryLists`, `QueryListArgWithXmlNameMember`, `QueryFlattenedListArgWithXmlName`, `QueryNestedStructWithList`, `QuerySimpleQueryMaps`, `QuerySimpleQueryMapsWithXmlName`, `QueryComplexQueryMaps`, `QueryEmptyQueryMaps`, `QueryQueryMapWithMemberXmlName`, `QueryFlattenedQueryMaps`, `QueryFlattenedQueryMapsWithXmlName`, `QueryQueryMapOfLists`, `QueryNestedStructWithMap` and their ec2Query counterparts (`Ec2Lists`, `Ec2EmptyQueryLists`, `Ec2ListArgWithXmlNameMember`, `Ec2ListMemberWithXmlName`, `Ec2ListNestedStructWithList`, `Ec2Query`, `Ec2QueryIsPreferred`, `Ec2XmlNameIsUppercased`, `Ec2QueryNameDistinctFromXmlNameAndMemberName`, `Ec2TimestampsInput`, `Ec2NestedStructures`, `Ec2ProtocolIdempotencyTokenAutoFillIsSet`), plus integral types, float and double spellings, `bigDecimal`, blobs (buffer position kept), percent-encoding of reserved, control, two-, three- and four-byte and unpaired-surrogate characters and of `Action`/`Version` and member names, long non-ASCII values, millisecond `date-time`, exact epoch-seconds, `http-date`, an unknown format, empty nested structures, lists of lists, sparse lists and maps, list timestamp formats, nested maps, empty list map values, ec2Query maps, documents, a non-member schema, `finish()` once and buffer growth, and the service-loader registration. The behaviors smithy-java's `QueryFormSerializerTest` checks are covered here, with the legacy paths in place of its doubled-member expectations; its pool tests do not apply. The generated `AwsQuery`/`AwsEc2` protocol tests run end to end in tasks 7.15 and 7.21.

## `core/http-binding` (task 7.8)
**Sources.** smithy-java 1.7.0 (`/Users/alexwoo/java/smithy-java`, commit `142eecf4d`), module `http/http-binding` (package `software.amazon.smithy.java.http.binding`), and from `io`, `uri.URLEncoding`.
**Target.** In `software.amazon.awssdk.httpbinding` (`@SdkPublicApi`): `HttpBinding`, `RequestSerializer`, `ResponseSerializer`, `RequestDeserializer`, `ResponseDeserializer`, `HeaderErrorSerializer`. In `software.amazon.awssdk.httpbinding.internal` (`@SdkInternalApi`): `HttpBindingSchemaExtensions` (public, registered in `META-INF/services/software.amazon.awssdk.schema.SchemaExtensionProvider`), `HttpBindingSerializer` and `HttpBindingDeserializer` (public, used by the public classes), and package-private `PathSerializer`, `PayloadSerializer`, `PayloadDeserializer`, `StructBodyProxy`, `BasicStringValueDeserializer`, `HttpHeaderDeserializer`, `HttpHeaderListDeserializer`, `HttpPrefixHeadersDeserializer`, `HttpPathLabelDeserializer`, `HttpQueryStringDeserializer`, `HttpQueryParamsDeserializer`, `ResponseStatusDeserializer`, `PrefixConstants`, plus the SDK additions `UriPattern`, `UrlEncoding` (port of `io.uri.URLEncoding`), `StringMapEntrySerializer` and `ValueSkipper`.
**Module.** Declares `annotations`, `utils`, `schema`, `codec-commons` and `http-client-spi`; `json-codec` and `xml-codec` are test-only. The task 1.2 javadoc and dependency-analysis skips and the unused `test-utils`/`jqwik` test dependencies are removed (property tests 7.9 and 7.10 add jqwik back). Only public APIs of other modules are used: `InternalApiBoundaryTest` allows internal APIs only within one root package, and `software.amazon.awssdk.httpbinding` is its own root, so nothing from `codecs.commons.internal` (for example `TimestampCodec`) is used. The internal types live in `.internal` because `PackageContainmentTest` requires a public `@SdkInternalApi` type to be there (as `CborSchemaExtensions`, `JsonSchemaExtensions`).
**Task text.** Task 7.8 names `BindingMatcher`. smithy-java has no `BindingMatcher` at `142eecf4d`; its role (classifying each member by binding trait) is `HttpBindingSchemaExtensions.MemberBinding` and the per-direction plans, which are ported.
| Source | Port | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| `HttpBinding` public constructor, `builder()`/`runtimeCodegen`; `HttpBindingCodegen`, `HttpBindingRuntimeCodegenBackend`, `HttpBindingWriter` | `HttpBinding.create()` (one stateless instance); no codegen classes; SDK addition `hasStreamingPayload(schema, response)` | Runtime codegen deferred (design D.3); static factory per SDK guidelines. `hasStreamingPayload` is the task 7.19 hook |
| `MemberSubsetCodec` fast paths (`BodyMemberSubset`, direct-builder reads in `HttpBindingDeserializer`/`PayloadDeserializer`) | Not ported; the body is always written through `StructBodyProxy` and read through a member filter (below) | `MemberSubsetCodec` is not in the SDK `codec-commons` (tasks 3.5, 7.1) |
| `HttpTrait.getUri()` is smithy-model's parsed `UriPattern` | `UriPattern` parses the SDK trait's string once per operation schema (inside `OperationBinding`): literal, label and greedy-label segments, query literals. Malformed patterns (label not a whole segment, two greedy labels, duplicate literal keys) throw `IllegalArgumentException` | The SDK runtime `HttpTrait` keeps the unparsed string (task 1.7) |
| `QueryStringBuilder` writes an encoded query string; `HttpRequest.setUri(endpoint.withConcatPath(path).withQuery(qs))` | Raw query parameters (`LinkedHashMap`, values grouped by name in first-seen order) set with `SdkHttpFullRequest.Builder.rawQueryParameters`; the request percent-encodes them through `SdkHttpUtils.urlEncode` (the same RFC 3986 unreserved set as `URLEncoding`). Path: `uri(endpoint)` then `encodedPath(SdkHttpUtils.appendUri(endpoint raw path, path))` | SigV4 and the rest of the pipeline read raw query parameters. `appendUri` is the joining `HttpClientProtocol` and `RpcV2CborProtocol` use |
| Query literal without `=` (`?tagging`) written as `tagging=`; pre-encoded once | Raw parameter with a null value, written as `tagging` | Matches the legacy `ProtocolUtils.addStaticQueryParametersToRequest` and the `RestJsonConstantQueryString` expectation (`queryParams: ["foo=bar", "hello"]`) |
| `@httpQueryParams` entries checked against the `@httpQuery` names added so far (member order matters) | Entries are collected and added after every member is written; a name written by an `@httpQuery` member or a query literal drops the entry. A null `@httpQuery` member does not block it | Requirement 15.6 independent of member order |
| `ModifiableHttpHeaders`, names canonicalized to lower case; `addHeader` appends | `TreeMap(CASE_INSENSITIVE_ORDER)` copied into the request/response builder; names as the model declares them (`X-Foo`), as the legacy marshallers do. Every header member writes one value with put-replace, so a `Content-Type` `@httpHeader` member replaces the protocol media type instead of adding a second value | SDK header model; legacy case on the wire |
| A list `@httpHeader` adds one header value per element | One header, elements joined with `", "` (Requirement 15.6). A string element containing `,`, `"` or `\` is wrapped in double quotes with `"` and `\` escaped (`RestJsonInputAndOutputWithQuotedStringHeaders`: `"b,c", "\"def\"", a`); timestamps are not quoted (`http-date` lists rely on the reader's every-other-comma split) | The SDK request has no joining step; Smithy's list-header rules. Quoting `\` makes the reader's escape handling return the element unchanged |
| List header/query timestamp elements use the struct member's format; `HttpHeaderListDeserializer` splits http-dates when the struct member has no `timestampFormat` **or `date-time`** | The element format is the list member's `timestampFormat`, else the location default; http-date splitting only for `http-date` elements | Source bug: `timestampFormat` cannot be on a member that targets a list, so smithy-java always split on every other comma and broke `date-time` lists |
| Blob payload `DataStream.ofByteBuffer`; Content-Type fixed when the payload is set (skipped when the input declares a `Content-Type` header member, even a null one) | `ContentStreamProvider.fromByteArrayUnsafe(ByteBufferUtils.getBytes(buffer))` (no copy for an exact array-backed buffer, else a copy); the raw-payload Content-Type (`@mediaType`, else `application/octet-stream` for blobs and `text/plain` for other scalars) is applied after all members when no `Content-Type` was written | Order-independent; a null `Content-Type` member no longer leaves the payload without a type |
| `Content-Length` from `DataStream` | `Content-Length` set on every request and response that has a body | The SDK request has no implicit length (as task 3.21) |

A `@streaming` payload member writes no body and is not read; the response body is then left unread. Event streams (`eventEncoderFactory`, `eventDecoderFactory`) are not ported | Tasks 7.19 and 7.20 |
| Empty-body bytes cached per schema from the first codec | Cached per plan for the codec instance that produced them | Runtime protocol selection (task 3.23) can use one schema with two codecs |
| Response payload read when `contentLength != 0` (scalars) or the buffer is non-empty (structures, unions, lists) | Every payload type is left unset when the body is empty | `RestJsonHttpPayloadTraitsWithNoBlobBody`; unknown-length SDK bodies |
| Body read with `createDeserializer(bb).readStruct(...)`, not closed; a non-body key is passed to a lambda that ignores it | The deserializer is closed (JSON rejects trailing content); a body key that names a member bound elsewhere is read and discarded by `ValueSkipper`; unknown members are forwarded | The SDK JSON reader requires the consumer to read each value; closing releases pooled state |

| Labels: `HTTP label for X cannot be empty` (null and empty) | `Required httpLabel member `<ShapeId>` must not be null` / `... must not be empty` | Requirement 15.7, design A.9 |
| `HttpHeaders`, `HttpRequest`, `HttpResponse`, `DataStream` | `SdkHttpHeaders`, `SdkHttpFullRequest`, `SdkHttpFullResponse`, `ByteBuffer`/`InputStream` (`ResponseDeserializer.body(ByteBuffer)` SDK addition; else `response.content()` is read to the end, not closed) | Design A.9 type map |
| `ResponseDeserializer.errorShapeBuilder(ShapeBuilder<? extends ModeledException>)` | `ShapeBuilder<?>` | Design A.9 says `SdkServiceException`; that type is in `sdk-core`, which `http-binding` does not depend on |
| `ResponseSerializer.errorSchema(Schema)`, `context(Context)`; `HeaderErrorSerializer.writeErrorType(schema, headers, context)` | `error(boolean)` (the schema is the shape value's); no context; `writeErrorType(schema, headers)`. Error status: `@httpError`, else `@error` default (400/500), else 500 | No `Context`/`sdk-core` dependency |
| `RequestDeserializer.request(HttpRequest)` parses the raw query with `QueryStringParser` | Reads `SdkHttpFullRequest.forEachRawQueryParameter` (already decoded); a parameter without a value reads as `""`. `QueryStringParser` not ported | SDK request model |
| `HttpPrefixHeadersDeserializer` keys are the lower-cased header names minus the prefix | Keys keep the received case (`x-meta-Foo` → `Foo`); the prefix still matches case-insensitively | SDK headers keep the wire case; matches the legacy unmarshaller |
| `record`s, `sealed` `HttpBindingExt`, switch expressions, `var`, `Set.of`, `HashSet.newHashSet`, `StringBuilder.isEmpty` | Final classes, a plain marker interface, statements, Java 8 collections | Java 8 (design "Porting conventions") |
| `HttpPrefixHeadersSerializer`, `HttpQueryParamsSerializer` | One `StringMapEntrySerializer`; null values and null list elements are dropped | Same entry logic with different consumers; smithy-java threw on nulls |
| `URLEncoding.urlDecode` | Not ported | No caller: labels are passed decoded and the SDK request holds decoded query parameters |
`HttpBindingSchemaExtensions` keeps the smithy-java name with a `CHECKSTYLE:OFF` around the class declaration (`PluralEnumNames`), as `CborSchemaExtensions`.
**Body-member subsetting (for 7.13 and 7.14).** smithy-java writes body members through `MemberSubsetCodec` when the codec has it and falls back to `StructBodyProxy`; the SDK always uses the proxy. `HttpBindingSerializer.serializeBody` calls `codec.createSerializer(out).writeStruct(schema, new StructBodyProxy(struct, bindings))`. The proxy's `serializeMembers` wraps the codec's member serializer in an `InterceptingSerializer` whose `before(member)` returns the codec serializer for a member whose direction-specific binding is `BODY` and `ShapeSerializer.nullSerializer()` otherwise; `getMemberValue` returns null for non-body members, so a codec that reads values directly (XML attributes) sees the same subset. Any codec that writes a structure by calling `serializeMembers` with the original member schemas (JSON, XML and CBOR do) works unchanged, and member indexes, `jsonName` and `xmlName` lookups are the codec's own. Reading: `codec.createDeserializer(body).readStruct(schema, state, BodyMemberConsumer)`; the consumer forwards `BODY` members and `unknownMember`, and skips any other member's value. Direction folding: `@httpResponseCode` is a body member in requests; labels, `@httpQuery` and `@httpQueryParams` are body members in responses. A structure with body members always gets a body, even when all of them are null (`RestJsonDoesntSerializeNullStructureValues` expects `{}`); `omitEmptyPayload(true)` drops the body only when there are no body members and no payload member (`RestJsonEmptyInputAndEmptyOutput` expects no body). Requirement 15.1's "neither a non-null `httpPayload` member nor any non-null unbound body member" wording is stricter than this; the port follows smithy-java and the protocol tests.
**Encoding compared with the legacy SDK** (`PathMarshaller`, `SdkHttpUtils`, `HeaderMarshaller`/`QueryParamMarshaller`, `InstantToString`, `DateUtils`). `UrlEncodingTest` and `RequestSerializerTest` compare the outputs directly.
| Value | Legacy SDK | http-binding |
|---|---|---|
| Labels and query values: space, `/` (non-greedy), `+`, `*`, `~`, `%`, reserved characters, non-ASCII (`ü`, `日本語`, `😀`), empty string | `SdkHttpUtils.urlEncode` (`%20`, `%2F`, `%2B`, `%2A`, `~`, UTF-8 percent-encoding) | Same bytes (query values are encoded by `SdkHttpUtils.urlEncode` itself) |
| Greedy label | `urlEncodeIgnoreSlashes`; restJson1 `PathMarshaller.GREEDY` trims one leading `/`, restXml `GREEDY_WITH_SLASHES` keeps it | Same encoding; the leading `/` is kept for every protocol (`/b//x/y`), as smithy-java |
| Lone surrogate in a label | `%3F` (`URLEncoder` replacement `?`) | `%EF%BF%BD` (U+FFFD) |
| Null or empty label | `IllegalArgumentException("X cannot be empty.")` | `SerializationException` naming the member; `HttpClientProtocol` turns both into `SdkClientException` |
| List header | One header line per element (`appendHeader`), blank strings skipped, no quoting | One header joined with `", "`, empty strings kept, Smithy quoting |
| `@httpQueryParams` entry with an `@httpQuery` name | Both values sent | The `@httpQuery` value only (`RestJsonQueryPrecedence`) |
| `@httpPrefixHeaders` key already starting with the prefix | Sent without adding the prefix again | Prefix always added |
| Prefix-header entry named like an `@httpHeader` member | Both values | The `@httpHeader` member wins |
| Header and query timestamps | `RFC_822`/`ISO_8601` (`DateUtils.formatRfc822Date`, `formatIso8601Date`): `Tue, 29 Apr 2014 18:30:38 GMT`, `2014-04-29T18:30:38.123Z` | Same text (`TimestampFormatter` `http-date`, `date-time`) |
| `epoch-seconds` header or query timestamp | `formatUnixTimestampInstant`: three fraction digits (`1398796238.000`) | Up to nine fraction digits, trailing zeros dropped (`1398796238`, `1.5`), as the codecs (tasks 3.5, 7.4) |
| Empty header value in a response | `firstMatchingHeader` treats it as absent | Same (members stay unset); smithy-java reads `""` |
**Not ported.** `HttpBindingCodegen`, `HttpBindingRuntimeCodegenBackend`, `HttpBindingWriter` (D.3), event stream binding (7.20), streaming data streams (7.19), and host prefixes: neither smithy-java's `http-binding` nor the SDK new path (`http-client-protocol`, tasks 3.19/3.21) applies `@endpoint`/`@hostLabel` yet, so 7.13/7.14 (or the endpoint stage) must add it, as the legacy marshallers' `hostPrefixExpression` does.
**Tests** (`core/http-binding/src/test/java/software/amazon/awssdk/httpbinding/`, 145 runs, with a `SchemaStruct` copied from the codec tests and hand-written `TestModel` schemas). Ported: smithy-java `PathSerializerTest` (all 26 label cases, plus static paths, literals around labels, null/empty labels and a missing label member), `PayloadSerializerTest` (blob, string, list and struct payloads, plus Content-Type, read-only buffers, null payloads, `allowEmptyStructPayload` and streaming), `PayloadDeserializerTest#stringPayloadIsReadWhenResponseIsReadonlyBuffer`, and from `HttpBindingDeserializerTest`: `contentTypeTest` (17 cases), the string/blob payload stream cases and the streaming-payload case (adapted: the body must not be read). Not ported: `HttpBindingWriterTest` (runtime codegen) and the `HttpBindingDeserializerTest` direct-builder, `MemberSubsetCodec` and `DataStream` discard cases (types not ported). SDK additions: `RequestSerializerTest` (every binding location with exact path, raw and encoded query, headers and body; null members; empty input with and without `omitEmptyPayload`; `Content-Type` member; query precedence in both member orders; prefix headers; labels and query values against `SdkHttpUtils` for 11 tricky values; greedy leading slash; http-date and date-time against `DateUtils`; empty strings and empty lists; the caller's body buffer used without copy), `ResponseDeserializerTest` (every location with case-insensitive names, quoted and multi-line list headers, http-date and date-time lists, epoch-seconds, prefix keys, status and a body key that names a header member; absent headers; 7 invalid header values naming the member; `body(ByteBuffer)` precedence; Content-Type validation; malformed and trailing body content; empty bodies; an XML body; payloads; the error builder), `RoundTripTest` (response and request round trips through all four public classes, default and error status codes, `X-Amzn-Errortype`), `HttpBindingSchemaExtensionsTest` (one plan per schema instance, member kinds and timestamp defaults, direction folding, error status defaults, operation binding, rejection of unbound schemas, header-list quoting), `UriPatternTest` and `UrlEncodingTest` (legacy encoder comparison for 20 values, every ASCII character, lone surrogates).
**For 7.13 (restJson1) and 7.14 (restXml).** Build the request with `HttpBinding.create().requestSerializer().operation(op).payloadCodec(codec).payloadMediaType(...).endpoint(endpoint).shapeValue(input)`; restJson1 sets `omitEmptyPayload(true)`; pass `bodyBuffer(newRequestBodyBuffer(op))` and record the size through `finishRequestBody` if the E1 hint is wanted. Read with `responseDeserializer().payloadCodec(codec).response(response).body(ResponseBodies.asByteBuffer(context, response))` for non-streaming outputs, then `outputShapeBuilder`/`errorShapeBuilder` and `deserialize()`; errors use the same deserializer with the error builder, after `HttpErrorDeserializer` resolves the type. Decide per protocol: whether to set `payloadMediaType` on the response deserializer (it enables Content-Type validation, which the legacy SDK does not do), whether restJson1 keeps the legacy greedy-label leading-slash trim (the port keeps the slash), legacy `{}` for a null structure payload (`allowEmptyStructPayload`), and host prefixes. **For 7.19.** A `@streaming` payload member is skipped in both directions and the response body is not read (`HttpBinding.hasStreamingPayload`); the protocol sets the request `ContentStreamProvider`, `Content-Type` (`@mediaType` or `application/octet-stream`) and length, and hands the unread response content to the `ResponseTransformer`. Do not pass `body(...)` for streaming outputs.
## `core/sdk-core` client protocol SPI (task 3.16)

In `software.amazon.awssdk.core.client.protocol` (all `@SdkPublicApi`): `MessageExchange`, `HttpMessageExchange`, `ClientProtocol`, `ClientProtocolFactory`, `ProtocolSettings`, `ClientTransport` (ports), and the SDK additions `ClientProtocolFactories`, `AsyncClientTransport` and `AsyncResponseHandoff`. New keys: `SdkClientOption.CLIENT_PROTOCOL`, `CLIENT_PROTOCOL_FACTORY`, `CLIENT_TRANSPORT`, `ASYNC_CLIENT_TRANSPORT`; `SdkExecutionAttribute.API_OPERATION`, `CLIENT_PROTOCOL`, `ERROR_REGISTRY`. Nothing in the SDK sets or reads them yet (tasks 3.17, 3.23, 3.24).

**Module.** `sdk-core` now depends on `schema` (`ApiOperation`, `Schema`, `ShapeId`, `Trait`, `TypeRegistry`) and on `codec-commons` (`ClientProtocol.payloadCodec()` returns `software.amazon.awssdk.codecs.commons.Codec`, which task 3.5 put in `codec-commons`, see that section). The design's dependency graph has only the `schema` edge. Neither module depends on `sdk-core`, so there is no cycle; both are already in `bom`.

| Source | Port | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| `ClientProtocol.createRequest`, `setServiceEndpoint` | `serializeRequest`, `updateServiceEndpoint`; otherwise the same parameters in the same order | OD-3 (SEP names, Requirement 11) |
| `Context` parameters (`ClientProtocol`, `ClientTransport`) | `ExecutionAttributes` | OD-4, Requirement 37.5 |
| `SmithyUri` (`serializeRequest`), smithy-java `Endpoint` (`updateServiceEndpoint`) | `java.net.URI`, `software.amazon.awssdk.endpoints.Endpoint` | Design A.10 |
| `deserializeResponse` throws `CallException` | Javadoc contract: a modeled or base `SdkServiceException` for an error response, `SdkClientException` for an unparseable success body | Design A.10, Requirements 11.13, 11.14 |
| `ClientTransport extends Closeable` with a default no-op `close() throws IOException`; static `remapExceptions` | `extends SdkAutoCloseable`, `close()` abstract; no `remapExceptions` | SDK convention; SDK exception mapping is kept (Requirement 13.5). `SdkClientConfiguration.close()` closes the `CLIENT_TRANSPORT` and `ASYNC_CLIENT_TRANSPORT` values (tested), so an abstract `close()` makes each transport state what it releases |
| `HttpMessageExchange` (`client.http`, over smithy-java `HttpRequest`/`HttpResponse`) | `core.client.protocol`, over `SdkHttpFullRequest`/`SdkHttpFullResponse`; adds `requestType()`, `responseType()` and a `toString()` naming both | Design A.10 (Requirements 12.5, 13.2) |
| `MessageExchange` | Unchanged (empty marker); Javadoc asks implementations to name their types in `toString()` | The design keeps it empty. The client-build mismatch check (task 3.23) can name a third-party exchange's types only through `toString()` |
| `ProtocolSettings` (no validation; `serviceSchema(...)` sets `service` only if `service` was set first) | Builder interface with `toBuilder()` (SDK `ToCopyableBuilder` convention). `build()` takes the service ID from the schema when `service` is unset, in either call order; requires one of them (`NullPointerException`); rejects a non-service schema or a schema whose ID differs from `service` (`IllegalArgumentException`) | Order-independent builder; the protocols resolve relative error codes against `service()`, so it is never null |
| — | `ProtocolSettings.baseExceptionBuilder()` (`Supplier<? extends SdkServiceException.Builder>`), default `SdkServiceException::builder` | Design A.10, Requirement 23.5. `sdk-core` cannot reference `AwsServiceException`, so the bound is the `sdk-core` base type; generated clients pass `<Svc>Exception::builder` |
| `ClientProtocolFactory<T extends Trait>` | `T extends software.amazon.awssdk.schema.traits.Trait` | Requirement 1.6 (design D.14) |
| — | `ClientProtocolFactories.forId(ShapeId)`: `ServiceLoader` over `ClientProtocolFactory`, with `ClassLoaderHelper.classLoader(ClientProtocolFactory.class)` (as the SDK HTTP client loader), run once under double-checked locking and published through a `volatile` unmodifiable map. Unknown ID: `SdkClientException("No ClientProtocolFactory registered for <id>")`. A factory with a null ID, or two factory classes for one ID, throw `IllegalStateException`; the same class listed twice is kept once | Requirements 11.12, 11.15. Same once-only discovery as `TraitFactories` (task 1.7). If discovery throws, nothing is cached and the next call retries |
| — | `AsyncClientTransport.send(ExecutionAttributes, RequestT, AsyncResponseHandoff<ResponseT>)` returning `CompletableFuture<ResponseT>` | OD-17; smithy-java has no async transport |
| — | `AsyncResponseHandoff`: `boolean onHeaders(ResponseT)` once, when status and headers arrive; if it returns `true`, `onStream(Publisher<ByteBuffer>)` once with the body and the future completes with the headers-only response; if `false` (or no hand-off is given), the transport buffers the body | The design names the type without a shape. Letting the caller decide after the headers keeps error bodies buffered for the protocol, so a response transformer never sees an error body (Requirement 14.3). Revisit with the streaming work (design A.13) |
| — | `SdkClientOption.ASYNC_CLIENT_TRANSPORT`, in addition to the task's `CLIENT_TRANSPORT` | `ClientTransport` and `AsyncClientTransport` are unrelated types, and `SdkClientOption` already keeps sync and async HTTP clients in separate keys (`SYNC_HTTP_CLIENT`, `ASYNC_HTTP_CLIENT`) |
| — | `SdkExecutionAttribute.ERROR_REGISTRY` is typed `TypeRegistry`, not `ErrorRegistry` | Matches `ApiOperation.errorRegistry()` and `deserializeResponse`'s parameter; the Dynamic_Client composes plain registries (design C), and `ErrorRegistry` is `@SdkProtectedApi` while `SdkExecutionAttribute` is public |

**Tests.** smithy-java has no tests for these types. SDK tests in `core/sdk-core/src/test/java/software/amazon/awssdk/core/client/protocol/`: `ClientProtocolFactoriesTest` (a factory registered in the test `META-INF/services` file is found and creates a protocol with the requested ID; repeated and concurrent first lookups return one instance; the not-found message; null ID; duplicate, conflicting and null-ID factories), `ProtocolSettingsTest` (values, defaults, service from schema in either order, the three validation failures, `toBuilder`, `toString`), `HttpMessageExchangeTest` (singleton, types, `toString`) and `ProtocolOptionsAndAttributesTest` (the four options and three attributes store and return their values; closing the configuration closes both transports once).

## `core/sdk-core` SDK HTTP client transports (task 3.17)

In `software.amazon.awssdk.core.internal.http.transport` (`@SdkInternalApi`): `SdkHttpClientTransport`, `SdkAsyncHttpClientTransport`, `TransportExecutionAttribute`, and the package-private `ResponseBodyAggregator` and `TransportFailures`. In `software.amazon.awssdk.core.client.protocol`, next to the transport SPI: `OwnedResponseBody` (`@SdkProtectedApi`; its transport-side `of`, `attach`, `detach` and `content` are `@SdkInternalApi` methods). smithy-java has no counterpart (its transports are `java.net.http`, not ported); the request-sending code is the legacy `MakeHttpRequestStage`/`MakeAsyncHttpRequestStage`, re-implemented without `RequestExecutionContext`. Nothing creates these transports yet (tasks 3.23, 3.26, 3.27).

| Design (A.12) or legacy behavior | This task | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| Close: "the adapter wraps the HTTP client in `NonManagedSdkHttpClient` when the user supplied it" | `create(client, closeHttpClient)`; `close()` closes the client once, only when `closeHttpClient` is `true` | The configuration's `SYNC_HTTP_CLIENT`/`ASYNC_HTTP_CLIENT` is already wrapped in `NonManaged*` for a user client and is closed by `SdkClientConfiguration.close()`, which also closes `CLIENT_TRANSPORT`/`ASYNC_CLIENT_TRANSPORT` (task 3.16). A transport over that client must therefore pass `false` or the SDK-created client is closed twice. Task 3.23 should pass `false` for the configuration's client; `true` is for a client created only for the transport. The ownership decision itself (user vs SDK client) is unchanged |
| Legacy sync stage wraps every body in `BytesWrittenTrackingInputStream` + `LengthAwareInputStream`, hiding `contentAsByteBufferOrNull()` (task 3.4) | A body whose buffer holds exactly the `Content-Length` bytes (or has no `Content-Length`) is given to the client as the same provider, unwrapped. Any other body gets the legacy wrapper | Requirement 13.6. A buffer whose length differs from the header goes through the wrapper, so the legacy truncate/fail behavior is unchanged |
| Legacy request-bytes metrics for that body (`firstByteWritten`/`lastByteWritten`/`bytesWritten` from the counting stream) | For a zero-copy body the transport records `bytesWritten = remaining()` and first = last = headers-read time after `call()` returns. `WRITE_THROUGHPUT` is then not reported (zero duration), as for a single-buffer body on the legacy async path | The client sends the buffer in one write, so there is nothing to count. Streamed bodies are counted as before |
| Legacy stages report `SERVICE_CALL_DURATION`, `TIME_TO_FIRST_BYTE`, register the request with the call/attempt timeout trackers, create the HTTP metric collector and check `InterruptMonitor` | The transport writes `API_CALL_ATTEMPT_START_NANO_TIME` and `HEADERS_READ_END_NANO_TIME` and the request/response byte counters (`REQUEST_BODY_METRICS`, `RESPONSE_BYTES_READ`) when present. It takes the HTTP metric collector and an `Abortable` listener from `TransportExecutionAttribute`. Reporting metrics, timeout trackers and interrupt checks stay in the pipeline (tasks 3.25–3.27) | Design A.12: "timeouts, metrics and abort tracking stay in the pipeline". The transport API carries only the request, so pipeline inputs travel as execution attributes; each is optional |
| Legacy async stage adds `Content-Length` when the body publisher declares a length | Not added; the request's headers are sent unchanged | Requirement 13.4; the protocol sets `Content-Length` |
| Legacy async streaming request body comes from `RequestExecutionContext.requestProvider()` | `TransportExecutionAttribute.ASYNC_REQUEST_BODY`; otherwise `SimpleHttpContentPublisher` over the request's content (zero copy through task 3.4) | The transport has no `RequestExecutionContext` |
| Exceptions: the legacy stages let the raw failure reach `RetryableStageHelper.setLastException`, which wraps non-SDK exceptions | Sync: `IOException` → `SdkClientException("Unable to execute HTTP request: <message>", cause)`; runtime exceptions propagate unchanged. Async: `CompletionException` unwrapped, `SdkException`/`Error` unchanged, anything else wrapped as for sync | Same type, message and cause as the Baseline_Build, so `RetryOnExceptionsCondition` classifies them the same (tested with the default retryable set). The mapping happens in the transport because a third-party transport may not go through the legacy helper |
| Racecar E10 (`BufferedResponseBody`) | `ResponseBodyAggregator`, a Reactive Streams subscriber (TCK whitebox verified) that copies each chunk once into an array presized from `Content-Length` (doubling growth when unknown or exceeded, capped at `Integer.MAX_VALUE - 8`); the response content is an `AbortableInputStream` over that array | Re-implemented (OD-16); no racecar code |
| Racecar E11 owned-buffer marker | `OwnedResponseBody`: the async transport stores the body under a private execution attribute; `OwnedResponseBody.ownedBuffer(attrs, response)` returns `ByteBuffer.wrap(array, 0, length)` only if `response.content()` is still the transport's stream and nothing has been read from it, else `null` (caller reads the stream). Each `send` clears the previous attempt's body | Task 3.19 (`ResponseBodies.asByteBuffer`, `http-client-protocol`) needs it across modules, hence `@SdkProtectedApi`, which must not be in an `.internal` package (`PackageContainmentTest`); it was first placed in `core.internal.http.transport` and moved to `core.client.protocol`, the package of the transport/protocol contract (`ClientTransport`, `AsyncResponseHandoff`). The methods only the transport calls are public there and marked `@SdkInternalApi`. Checking the content identity keeps it correct when an interceptor replaces or reads the content (`modifyHttpResponseContent`) |
| — | Async hand-off: `onHeaders` is called with the headers-only response; on `true` the body publisher goes to `onStream` and the future completes with the headers-only response; on `false`, or with no hand-off, the body is buffered. A hand-off that throws fails the future and the body is cancelled | Follows the 3.16 `AsyncResponseHandoff` contract |
| — | Cancellation: failing or cancelling the returned future fails the HTTP client's future (`CompletableFutureUtils.forwardExceptionTo`) and cancels a body being buffered, or a body that arrives later. A client that completes without headers fails the future; one that completes after headers without calling `onStream` yields an empty body | Async guidelines: the future always completes, once, and nothing blocks. Completion runs on the HTTP client's thread; moving it to the future-completion executor is the pipeline's job (task 3.27) |

**Tests** (`core/sdk-core/src/test/java/software/amazon/awssdk/core/internal/http/transport/`): `SdkHttpClientTransportTest` (pass-through of method, URI, headers and body; no body; a `ByteBufferOutputStream` body reaches the client as the same provider and array, writable and array-backed; stream-only body through the length check; a buffer shorter than `Content-Length` fails as the legacy check does; response status, headers and body stream unchanged; IOException mapping and retry classification; runtime and SDK exceptions unchanged; metric collector and abortable hook; attempt times; byte counts for zero-copy and streamed bodies; close ownership), `SdkAsyncHttpClientTransportTest` (pass-through; zero-copy publish; streaming request body; HTTP options; multi-chunk aggregation with and without `Content-Length` into one owned buffer; empty body; no `onStream`; no headers; error mid-stream; execute-future failure; SDK exceptions and errors unwrapped; synchronous throw; cancellation while buffering and before the body; hand-off accept, reject and throw; owned buffer invalidated by replaced or read content; metrics; close ownership) and `ResponseBodyAggregatorTckTest`. The pass-through property is optional task 3.18.

## `core/protocols/http-client-protocol` (task 3.19)

In `software.amazon.awssdk.protocols.client.http`: `HttpClientProtocol`, `HttpErrorDeserializer`, `AmznErrorHeaderExtractor`, `ErrorTypeUtils` (ports, `@SdkPublicApi`), and the SDK addition `ResponseBodies` (`@SdkProtectedApi`). `ResponseBodies` reads the async transport's owned body through `software.amazon.awssdk.core.client.protocol.OwnedResponseBody` (moved there from `core.internal.http.transport`, see task 3.17). In `.internal`: `MarshallBufferSizeTracker` (`@SdkInternalApi`). Ported from smithy-java `client/client-http`; racecar E1, E3.3 gate and E11 are re-implemented (OD-16), no racecar code.

**Module.** Declares `utils`, `schema`, `codec-commons`, `http-client-spi`, `endpoints-spi`, `sdk-core` and `aws-core` (for `AwsServiceException`/`AwsErrorDetails`), and `cbor-codec` for tests only. `http-binding` and `eventstream` stay declared, as the design's module table lists them, and are in the module's `ignoredUnusedDeclaredDependencies` until tasks 7.13 and 7.20 use them. The task 1.2 javadoc and dependency-analysis skips are removed.

| Source | Port | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| `HttpClientProtocol` implements `createRequest`/`deserializeResponse` in each subclass | Template methods: `serializeRequest` and `deserializeResponse` are `final` and call the abstract `createRequest`, `deserializeOutput` and `createError`. A non-`SdkException` runtime exception becomes `SdkClientException("Unable to marshall <op name>: <cause message>")` / `"Unable to unmarshall ..."` with the cause (an `UncheckedIOException` is unwrapped to its `IOException`, so retry classification sees the I/O failure); `SdkException`s pass through. A response that `isErrorResponse` (default: not 2xx) reports as an error goes to `createError`, whose result is thrown | Requirement 11.14 and the `ClientProtocol` Javadoc contract hold for every subclass. `<op>` is `ApiOperation.name()` (the shape name users call) |
| `setServiceEndpoint` (`SmithyUri.withEndpoint`; merges the endpoint's HTTP headers) | `updateServiceEndpoint`: scheme, host and port from `Endpoint.url()`; path `SdkHttpUtils.appendUri(endpoint raw path, request encoded path)`; query, headers and body unchanged; endpoint URL query and `Endpoint.headers()` ignored | Requirement 11.8 says headers are unchanged; the SDK pipeline applies endpoint headers. `appendUri` is the joining `AwsEndpointProviderUtils.setUri` uses today |
| — | `newRequestBodyBuffer(op)` / `finishRequestBody(op, out)` on `HttpClientProtocol`, backed by `MarshallBufferSizeTracker` (racecar E1) | Design D.6 (E1). ApiOperation has no ordinal, so each protocol instance assigns one: the slot of the operation in an identity hash table (`System.identityHashCode`, linear probing) whose parallel `int[]` holds the hints. Reads are lock-free volatile reads; a new operation is added by copy-and-CAS. Hint writes are plain racy `int` writes (documented as benign: only a starting capacity). Hint = last body size + 1/8, clamped to [32 B, 1 MiB]; unknown operations start at 256 B; at most 4096 operations are tracked so per-call `ApiOperation` instances cannot grow it without bound |
| `HttpErrorDeserializer` returns `CallException`; `UnknownErrorFactory` builds unknown errors from hints | Returns `SdkServiceException`; the unknown-error path is the service base exception from `baseExceptionBuilder` (default `AwsServiceException::builder`; `protocolSettings(ProtocolSettings)` copies the service ID, the settings' supplier and `awsQueryCompatible` from the service schema's trait) | Requirements 11.13, 23.5 |
| `HeaderErrorExtractor.hasHeader`/`resolveId`/`errorCode` | `errorType(response)` (raw value) and `default errorMessage(response)`; resolution is `ErrorTypeUtils.resolve` for every source | One normalization path for headers, bodies and query codes (Requirement 23.3). `AmznErrorHeaderExtractor` adds `X-Amzn-Error-Message`, which `AwsJsonErrorMessageParser` reads first today, and an `INSTANCE` |
| `ErrorPayloadParser.parsePayload` resolves the ID and calls the known-error factory; `extractErrorType(Document, ns)` | `parse(codec, response, body)` returns `ErrorPayload` (error type, message, request ID). `DEFAULT_ERROR_PAYLOAD_PARSER` (`__type`, then `code`) and `TYPE_ONLY_ERROR_PAYLOAD_PARSER` (`__type`, rpcv2Cbor); both read the message from `message`/`Message`/`errorMessage` like `SdkJsonErrorMessageParser` | The deserializer needs code, message and request ID even when no modeled error applies. A body request ID (XML `RequestId`) wins over headers, as in `AwsXmlErrorUnmarshaller` |
| `KnownErrorFactory.createError`/`createErrorFromDocument` builds the error | `populateError(context, codec, response, body, builder)` only populates the registry's builder; the deserializer then sets SDK fields and builds. Default: `builder.deserialize(codec.createDeserializer(body))` for a non-empty body | The SDK fields must be set on the builder before `build()`. The SDK `Document` has no `deserializeInto`, so the body is parsed twice (document for the discriminator, then the builder); error responses are not a hot path |
| Parse failures caught: `SerializationException`, `DiscriminatorException` | Any runtime exception while reading the body, parsing it, creating the builder, populating or building the modeled error falls back to the base exception (logged at DEBUG); a registry builder that is not an `SdkServiceException.Builder` also falls back | Requirement 23.5: never a deserialization exception in place of the base exception |
| — | Discriminator order: `x-amzn-query-error` code (before `;`) when `awsQueryCompatible`, then the header extractor, then the payload. If the query code does not resolve, the protocol's own discriminator is tried. Reported error code: the query code when present, else `removeNamespaceAndUri(type)` | Requirement 23.2; matches `AwsJsonProtocolErrorUnmarshaller.getEffectiveErrorCode`. `awsQueryCompatible` implies `useErrorCodeIndex` (`resolveCode` first, Requirement 23.3) |
| — | Population (Requirement 23.4, matching `AwsJsonProtocolErrorUnmarshaller`/`AwsXmlErrorUnmarshaller`): `message` (header message, then payload message, then a modeled `message` member), `statusCode`, `requestId` (payload, then `x-amzn-RequestId`, then `x-amz-request-id`), `extendedRequestId` (`x-amz-id-2`); for `AwsServiceException.Builder` also `AwsErrorDetails` (code, message, `SERVICE_NAME`, raw body, `sdkHttpResponse`) and `clockSkew` from `TIME_OFFSET` | Legacy JSON replaces a missing message with `"Service returned error code X"`/`"... HTTP status code N"`; this port leaves it null (Requirement 23.5 "null if absent"; `AwsServiceException.getMessage()` still prints service, status and request IDs). Only a plain `SdkServiceException` base, which has no code field, gets `"Service returned error code X"`. Revisit if a 3.22/7.x parity case needs the legacy JSON text |
| `ErrorTypeUtils` (`readType`, `readTypeAndCode`, `removeUri`, `removeNamespaceAndUri`) | Same, plus `readMessage`, `queryErrorCode` and `resolve(type, namespace, registry, useErrorCodeIndex)`: `resolveCode` first (only when the registry is an `ErrorRegistry`), drop from the first `:`, exact match for `#` IDs, else the shape name in the service namespace (also the fallback for an absolute ID from another namespace, as `AmznErrorHeaderExtractor.toShapeId` does). Uses `TypeRegistry.contains` so no error class is loaded; member IDs and invalid IDs resolve to null | Requirement 23.3. A composed `TypeRegistry` (Dynamic_Client) has no code index, so custom codes resolve only through an `ErrorRegistry` |
| `DataStream.asByteBuffer()` | `ResponseBodies.asByteBuffer(context, response)`: the async transport's owned buffer (`OwnedResponseBody.ownedBuffer`) with no copy (E11); else an exact-size array for a valid `Content-Length` (a shorter body is returned as read, a longer one continues in a growable array); else a doubling array from 1 KiB, not trimmed (E3.3 gate). Reads to EOF without closing; I/O failures are `UncheckedIOException` | Design D.6 (E3.3 gate, E11). `@SdkProtectedApi` because every protocol module uses it |

**Tests** (`core/protocols/http-client-protocol/src/test/java/software/amazon/awssdk/protocols/client/http/`, with hand-written `TestShapes` standing in for generated classes and the CBOR codec): `HttpClientProtocolTest` (ID and exchange; path joining for 7 endpoint/path cases including trailing slashes, encoded segments and empty paths; scheme/host/port taken, query/headers/body kept, endpoint headers and query ignored, original unchanged, default port; serialize round trip with a zero-copy body; buffer hint per operation and per protocol instance; marshall failure messages and cause, SDK exceptions passed through; success deserialization; unmarshall failure for a malformed body and for an I/O failure with the `IOException` cause; error and 3xx responses throw the created error), `HttpErrorDeserializerTest` (modeled error with members, status, request IDs, error details, raw body and message; `code` with the default parser only; header type and message precedence; unknown type, no discriminator and empty body, malformed body, wrong member type, unreadable body, non-exception registry entry and null registry fall back to the base exception; an error the operation does not declare still resolves (23.7); custom codes with the index; `x-amzn-query-error` first and fallback to the body type; `protocolSettings`; plain `SdkServiceException` base; payload request ID precedence; a custom known-error factory; builder validation; header extractor), `ErrorTypeUtilsTest` (11 normalization cases, code index on `ErrorRegistry` vs plain registry, null inputs, type/code/message readers, URI/namespace stripping, `queryErrorCode`), `ResponseBodiesTest` (exact-size array, partial reads, shorter and longer than `Content-Length`, unknown length at 6 sizes, invalid `Content-Length`, no content, read failure, the async transport's owned buffer returned by identity and ignored when the content is replaced) and `internal/MarshallBufferSizeTrackerTest` (default, last-size hint, clamping, identity keys, table growth, the operation cap, concurrent first records). The endpoint-update property is optional task 3.20.

**Added in task 3.21.** `HttpClientProtocol.buildOutput(builder, response)` (`protected final`, SDK addition) builds every protocol's output: an `AwsResponse.Builder` gets `DefaultAwsResponseMetadata` with the first value of every header plus `AWS_REQUEST_ID` from `x-amzn-RequestId`/`x-amz-request-id` (the map `AwsJsonResponseHandler` builds), an `SdkResponse.Builder` gets the HTTP response through `SchemaSerdeSupport.buildResponse` before `build()`, and any other builder (Dynamic_Client documents) is built as is. Design A.10 puts this on `HttpClientProtocol`; doing it on the builder avoids the `toBuilder()` copy the legacy handlers make, so task 3.24's `ProtocolResponseHandling` receives outputs that already carry `sdkHttpResponse`. `HttpClientProtocolTest.buildOutputAttachesTheHttpResponseAndResponseMetadata` covers it with an `AwsResponse`-based `TestShapes.GreetingResponse`.

## `core/protocols/rpcv2-cbor-client-protocol` (task 3.21)

In `software.amazon.awssdk.protocols.client.rpcv2`: `AbstractRpcV2ClientProtocol` (`@SdkProtectedApi`) and `RpcV2CborProtocol` with the nested `Factory` (`@SdkPublicApi`), registered in `META-INF/services/software.amazon.awssdk.core.client.protocol.ClientProtocolFactory`. Ported from smithy-java 1.7.0 `client/client-rpcv2` and `client/client-rpcv2-cbor`; the two smithy-java modules are one SDK module (design module table).

**Module.** Declares `annotations`, `utils`, `schema`, `codec-commons`, `cbor-codec`, `http-client-spi`, `sdk-core` and `http-client-protocol`; `aws-core` is test-only (the test shapes extend `AwsResponse`/`AwsServiceException`; the main code reaches it only through `http-client-protocol`). The task 1.2 javadoc and dependency-analysis skips and the unused `test-utils`/`jqwik` test dependencies are removed (property test 3.22 is optional and adds jqwik back if implemented).

| Source | Port | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| `AbstractRpcV2ClientProtocol` is `@SmithyInternalApi` (design D.14 Porting_Map: `@SdkInternalApi`) | `@SdkProtectedApi`, in the public package | `PackageContainmentTest` requires `@SdkInternalApi` types to be in an `internal` package, and the class is the superclass of the public `RpcV2CborProtocol` (and of a future rpcv2Json protocol), so it cannot move there. Protected API, like `ResponseBodies` |
| `AbstractRpcV2ClientProtocol(protocolId, service, mediaType)` with an abstract `codec()` and a lazily built `HttpErrorDeserializer` | `(protocolId, ProtocolSettings, codec, mediaType)`; `payloadCodec()` is `final` and the error deserializer is built in the constructor with `protocolSettings(settings)` and `TYPE_ONLY_ERROR_PAYLOAD_PARSER` | `ProtocolSettings` carries the base exception supplier and the service schema (awsQueryCompatible). Passing the codec avoids calling an overridable method from the constructor and the unsynchronized lazy field |
| `RpcV2CborProtocol(ShapeId service)`; `Factory` with only the implicit constructor | `RpcV2CborProtocol(ProtocolSettings)`; `Factory.INSTANCE` plus a public no-argument constructor for `ServiceLoader`; `createProtocol` returns `RpcV2CborProtocol` (covariant) and ignores the trait, as smithy-java does | Design A.11: generated builders reference `Factory.INSTANCE` directly. The codec is `Rpcv2CborCodec` with `CborSettings.RPCV2` |
| Template `HttpRequest` with `smithy-protocol` and `Accept`; `endpoint.withConcatPath("/service/<svc>/operation/<op>")` | Fresh `SdkHttpFullRequest.Builder` per call: `POST`, path `SdkHttpUtils.appendUri(endpoint raw path, "/service/<service shape name>/operation/<ApiOperation.name()>")`, `smithy-protocol: rpc-v2-cbor`, `Accept: application/cbor` | Same wire result; `appendUri` is the joining `HttpClientProtocol.updateServiceEndpoint` uses. The service name is `ProtocolSettings.service().getName()` (for CloudWatch the converter's `GraniteServiceVersion20100801`) |
| Body `codec().serialize(input)` (`DataStream.ofByteBuffer`, which sets the content type and length) | Body serialized into `newRequestBodyBuffer(op)` and sent as `finishRequestBody(op, out)` (the zero-copy `ByteBufferOutputStream.toContentStreamProvider()`), with explicit `Content-Type: application/cbor` and `Content-Length` | Per-operation buffer hint (E1) and zero-copy body (Requirement 13.6). The SDK request has no implicit length, so the protocol sets it as `JsonProtocolMarshaller` does; the `empty_input` protocol test requires it |
| Unit input: `DataStream.ofEmpty()`, no `Content-Type` | No content provider, no `Content-Type`, `Content-Length: 0`. Unit is detected by the `unitType` trait on the input schema, which the generated synthetic request classes carry | `no_input` protocol test forbids `Content-Type` and requires an empty body. `Content-Length: 0` matches what smithy-java's empty `DataStream` sends and what Apache adds for an entity-less `POST`, so every HTTP client sends the same request |
| — | `x-amzn-query-mode: true` on every request when the service schema has `aws.protocols#awsQueryCompatible`; `x-amzn-query-error` resolution comes from `HttpErrorDeserializer.protocolSettings` | Not in smithy-java's rpcv2 client. Design A.10 and Requirement 23.2; matches `JsonProtocolMarshaller` |
| Error when `statusCode() != 200` | `HttpClientProtocol.isErrorResponse` default: any status outside 2xx | Matches the legacy SDK's success classification (Requirement 23.8 compares with the Baseline_Build). No rpcv2Cbor protocol test uses a non-200 2xx code |
| Output: empty `content.contentLength() == 0` → `builder.build()`; otherwise `codec().deserializeShape(bytes, builder)` | `ResponseBodies.asByteBuffer(context, response)` (the async transport's owned buffer without a copy); a non-empty body is deserialized into the builder; `errorCorrection()` then `buildOutput` in both cases | One build path, so empty outputs also get `sdkHttpResponse` and response metadata. Generated builders do not override `errorCorrection()`, so calling it for an empty body changes nothing for them |
| Error type `DocumentDeserializer.parseDiscriminator(removeUri(readType(doc)), ns)` | `TYPE_ONLY_ERROR_PAYLOAD_PARSER` plus `ErrorTypeUtils.resolve` (task 3.19): an absolute `__type` from another namespace falls back to the shape name in the service namespace | Same as the 3.19 resolution for every protocol, and the legacy SDK's namespace-stripping code match |
| Event streams: `RpcEventStreamsUtil`, `AwsEventEncoderFactory`/`AwsEventDecoderFactory` | Not ported yet: an operation with an input or output event stream fails with `UnsupportedOperationException`, which `HttpClientProtocol` turns into `SdkClientException("Unable to (un)marshall <op>: ...")` | Event streams are task 7.20 (`aws-event-streams` lands in `http-client-protocol` then) |

**Tests** (`core/protocols/rpcv2-cbor-client-protocol/src/test/java/software/amazon/awssdk/protocols/client/rpcv2/`, with hand-written `TestShapes` shaped like generated classes; the CloudWatch generated classes are not used because `services/cloudwatch` depends on this module): `RpcV2CborProtocolTest` covers ID, codec settings and exchange; method, URI, the exact header set and exact body bytes (`{"message":"hi"}` as an indefinite-length map) with a zero-copy body and matching `Content-Length`; endpoint path prefixes; HTTP binding traits and `jsonName` ignored on input and output; Unit input; `x-amzn-query-mode` only for awsQueryCompatible services; output with `sdkHttpResponse` and response metadata; no content, empty body and empty map as empty outputs; malformed success body as `SdkClientException`; absolute, relative and URI-suffixed `__type` resolving to the modeled error with status, request ID, code and message; another namespace's absolute type; unknown type, no `__type` (`code` member and `X-Amzn-Errortype` ignored), no body and malformed body as the base exception; `x-amzn-query-error` resolved first (and over the body type) for awsQueryCompatible services and ignored otherwise; `ClientProtocolFactories.forId` discovery, `Factory.INSTANCE`, and `createProtocol` from settings with the default base exception. The generated Smithy protocol tests run in task 3.34.

## `core/protocols/aws-json-client-protocol` (task 7.11)

In `software.amazon.awssdk.protocols.client.awsjson`: `AwsJsonProtocol` (package-private, `@SdkProtectedApi`), and `AwsJson1Protocol` and `AwsJson11Protocol` with their nested `Factory` (`@SdkPublicApi`), both registered in `META-INF/services/software.amazon.awssdk.core.client.protocol.ClientProtocolFactory`. Ported from smithy-java `aws/client/aws-client-awsjson` (commit `142eecf4d`), on the task 3.19 `HttpClientProtocol` and `HttpErrorDeserializer` and the task 7.1 `JsonCodec`.

**Module.** Declares `annotations`, `utils`, `schema`, `codec-commons`, `json-codec`, `http-client-spi`, `sdk-core` and `http-client-protocol`; `aws-core` is test-only, as in `rpcv2-cbor-client-protocol`. The task 1.2 javadoc and dependency-analysis skips and the unused `test-utils`/`jqwik` test dependencies are removed (property tests 7.12 and 7.16 are optional and add jqwik back if implemented).

| Source | Port | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| `abstract sealed class AwsJsonProtocol ... permits AwsJson1Protocol, AwsJson11Protocol` | Package-private `abstract class`, `@SdkProtectedApi`; `createRequest`, `deserializeOutput`, `createError` and `payloadCodec` are `final` | Java 8 has no `sealed`; package-private plus final template methods keeps the two subclasses the only ones (design D.14). Checkstyle `MissingSdkAnnotation` requires an API annotation on every class, and `PackageContainmentTest` forbids `@SdkInternalApi` outside `internal` |
| `AwsJsonProtocol(trait, service, errorPayloadParser)`; abstract `contentType()`; `AwsJson1Protocol(ShapeId service)`; `Factory` with the implicit constructor and `Objects.requireNonNull(settings.service())` | `AwsJsonProtocol(protocolId, ProtocolSettings, contentType)`; `AwsJson1Protocol(ProtocolSettings)`/`AwsJson11Protocol(ProtocolSettings)`; `Factory.INSTANCE`, a public no-argument constructor for `ServiceLoader`, covariant `createProtocol` that ignores the trait (may be null) | As `RpcV2CborProtocol` (task 3.21): `ProtocolSettings` carries the base exception supplier and the service schema, and `ProtocolSettings.service()` is never null. Passing the content type avoids calling an overridable method from the constructor |
| `JsonCodec.builder().defaultNamespace(ns).useTimestampFormat(true)` | `JsonCodec.builder().settings(JsonSettings.AWS_JSON).defaultNamespace(ns)`: `useJsonName=false`, `useTimestampFormat=true`, epoch-seconds default, one codec per protocol instance | Same settings; `AWS_JSON` is the design's preset (Requirement 11.10). `defaultNamespace` has no effect until `JsonDocuments` (task 11.5) |
| `HttpRequest` with `X-Amz-Target` and `Content-Type`; `setUri(endpoint)`; body `codec.serialize(input)` as a `DataStream` | Fresh `SdkHttpFullRequest.Builder`: `POST`, path `SdkHttpUtils.appendUri(endpoint raw path, "/")`, `X-Amz-Target: <service shape name>.<ApiOperation.name()>`, body written into `newRequestBodyBuffer(op)` and sent as `finishRequestBody(op, out)`, explicit `Content-Type` and `Content-Length` | Same wire request. `appendUri(path, "/")` is what the legacy `ProtocolUtils.createSdkHttpRequest` does with the `/` request URI, so an endpoint path `/prefix` gives `/prefix/` on both paths. Buffer hint (E1) and zero-copy body as in task 3.21 |
