| `AwsJson10ClientPopulatesDefaultsValuesWhenMissingInResponse`, `RestJsonClientPopulatesDefaultsValuesWhenMissingInResponse` | awsJson1_0, restJson1 | A member missing from the response stays null; same decision |
| `AwsJson10ClientPopulatesNestedDefaultValuesWhenMissing`, `RestJsonClientPopulatesNestedDefaultValuesWhenMissing` | awsJson1_0, restJson1 | `@default` values of members of nested structures, list elements and map values are not populated on input; same decision |
| `AwsJson10ClientPopulatesNestedDefaultsWhenMissingInResponseBody`, `RestJsonClientPopulatesNestedDefaultsWhenMissingInResponseBody` | awsJson1_0, restJson1 | The same for output; same decision |
| `AwsJson10ClientErrorCorrectsWhenServerFailsToSerializeRequiredValues`, `AwsJson10ClientErrorCorrectsWithDefaultValuesWhenServerFailsToSerializeRequiredValues` | awsJson1_0 | No client error correction: a `@required` output member the response omits stays null instead of taking its zero or `@default` value, as in the legacy SDK; part of the same open `@default` decision |
| `RestJsonHttpChecksumRequired` | restJson1 | SDK checksum behavior: since 2.30.0 the shared `HttpChecksumStage` sends a CRC32 flexible checksum (`x-amz-checksum-crc32`) for `@httpChecksumRequired`, and `Content-MD5` only when no algorithm resolves (`HttpChecksumUtils.isMd5ChecksumRequired`). Both paths fail the case the same way; the protocol's request matches it otherwise |
| `SdkRestJsonNoContentWithBlobPayload`, `SdkRestXmlNoContentWithBlobPayload` | restJson1, restXml | **Behavior change, open question.** On the schema path an empty body leaves a blob `@httpPayload` unset (null), as the upstream `RestJsonHttpPayloadTraitsWithNoBlobBody`/`HttpPayloadTraitsWithNoBlobBody` require (both pass); the legacy SDK returns empty `SdkBytes`, which these SDK-owned cases (legacy-disposition.csv `rest-core-output.json#6`) expect |

**Not covered.** Event streams: the generator does not select `smithy.test#eventStreamTests`, and event-stream operations stay on the legacy path (task 7.20 stages (c)–(e) deferred by the user). `legacy-disposition.csv` still records `rest-core-output.json#6` as ported to the two skipped SDK-owned cases; it changes once the blob decision is made.

**Open questions.** (1) Empty blob `@httpPayload`: keep null (upstream Smithy, smithy-java) or restore legacy empty `SdkBytes` on the new path (then skip the two upstream cases instead). (2) `@default` population and client error correction: 12 skips across awsJson1_0, restJson1 and rpcv2Cbor wait on one decision (task 3.34). (3) `RestJsonHttpChecksumRequired` is skipped as SDK checksum behavior rather than changed to `Content-MD5`.

**For checkpoint 8.** Build the protocol modules and `smithy-protocol-tests` as checkpoint 8 lists; the default `mvn clean install -pl :smithy-protocol-tests` now runs all seven protocols and should report the table above. `codegen` must be installed first: the fix above is in `codegen`, which the `codegen-maven-plugin` loads from the local repository. `test/codegen-generated-classes-test` has 40 failures that predate this task (same list as checkpoint 6). Properties 21, 23–25 and 39 and the streaming tests are not run by this module.

## Checkpoint 6: Smithy validation for C2J test models

**Target.** Since task 1.22, stage 4 of `CodegenPipeline` validates every converted C2J model and fails on `ERROR` and `DANGER` events. The 30 C2J models of `test/codegen-generated-classes-test` then failed to generate, and once they generated, some tests did not compile. Each failure was either a C2J_Converter or Smithy-route loader gap, fixed with a test, or a C2J form that a test model uses on purpose and that a Smithy model cannot hold, suppressed per service with a recorded reason. Real services stay strictly validated. `test/protocol-tests` is not covered: it is not in the reactor (Requirement 32.9; `test/smithy-protocol-tests` replaces it).

**Converter and loader fixes.**

| Component | Problem | Fix |
|---|---|---|
| `C2jInput` | Test models annotate shapes with `//` comments (`endpointproviders`), and stage 1 failed | Java comments enabled, as codegen's C2J loader does |
| C2J_Converter | codegen's C2J loader spells the big number types `bigdecimal`/`biginteger`; the converter knew only camel case (`waiters` failed in stage 3) | Both spellings converted |
| C2J_Converter | codegen reads rule-set parameter types ignoring case; Smithy accepts only `stringArray` (`stringarray`'s `StringArray` failed assembly) | Parameter type normalized to `stringArray` |
| C2J_Converter | 25 test models ship an empty `endpoint-tests.json`; as `@endpointTests` every parameter is reported unused (DANGER) and every required one missing (ERROR) | Omitted; codegen generates no endpoint tests for an empty suite either way |
| C2J_Converter | A `location: querystring` map was bound with `@httpQuery`, which Smithy does not allow on a map (also used by apigateway, amp, route53globalresolver) | Bound with `@httpQueryParams`; a `locationName` on such a member is recorded as a gap |
| C2J_Converter | C2J can mark a member streaming instead of its blob target; the converter put `@streaming`/`@requiresLength` on the member, where Smithy does not allow them | The member targets a streaming blob (the C2J blob, or a `<Name>Streaming` copy when non-streaming members also use it); optional streaming members get `@default("")`, as in S3's published model |
| C2J_Converter | Seven REST test models have an event stream output member and no `payload`; Smithy requires `@httpPayload` on it | `@httpPayload` added when the event stream is the structure's only member without an HTTP location (published AWS models always name the payload) |
| `AddSmithyOperations` | An operation whose `@auth` lists several schemes or sigv4a got the auth type `V4A`, which has no legacy signer ("Unsupported authtype for AWS Request: V4A") | Such operations get no auth type, as their C2J auth list did |
| `AddSmithyMetadata` | Without `@auth`, only sigv4 or else bearer became the service auth list; the converter omits `@auth` when a C2J auth list is already in Smithy's order (`environmenttokenprovider`: `[sigv4, httpBearerAuth]`), and sigv4a (`sigv4aonly`) was never listed | Every applied sigv4, sigv4a and bearer scheme, in `ServiceIndex` order; `NONE` if there is none |
| `test/codegen-generated-classes-test/pom.xml` | The rpcv2Cbor test client (`sdkrpcv2`) now generates and runs on the new path (task 3.23), and did not compile without `RpcV2CborProtocol` | Depends on `rpcv2-cbor-client-protocol` |
| `SmithyIntermediateModelBuilder` | The `endpointParameters` and `customOperationContextParams` customizations were not applied (`endpointproviders`, `restjson`: `OperationContextParametersTest`, `MapEndpointParamTest` did not compile) | Both routes call the same `IntermediateModelBuilder` methods |
| `AddSmithyShapes.buildHttpMapping` | An RPC protocol ignores HTTP bindings, so a streaming C2J `payload` member (kept as `@httpPayload`) was not the payload, and the awsQuery test client had no `RequestBody`/`AsyncResponseTransformer` overloads (`AsyncOperationCancelTest` did not compile) | A top-level `@httpPayload` streaming blob is the payload in every protocol, as on the C2J route. `ProtocolTestGeneratorTest`'s awsJson1_0 `@httpPayload` stream case is now generatable (`rpcProtocolHttpPayloadStream_isPassedAsARequestBody`) |
| `AddSmithyOperations` | An operation `@auth([])` (the converter's form of C2J `authtype: none`) stayed authenticated with no auth type and was signed (`NoneAuthTypeRequestTest`) | Unauthenticated, auth type `NONE`, auth `[NONE]`, as `authtype: none` on the C2J route |

**Suppression mechanism.** `customization.config` key `smithyValidationSuppressions`: a list of `{id, shapes?, reason}` (`SmithyValidationSuppression`, applied by `SmithyValidationSuppressions` in stage 4).

- Only C2J models, and only in modules whose POM sets the `codegen-maven-plugin` parameter `allowSmithyValidationSuppressions` (no user property, so not settable from the command line). `GenerationMojo` rejects it for modules whose parent is `services`. Without it, a model with suppressions fails generation. Smithy's own `@suppress` and metadata suppressions cannot be used: they never apply to `ERROR` events.
- `reason` is required; `shapes` must be absolute shape IDs, and an entry with shapes matches only events on those shapes. `id` matches as `ValidationEvent.containsId` (so `HttpMethodSemantics.UnexpectedPayload` is specific).
- `Model`, `Target` and `ResourceCycle` cannot be suppressed, and loading is checked with validation disabled first. With suppressions, validation runs in Smithy's legacy mode so that a suppressed critical validator (`TraitTarget`, `TraitValue`) does not stop the others.
- An entry that matches no `ERROR` or `DANGER` event is stale and fails generation. Each applied entry is logged as a warning with the service, event count and reason.
- `C2jTestModelsPrepareTest` (`codegen-maven-plugin`) runs stages 1 to 5 on every C2J model of the module, as `GenerationMojo` does, so a converter change that breaks one fails there.

**Suppressed events** (each also recorded in the model's `customization.config`):

| Event ID | Models | Why a suppression, not a fix |
|---|---|---|
| `SdkServiceId` | 15 models (`query`, `xml`, `h2`, `stringarray`, ...) | Test service IDs contain "Amazon"/"Aws" or end in "Service"; the generated client and package names derive from them and the tests use those names |
| `ExclusiveStructureMemberTrait` | 7 (shape `IdempotentOperationStructure`) | An idempotency token in a header, a URI label and the query string at once, to test each binding |
| `HttpMethodSemantics.UnexpectedPayload` | 8 | GET/DELETE operations with body members, to test that the SDK marshals them as C2J defines |
| `HttpUriConflict` | 6 | Test operations share request URIs; the client never routes by URI |
| `RuleSetParameter.RuleSet.UnmatchedName` | `bearerauth`, `mixedauth`, `mixedauthservice`, `multiauth`, `endpointproviders` | Shared test rule sets declare parameters the model never binds; in `endpointproviders`, `StaticStringParam` vs `staticStringParam`, which codegen matches ignoring case |
| `TraitValue` | `customresponsemetadata`, `serviceconfig` (one shape each) | Lowercase response checksum algorithms; the SDK matches them ignoring case |
| `TraitTarget` | `tostring` (one member), `documenttype` (two members) | A member-level C2J `sensitive` flag; a `RegistryName` URI label member on an operation without that label |
| `HttpLabelTrait`, `HttpPayload` | `documenttype` (same members) | Same `RegistryName` binding |
| `StreamingTrait` | `endpointproviders`, `waiters` (one shape) | A `@requiresLength` streaming structure used as both input and output |
| `WaitableTrait` | `waiters` (one shape) | Test waiters with only failure and retry acceptors |
| `RuleSetAuthSchemes` | `endpointbasedauth` | A sigv4a `signingRegionSet` that Smithy's validator rejects, to test endpoint-based auth resolution |
| `RuleSetTestCase`, `RuleSetParameter.TestCase.RequiredMissing`, `RuleSetParameter.TestCase.Unused` | `stringarray` | Endpoint test cases with an empty `stringArray` and without the required `Region` |
| `rpcv2Cbor.NoDocuments` | `sdkrpcv2` (one member) | A document member, to test documents in rpcv2Cbor |

None of these hides a converter gap that a prototype service hits: the prototype services are validated without suppressions, and `LegacyRouteParityTest` shows their generated sources unchanged.

**Open: Smithy-route behavior gaps in `test/codegen-generated-classes-test`.** The module generates and compiles, and 1633 of its 1673 tests pass. The 40 that fail, in 9 classes, are C2J-route behaviors that the C2J_Converter or the Smithy IntermediateModel does not carry yet. None of them is suppressed or skipped, and none affects the seven prototype services (`LegacyRouteParityTest`):

| Test class (failures) | Evidence | Likely gap |
|---|---|---|
| `EndpointDiscoveryAndEndpointOverrideTest` (16) | Requests go to `https://endpointoverride/`, expected `/DescribeEndpoints`, `/TestDiscoveryOptional` | RPC `requestUri` other than `/`: the converter keeps it only in the gap marker (`operation.http`), and the Smithy route sends to `/` |
| `HttpChecksumInHeaderTest` (4), `HttpChecksumRequiredTest` (2) | rest-xml body checksums differ (`0/Va+Gm0...` vs `FB/utBbw...`, `caVLcw==` vs `YP74zg==`); JSON cases pass | The rest-xml request body differs (root element or namespace) |
| `HostPrefixTest` (4) | Host `localhost`, expected `123-foo.localhost`; invalid prefix not rejected | C2J `endpoint.hostPrefix` does not reach the operation model |
| `BlobUnmarshallingTest` (8) | Four rest-xml cases (sync and async, two content lengths): a present blob payload fails with "Could not parse XML response" at [1,1], and a missing one is null instead of empty `SdkBytes`; JSON cases pass | A rest-xml blob `@httpPayload` response is read as an XML document, not as the raw body |
| `MoveQueryParamsToBodyTest` (2) | Body `Action=AllTypes`, expected `Action=QueryService.AllTypes` | The C2J `targetPrefix` of an awsQuery service is only in the metadata marker |
| `SensitiveDataRedactedTest` (1) | `ListOfListOfSensitiveString=[[Value]]` is not redacted | Sensitivity of a nested list's element target is not propagated |
| `CustomSdkShapeTest` (1) | The request body has no `SdkPartType` | The `customSdkShapes` customization is not applied on the Smithy route |
| `RpcV2CborUserAgentTest` (2) | `BadCborException: unexpected end of payload` for `OperationWithNoInputOrOutput` | New path: an empty rpcv2Cbor response body is not read as an empty structure (`rpcv2-cbor-client-protocol`) |

## Phase 5 behavior policy (user decision)

The prototype services that tasks 9.2–9.4 move to the schema path (SQS, DynamoDB, Secrets Manager, Account, Route 53 and SNS) keep the new path's Smithy-aligned behavior everywhere. The new path does not reproduce the legacy behavior below for any service. Each row is an accepted behavior change. A module test that asserts the legacy behavior is listed with this section as its reason in `docs/design/schema-serde/excluded-tests.md` (task 9.2). This closes the open questions of tasks 7.15 and 7.21.

| # | Behavior | Legacy SDK | Schema path (kept) | Where recorded | Protocols |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Form encoding of space, `*` and `~` in query request bodies | `+`, `*`, `%7E` | `%20`, `%2A`, `~` (RFC 3986 unreserved set, as smithy-java and `SdkHttpUtils.urlEncode`) | Tasks 7.5, 7.15. Both forms decode to the same values | awsQuery, ec2Query (SNS) |
| 2 | Query request `Content-Type` | `application/x-www-form-urlencoded; charset=utf-8` | `application/x-www-form-urlencoded`, as the Smithy request tests and smithy-java | Task 7.15 | awsQuery, ec2Query (SNS) |
| 3 | Request path for an endpoint with a path (`https://example.com/custom`) | `/custom` | `/custom/` (`QueryHostWithPath`, `Ec2QueryHostWithPath`) | Task 7.15 | awsQuery, ec2Query (SNS) |
| 4 | Empty response body for a blob `@httpPayload` output member | Empty `SdkBytes` | Unset (null), as the upstream `RestJsonHttpPayloadTraitsWithNoBlobBody` and `HttpPayloadTraitsWithNoBlobBody` require (both pass) | Task 7.21. The SDK-owned `SdkRestJsonNoContentWithBlobPayload` and `SdkRestXmlNoContentWithBlobPayload` stay skipped, with "accepted behavior change (user decision)" in `protocol-test-skips.json`. `legacy-disposition.csv` row `rest-core-output.json#6` changes from `ported` to `retired` for both protocols (Requirement 32.10 allows only `ported` or `retired`, and the case it was ported to no longer describes SDK behavior) | restJson1, restXml |
| 5 | `@default` values and client error correction | Not populated: an unset member is null and is not sent; a `@required` output member the response omits stays null | Not populated, as legacy (no change from the legacy SDK; a deviation from the Smithy protocol tests). The 12 skips stay | Tasks 3.34, 7.21 | rpcv2Cbor, awsJson1_0, restJson1 (skips); all protocols (behavior) |
| 6 | Event-stream `eventHeader` members | String-typed headers | Typed headers (`fromInteger`, `fromTimestamp`, …), as the Smithy event stream spec. The decoder also accepts a legacy string header for a non-string member | Task 7.20 (header typing decided: typed) | Event-stream operations once they run on the new path (task 7.20 stages (c)–(e)) |
## `services/sqs` and `services/dynamodb` on the schema path (task 9.2)
**Target.** `core/sdk-core`: `Crc32ResponseValidation` (new), `SyncApiCallPipeline`, `AsyncApiCallPipeline`. `services/sqs`: `customization.config`, `pom.xml`. `services/dynamodb`: `dynamodb/customization.config`, `dynamodbstreams/customization.config`, `pom.xml`. Guard tests in `codegen-maven-plugin` and `test/architecture-tests`. `docs/design/schema-serde/excluded-tests.md` (new).
| Task text or earlier note | This task | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| CRC32 (`x-amz-crc32`) not applied on the new path (task 3.28 row); must move into the pipeline, protocol or transport | In the pipeline: `Crc32ResponseValidation.validate` runs before `ProtocolResponseHandling.handle` for every non-streaming response (success and error) and for the buffered error responses of streaming calls, sync and async. It reuses `Crc32Validation` (checksum of the body as received when `CRC32_FROM_COMPRESSED_DATA_ENABLED`, DynamoDB; otherwise of the decompressed body; gzip decoding; the "no content" case), so the exception type (`Crc32MismatchException`) and messages are the legacy ones. The whole body is read and checked before the protocol parses it, and handed on as an `OwnedResponseBody` so the protocol does not copy it again; an async transport-owned body that is not gzip is checked in place without a copy | One place for both pipelines and every protocol, as the legacy client handlers do it for every protocol. The protocol SPI has no hook for response decoding, and the transport has no client configuration |
| Same behavior as legacy `Crc32Validation` | Same checks and exceptions. One ordering difference: legacy validates when the unmarshaller closes the stream, so a body that is both malformed and mismatching fails as an unmarshalling error there, and as `Crc32MismatchException` here | New (not a policy row). The new order reports the transport error, which is the cause |
| A mismatch is retryable | `Crc32MismatchException.retryable()` is true, as before, but the standard retry strategy (`SdkDefaultRetryStrategy`) retries an `SdkClientException` only if it is a `RetryableException`, an `IOException` cause or a timeout, so neither path retries it with the default strategy | Unchanged behavior: both paths run the same retry strategy on the same exception. `DynamoDbNewPathTest` asserts one attempt |
| SQS (awsJson1_0 + awsQueryCompatible) through the converter, on the new path | `"stagedClientProtocols": true` (task 7.11): the generated builder selects `AwsJson1Protocol.Factory`, all 23 operations are routed and no `transform` package is generated. `x-amzn-query-mode: true` is sent and the `x-amzn-query-error` code is the reported error code (modeled class from `__type`), as legacy. `MessageMD5ChecksumInterceptor` and the batch manager are unchanged and their tests pass | `FACTORIES` stays as it is (task 7.11) |
| SQS module dependencies | `aws-json-client-protocol` replaces `aws-json-protocol` and `protocol-core` (`dependency:analyze` reports both unused). **Runtime classpath change:** neither is in `sqs`'s runtime tree any more; it gains `aws-json-client-protocol` and `http-client-protocol`. japicmp (SQS is in the root `includeModules`) excludes `software.amazon.awssdk.services.sqs.transform.*`: the 23 `@SdkInternalApi` `<Operation>RequestMarshaller` classes are removed (Requirement 17.12); the rest of the diff is 85 modified classes, 3 modified enums, 1 modified interface and 2 new classes, all compatible | As CloudWatch in task 5.1 |
| DynamoDB (awsJson1_0, benchmark baseline) on the new path | `"stagedClientProtocols": true`: all 57 DynamoDB operations are routed (the Kinesis streaming destination operations too, since task 3.34) and `services.dynamodb.transform` is gone. Endpoint discovery (`EndpointDiscoveryTest`) and the account-ID endpoint (`DynamoDbNewPathTest`: `123456789012.ddb.us-west-2.amazonaws.com`) work on the new path | |
| DynamoDB and DynamoDB Streams both route (`routesEveryOperation`) | **Not met for DynamoDB Streams.** Streams shares DynamoDB's module and model package (`shareModelConfig`); `SchemaCodegen` generates no schemas for a model sharer (task 1.27), so the Streams clients keep the legacy marshallers (`services.dynamodb.streams.transform`, 4 classes) and the legacy JSON protocol. Its `customization.config` also sets `stagedClientProtocols`, which has no effect until it has schemas. Generated Streams code is unchanged (`legacy-route-parity/dynamodb.txt` lists only the two DynamoDB builder files) | Blocked on codegen: schemas for a model-sharing service (its own shapes in the shared package, its `Operations` class and service schema, and the shared classes' schemas whose ShapeIds are in DynamoDB's namespace). Planned for phase 6 (task 1.27 note). No D.2 fallback applies: DynamoDB is the benchmark addition (Requirement 33.4), not a protocol slot |
| DynamoDB module dependencies | `aws-json-client-protocol` added; `aws-json-protocol` and `protocol-core` kept, used by the Streams clients | `dependency:analyze` passes |
| Existing module tests pass, exclusions listed | SQS 119 tests, DynamoDB 65 tests, all passing, no exclusions. `PutItemRequestMarshallerTest` is rewritten to the same wire assertion through the client (it called the removed `PutItemRequestMarshaller`); recorded in `excluded-tests.md` | |
| Fallbacks recorded | None used | |
**Guard tests.** `NoTransformPackageTest.SCHEMA_BASED_MODULES` gains `sqs`; `dynamodb` stays unlisted because Streams is not schema-based (the legacy-module test still passes for it, as its Javadoc foresees for a mixed module). `NoMarshallerDependencyTest.SCHEMA_BASED_SERVICES` gains `sqs` (already a dependency of `architecture-tests`); `dynamodb` cannot be listed while its JAR holds the Streams legacy classes. `MarshallerDependencyScannerTest` takes its real legacy fixture from DynamoDB Streams instead of SQS. `legacy-route-parity/sqs.txt` and `dynamodb.txt` list the builder changes (the protocol factory option, `protocol(ClientProtocol)`, `protocolSettings()`, and the removed `ENABLE_FAST_UNMARSHALLER`), as `cloudwatch.txt` does. The size report is unchanged (CloudWatch only; task 9.27 extends it).
**Behavior differences.** Phase 5 policy rows 1–4 are query, restJson1 and restXml behaviors and do not apply to awsJson1_0; row 5 (no `@default` population) is unchanged from legacy. New: the CRC32 ordering row above. No SQS or DynamoDB module test asserts a different legacy behavior.
**For tasks 9.3, 9.4, 9.17 and 9.27.** 9.3/9.4: opt in with `stagedClientProtocols` per service, add each module to `SCHEMA_BASED_MODULES`, `SCHEMA_BASED_SERVICES` and a parity list (`LegacyRouteParity.main <module>` prints it, run from `codegen-maven-plugin` with its test classpath); a service in the root japicmp `includeModules` needs the `transform.*` exclude; add rows to `excluded-tests.md`. CRC32 now applies on the new path for every service, but only DynamoDB sends the header. 9.17: DynamoDB is on the new path for the benchmark, Streams is not; SQS's runtime tree no longer has the legacy JSON modules, DynamoDB's still does (Streams). 9.27: the SQS and DynamoDB JARs lose their `transform` packages (SQS 23 classes; DynamoDB 57 operations' marshallers) and gain schemas; DynamoDB keeps `aws-json-protocol` and `protocol-core` until Streams moves.

## `services/cloudwatch` protocol overrides (task 9.5)
**Target.** `services/cloudwatch`: `pom.xml`, `CloudWatchProtocolOverrideTest` (new). `docs/design/schema-serde/size-report.md` (regenerated). No codegen or runtime change, and no regeneration: the generated builder's `protocol(ClientProtocol)` (task 3.23) already accepts any protocol.
| Task text or earlier note | This task | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| CloudWatch depends on `rpcv2-cbor-client-protocol`, `aws-json-client-protocol` and `aws-query-client-protocol` (Requirement 33.2, A.11) | `rpcv2-cbor-client-protocol` stays `compile` (the generated base builder references `RpcV2CborProtocol.Factory`). The other two are **`runtime`** scope. No CloudWatch class references them, so `dependency:analyze` would report them unused at `compile` scope; the root pom sets `ignoreNonCompile`, so at `runtime` scope it passes ("No dependency problems found") without an `ignoredUnusedDeclaredDependencies` entry | They are loaded through `ServiceLoader` (`ClientProtocolFactories.forId`), which needs them on the runtime class path only. A user who writes `AwsJson1Protocol.Factory.INSTANCE` in their own code declares the protocol module themselves, as for any class they compile against |
| `protocol(...)` overrides to awsJson1_0 and awsQuery work with no regeneration | Both work, sync and async. The builder takes one `ClientProtocol` instance (design A.11; there is no `protocol(ShapeId)` or factory setting, and none was added). A user creates it from the factory class or by protocol ID: `ClientProtocolFactories.forId(ShapeId.from("aws.protocols#awsQuery")).createProtocol(settings, null)`. Both factories ignore the trait argument (null is accepted). After `build()`, `CLIENT_PROTOCOL` is the override instance, and the `CLIENT_PROTOCOL` execution attribute of every call is the same instance | Requirements 12.1, 12.2 |
| How a user obtains `ProtocolSettings` (open since task 3.23) | **Still open; reported, not solved.** The settings the generated builder uses (`protocolSettings()`: `CloudWatchServiceSchemas.SERVICE`, `serviceVersion("2010-08-01")`, `CloudWatchException::builder`) are private, and every route to the service schema is `@SdkInternalApi` (`CloudWatchServiceSchemas`, `CloudWatchOperations.SERVICE`, design 1244). With public API only (`ProtocolSettings.builder().service(ShapeId.from("com.amazonaws.cloudwatch#GraniteServiceVersion20100801")).serviceVersion("2010-08-01").baseExceptionBuilder(CloudWatchException::builder)`) the override works but the settings have no `awsQueryCompatible` trait: the awsJson1_0 override sends no `x-amzn-query-mode` and ignores `x-amzn-query-error` (the error code is then `DashboardNotFoundError`, not `ResourceNotFound`); the class is still resolved from `__type`. A forgotten `serviceVersion` fails awsQuery creation with the task 7.15 `NullPointerException`. The tests use the internal schema, as the generated builder does | Design A.11 specifies only the builder setting. Fixing it needs public API beyond the design (for example a builder overload taking a `ClientProtocolFactory` or protocol ID that the builder resolves with its own settings, or a public settings accessor); left for an API review |
| awsJson1_0 wire shape (task 7.11) | `POST /`, `X-Amz-Target: GraniteServiceVersion20100801.GetDashboard`, `Content-Type: application/x-amz-json-1.0`, `x-amzn-query-mode: true`, body `{"DashboardName":"my dashboard"}`; no `smithy-protocol` header | |
| awsQuery wire shape (task 7.15, SNS in 9.4) | `POST /`, `Content-Type: application/x-www-form-urlencoded` (no charset, policy row 2), body `Action=GetDashboard&Version=2010-08-01&DashboardName=my%20dashboard` (`%20`, policy row 1) and `Action=ListMetrics&Version=2010-08-01&Namespace=AWS%2FEC2`; no `x-amzn-query-mode` or `X-Amz-Target`. Policy row 3 (endpoint path) is not exercised: CloudWatch's endpoints have no path | Phase 5 policy rows 1–3 |
| rpcv2Cbor default, for comparison | `POST /service/GraniteServiceVersion20100801/operation/GetDashboard`, `smithy-protocol: rpc-v2-cbor`, `Content-Type: application/cbor`, `x-amzn-query-mode: true` | Unchanged; `CloudWatchNewPathTest` (12) and `CloudWatchInterceptorOrderTest` (8) pass |
| Outputs (Requirement 12.6) | `GetDashboard` and `ListMetrics` (two metrics and `NextToken`) give member-equal outputs (`equalsBySdkFields`) for all three protocols, with the request ID from `x-amzn-RequestId` (JSON, CBOR) or `<ResponseMetadata><RequestId>` (awsQuery). `equals()` of a whole response also compares `sdkHttpResponse` and the response metadata, so it differs between any two calls | Property 27 (task 9.6) must compare members, not `equals()` |
| Modeled errors (Requirement 12.7) | A 404 missing dashboard throws `DashboardNotFoundErrorException` with code `ResourceNotFound`, status 404, message and request ID for each protocol. rpcv2Cbor and awsJson1_0 read `x-amzn-query-error: ResourceNotFound;Sender` first (`awsQueryCompatible`; `__type` alone gives `DashboardNotFoundError`); awsQuery reads `<Code>ResourceNotFound</Code>`, which the code index resolves to `DashboardNotFoundError` (first of the two shapes with that code, task 7.15) | Same type and code only when the JSON/CBOR response carries the header, as a query-compatible service sends it |
| Schema extensions | `aws-query-client-protocol` registers `AwsQuerySchemaExtensions` and `json-codec`/`xml-codec` their own providers, now on CloudWatch's runtime class path. Extensions are computed on first use per key, so the default rpcv2Cbor client computes none of them; the per-schema extension array is sized by the registry, so it gains slots | No behavior change for the default |
**Size report.** `SizeReportDocumentTest` failed after the dependency change and `size-report.md` is regenerated (JDK 21.0.2, baseline in `~/.cache`). From this task: the runtime-modules table gains `aws-json-client-protocol` (17,521 B), `aws-query-client-protocol` (47,263 B), `json-codec` (78,641 B) and `xml-codec` (123,693 B), and the CloudWatch JAR grows by 254 B (the embedded `pom.xml`). The same regeneration also picks up drift from the phase 7 tasks, which changed modules without regenerating the report (it was last written in task 5.4): `codec-commons` 43,039 → 75,617, `http-binding` 8,106 → 101,960, `http-client-protocol` 37,868 → 83,455, `rpcv2-cbor-client-protocol` 15,440 → 16,096, `schema` 218,802 → 224,109, `sdk-core` 1,222,861 → 1,260,355. The CloudWatch class count and uncompressed bytes are unchanged.
**Tests.** `CloudWatchProtocolOverrideTest` (20): wire shape and output of `GetDashboard`, output and wire body of `ListMetrics`, and the modeled error, each for rpcv2Cbor (default), awsJson1_0 (factory class) and awsQuery (factory by ID), sync and async (18); both IDs resolve to the factories of the runtime dependencies (1); the awsJson1_0 override with public-only settings (1, records the gap above). Module total 302, no exclusions. japicmp (both executions) and `dependency:analyze` pass; CloudWatch's public API is unchanged.
**For tasks 9.6, 9.27 and 9.28.** 9.6: create the overrides as this test does (internal service schema settings, or the public-only settings with the header gap above); compare outputs with `equalsBySdkFields`; for errors, send `x-amzn-query-error` with the JSON and CBOR responses, or the error codes differ (`DashboardNotFoundError` vs `ResourceNotFound`) while the exception type is the same. Error codes that map to two shapes (`ResourceNotFound`) resolve to `DashboardNotFoundError` on every protocol. 9.27: CloudWatch's runtime tree now also has `aws-json-client-protocol`, `aws-query-client-protocol`, `json-codec` and `xml-codec` (see the size report); a per-protocol size comparison should count them as override cost, not default cost. 9.28: no public API change for CloudWatch in this task; the `ProtocolSettings` gap above is the open API question for Requirement 12.

## Prototype dependency trees (task 9.8)

**Target.** `services/pom.xml` (the `ban-smithy-runtime` enforcer execution and the `schema-serde.ban-smithy.skip` property, default `true`); the seven Prototype_Service_Set `pom.xml` files (`sqs`, `secretsmanager`, `account`, `route53`, `sns`, `cloudwatch`, `dynamodb`), each setting the property to `false`; `test/architecture-tests`: `dependencies.DependencyTreeTest`, `dependencies.ReactorDependencyGraph`, and the `dependency-tree.root` surefire property.

| Task or requirement text | This task | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| `maven-enforcer` rule on every prototype service module banning `software.amazon.smithy:*` from compile and runtime scopes (Requirement 1.6) | One execution in `services/pom.xml` `<build><plugins>`, inherited by every service and skipped unless the module sets `schema-serde.ban-smithy.skip` to `false`. `bannedDependencies` with `searchTransitive` and the excludes `software.amazon.smithy:*:*:*:compile` and `:runtime`. Plugin version 3.5.0, as json-codec's `ban-jackson-databind` (task 7.1). `provided` and `test` scopes are allowed | Opting in per module keeps the rule to the Prototype_Service_Set that Requirement 1.6 names; the fleet services (`-P fleet-services`) inherit it switched off. Requirement 1.6 names only `smithy-model`; banning every Smithy artifact is the task's wording and stricter |
| Codegen's Smithy dependencies | Not seen by the rule: they are dependencies of `codegen-maven-plugin` (a build plugin of the `generated-service` profile), not of the service module | Verified: `mvn -o validate` passes on all seven modules |
| `DependencyTreeTest` asserting `dynamic-client` is the only published module depending on `smithy-model` (Requirement 1.7) | Reads the reactor's `pom.xml` files with DOM (no Maven run; about 0.4 s), following every `<module>` including profile modules, with parent-inherited and profile dependencies and `dependencyManagement` scopes. A published module is one managed by `bom/pom.xml`. The walk is transitive over compile and runtime edges, with Maven's rules for optional dependencies and exclusions. Artifacts outside the reactor are not expanded; every `software.amazon.smithy` artifact except `smithy-utils` counts as carrying `smithy-model` | Running `mvn dependency:tree` per module in a test is slow and needs a network or a full local repository. Requirement 1.6's real resolution for the prototypes is the enforcer rule above |
| "other than the codegen modules, the codegen Maven plugin and test modules" (Requirement 1.7) | Allowlist with reasons: `codegen`, `codegen-c2j-to-smithy` (codegen modules), `codegen-maven-plugin`. Unpublished modules (every `test/` module except `client-mock-plugin`) are not checked. `client-mock-plugin` is published and users put it on their classpath, so it is checked as a runtime module. A test fails if an allowlist entry is no longer published or no longer reaches `smithy-model` | `codegen-lite` and `codegen-lite-maven-plugin` are published codegen modules but do not depend on Smithy, so they need no entry |
| "declares a compile-scope or runtime-scope dependency" (Requirement 1.7) | **Interpretation, flagged:** the test is transitive, so a module that reaches `smithy-model` through another reactor module fails, including through `dynamic-client`. Requirement 1.7's literal text checks only declared (direct) dependencies | Requirement 1.6 and design K9 intend no Smithy on runtime class paths other than the Dynamic_Client's. A future module that builds on `dynamic-client` needs a decision and an allowlist entry |

**Negative evidence (not committed).** Enforcer, on `services/sqs/pom.xml`: a `compile` or `runtime` `smithy-model` dependency fails `validate` with `software.amazon.smithy:smithy-model:jar:1.71.0 <--- banned via the exclude/include list`; a `test` one passes; a `compile` dependency on `dynamic-client` fails on its transitive `smithy-model` and `smithy-rules-engine`. `DependencyTreeTest`, with a `smithy-model` dependency added to `core/schema/pom.xml`: the main test fails with 908 paths, for example `sdk-core -> schema -> smithy-model` and `aws-core -> regions -> sdk-core -> schema -> smithy-model`. Both files were restored.

**Tests.** `DependencyTreeTest` (5): the reactor check; a sanity check that the scan finds `dynamic-client`, `sqs`, `schema` and `client-mock-plugin` and the `dynamic-client` path; the allowlist check; a synthetic graph that reports transitive compile and runtime paths and ignores test and provided scope, an optional dependency below the declaring module, an exclusion, `smithy-utils`, an unpublished and an allowlisted module; and a synthetic module depending on `dynamic-client`, which is reported.

## Codec convenience methods (task 9.9)

**Target.** `core/codecs/codec-commons`: `Codec` (the `deserializeShape(byte[], ShapeBuilder)` and `deserializeShape(ByteBuffer, ShapeBuilder)` defaults) and the new `internal.CodecSupport`; `JsonCodec` and `XmlCodec` (their `deserializeShape` overrides removed). Source: smithy-java `core/src/main/java/software/amazon/smithy/java/core/serde/Codec.java` (commit `142eecf4d`).

| Task, design or source | This task | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| Task text: `Codec.serialize(SerializableShape)` "to bytes" and `Codec.deserialize(byte[] \| ByteBuffer, ShapeBuilder<T>)` | No new methods. `ByteBuffer serialize(SerializableShape)` and `deserializeShape(byte[] \| ByteBuffer \| String, ShapeBuilder<T>)` already existed (codec-commons, task 3.5) with the design A.8 and smithy-java names and signatures. `serialize` keeps returning the zero-copy `ByteBufferOutputStream.toByteBuffer()` view (JSON and CBOR override it with their pooled provider paths) | User decision: the task text is shorthand for design A.8; a `deserialize` alias would give one operation two public names, and a rename would break 79 call sites and diverge from smithy-java |
| smithy-java `deserializeShape`: `builder.deserialize(createDeserializer(source)).errorCorrection().build()`; the deserializer is never closed | The defaults call `CodecSupport.deserializeShape(this, source, builder)`: create the deserializer, read, `errorCorrection()`, build, then `close()`. On a failure the deserializer is closed quietly and a close failure is added as suppressed; no value is returned | One implementation for every codec. Closing is how the SDK deserializers reject trailing content (JSON 7.1, CBOR 3.6, XML 7.4). Before this task only `JsonCodec` and `XmlCodec` did this, each with a private copy of the helper; `Rpcv2CborCodec.deserializeShape` accepted trailing bytes |
| `JsonCodec.deserializeShape`, `XmlCodec.deserializeShape` overrides (tasks 7.1, 7.4 rows above) | Removed; both inherit the `Codec` defaults, which behave the same plus the ShapeId context below | Duplicate code |
| Requirement 39.4: a truncated or malformed input throws a `SerializationException` that names the Schema ShapeId | **Deviation from the SDK guideline "MUST NOT catch and rethrow the same exception type", user decision.** On failure only, a `SerializationException` (from `createDeserializer`, the read, the build or the closing check) whose message does not contain the builder's `sdkSchema().id()` is replaced by `new SerializationException("Unable to deserialize <ShapeId>: <message>", original)`. Messages that already name it (type mismatches, range errors) are unchanged | The codecs' syntax errors are raised below the schema level and don't name it: JSON `Expected field name, found: end of input`, CBOR `incomplete map: expecting stream break`, XML `Content is not allowed in prolog`. One place covers all codecs; `createDeserializer` paths used by the protocols are unchanged |
| Non-`SerializationException` failures | Thrown unchanged (not wrapped) | A codec that throws another type for bad input has a bug that Property 15 should find; a builder bug should stay visible. None was seen for the inputs below |
| Requirement 39.5 null arguments | Unchanged: `Objects.requireNonNull` first, `shape`/`source`/`builder must not be null`; nothing is created | |
| Requirement 39.3 / 39.6 | The methods use only the codec instance (its settings, providers and pools); no client, protocol, transport or pipeline. No new state: `CodecSupport` is stateless, and codec instances stay immutable | |

**Tests.** `codec-commons` `CodecTest` (+5, 15 total): the deserializer is closed after building; a closing failure (trailing content) is thrown with the ShapeId and the original as cause; a read failure closes quietly, adds the close failure as suppressed and builds nothing; a `createDeserializer` failure gets the ShapeId; an `IllegalStateException` propagates as the same instance. New per-codec classes, each with a generated-like nested structure (string, integer, nested structure with a list) from the codec's existing test model: `JsonCodecConvenienceTest` (13, `BenchModel.NESTED_STRUCT`, `REST_JSON_1`), `Rpcv2CborCodecConvenienceTest` (16, a local `test#NestedStruct`, `RPCV2`) and `XmlCodecConvenienceTest` (26, `XmlTestModel.NESTED_STRUCT`, `REST_XML`, StAX and native). Each covers: `serialize` byte-identical to the `createSerializer` path; `deserializeShape` equal to the `createDeserializer` path and to the original; `ByteBuffer` sources with a non-zero position and a limit before trailing bytes, read-only and direct, with the position unchanged; null arguments; malformed, truncated and mistyped inputs and trailing content, on both `byte[]` and `ByteBuffer`, as `SerializationException` naming the ShapeId. The concurrency and equivalence properties are task 9.10 (Property 16).

## Response_Generator (task 9.12)
**Target.** `test/client-mock-plugin`: `ResponseGenerator` (public SPI, `forProtocol` through `ServiceLoader`) and `internal.*`: one generator per Supported_Protocol (`RestJson`, `RestXml`, `AwsJson10`, `AwsJson11`, `RpcV2Cbor`, `AwsQuery`, `Ec2Query`), registered in `META-INF/services/software.amazon.awssdk.testutils.mock.ResponseGenerator`. Sources: smithy-java `client/client-mock-plugin` `MockPlugin` (the `ServerProtocol` lookup), `aws/server/aws-server-restjson` `AwsRestJson1Protocol.serializeOutput` and `server/server-rpcv2` `AbstractRpcV2ServerProtocol.serializeOutput` (commit `142eecf4d`).
| Task, design or source | This task | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| smithy-java `MockPlugin` finds a `ServerProtocolProvider` (server-core) and calls `serializeOutput`/`serializeError` | `ResponseGenerator.forProtocol(ShapeId)` loads the SDK SPI once (first registration per protocol ID wins) and throws `IllegalArgumentException` naming the protocol and the registered ones | Design D.4: server-core is out of scope |
| Where the implementations live | All seven in `client-mock-plugin` (`internal`), which depends on `http-binding` and the JSON, CBOR and XML codecs, not on the client protocol modules (those are test scope, for round trips) | Design D.4 ("Implementations live in `client-mock-plugin`"). The generators need only Schemas, codecs and Http_Binding_Serde, so the protocol modules stay independent of the test module |
| smithy-java modeled exceptions serialize `message` as a member; the SDK's do only when the error shape models a `message`/`Message` member | Error bodies use an augmented schema: the error's traits and members copied in order (member indexes are checked to match, so codec field tables and HTTP binding plans by index still apply), plus extra string members written first. awsJson and rpcv2Cbor: `__type` (absolute shape ID) and `message`; restJson1: `message`; restXml and awsQuery: `Type`, `Code`, `Message`; ec2Query: `Code`, `Message`. An extra whose name matches a modeled member ignoring case is not added (that member carries the value). The message is `SdkException.rawMessage()` | The clients read the message from these locations (`ErrorTypeUtils.readMessage`, the XML error payload parsers); without them a message would not round-trip |
| `AbstractRpcV2ServerProtocol`: no `__type` | `__type` written in the CBOR body | Requirement 35.2(b); the client's `TYPE_ONLY_ERROR_PAYLOAD_PARSER` resolves it |
| awsJson `X-Amzn-Errortype` | Written with `HeaderErrorSerializer.AMZN_ERROR_HEADER` (absolute shape ID), as restJson1's `ResponseSerializer` does | One error-header format |
| restXml `<ErrorResponse>` wrapper | `ResponseSerializer` (with `HeaderErrorSerializer.NONE`) writes the bindings and the `<Error>` element (augmented schema with `xmlName` `Error`); the generator then writes the declaration, `<ErrorResponse xmlns=…>` around that body and `<RequestId>`. With `noErrorWrapping` the bare `<Error>` is the body, in the service namespace | `ResponseSerializer` writes one struct per body; an envelope struct would re-implement its binding split. smithy-java has no restXml server protocol |
| Query envelopes | Built as schemas and serialized with `XmlCodec` (`AWS_QUERY_RESPONSE`, service `xmlNamespace`): awsQuery output `<OpResponse><OpResult>`, ec2Query output `<OpResponse>` (augmented output schema with `xmlName`), errors `<ErrorResponse><Error>…</Error><RequestId/>` and `<Response><Errors><Error>…</Error></Errors><RequestID/>`; content type `text/xml` | `XmlCodec.wrapperElements` is read-only |
| Task context: `<ResponseMetadata><RequestId>` in awsQuery outputs, `<requestId>` in ec2Query outputs | Not written | An output value has no request ID to write; the client falls back to the `x-amzn-RequestId` header. Error request IDs (`SdkServiceException.requestId()`) are written in the envelope and as `x-amzn-RequestId` |
| Error status | `httpError`, else `ErrorTrait.getDefaultHttpStatusCode()` (400 client, 500 server); the REST generators get the same from `ResponseSerializer` | Requirement 35.2(a) |
| `x-amzn-query-error` | `<awsQueryError code or shape name>;Sender\|Receiver` for a service with `awsQueryCompatible`, on the restJson1, awsJson and rpcv2Cbor generators; not on awsQuery/ec2Query, whose body carries the code | Matches what the clients parse (`ErrorTypeUtils.queryErrorCode`) |
| Event-stream and streaming-blob outputs | Not handled specially: REST goes through `ResponseSerializer`; the RPC generators serialize the struct | Out of the task's scope; 9.14 queues outputs of the prototype operations, none of which stream |
**Tests.** `ResponseGeneratorRoundTripTest` (29): `forProtocol` for the seven protocol IDs and an unknown ID; `generateError` on a non-error; CloudWatch `DescribeAlarms` output (list of nested structures with a nested list, string list, timestamp, double, integer) through awsJson1_0, awsJson1_1, rpcv2Cbor, awsQuery and ec2Query clients; CloudWatch errors `DashboardNotFoundError` (`httpError` 404, `awsQueryError`, `message` member), `InternalServiceFault` (server, `httpError` 500, `Message` member) and `KmsKeyNotFoundException` (no `httpError`, 400) through the same five; `x-amzn-query-error` and `X-Amzn-Errortype` for awsQueryCompatible CloudWatch; Route 53 `CreateHostedZone` (restXml: `Location` header, 201 from the `http` trait, nested body with list and timestamp) and `NoSuchHostedZone` (404, wrapped); Account `GetAlternateContact` (restJson1 nested body) and `ResourceNotFound`/`InternalServer` (404/500, header-bound `errorType`). Each error is read back with the client protocol as the same class with equal members, message, status and request ID. No prototype output has an `httpPayload` or `httpResponseCode` member or a map; `ResponseSerializer` covers those in `http-binding` `RoundTripTest` (task 7.8), and Property 48 (task 9.13) covers all prototype classes.
## Mock_Plugin (task 9.14)
**Target.** `test-utils/client-mock-plugin` (moved from `test/client-mock-plugin`, design OD-9): `MockPlugin`, `MockQueue`, `MockedResult`, `MockedRequest`, `MatcherRequest` and `internal.ClientConfigurationAccess`; the aggregator `test-utils/pom.xml` (`software.amazon.awssdk:test-utils-parent`); `test/architecture-tests`: `dependencies.DependencyScopeTest` and `ReactorDependencyGraph.dependencyPath`/`childrenOf`. Source: smithy-java `client/client-mock-plugin` (`MockPlugin`, `MockQueue`, `MockedResult`, `MockedRequest`, `MatcherRequest`, `CurrentRequest`, `MockService`; commit `142eecf4d`).
| Task, design or source | This task | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| smithy-java `configureClient` calls `config.transport(...)` and wraps `config.protocol()` in a `MockProtocol` | `configureClient` sets `SdkClientOption.CLIENT_TRANSPORT` and `ASYNC_CLIENT_TRANSPORT` (both, so one plugin serves sync and async clients) on the client's `SdkClientConfiguration.Builder`. The protocol is not wrapped | **Not public API.** `SdkServiceClientConfiguration.Builder` has no transport setter and does not expose the client configuration. `ClientConfigurationAccess` reads the private `SdkClientConfiguration.Builder` field of the generated `<Service>ServiceClientConfigurationBuilder` by reflection (found by type, not name) and fails with `IllegalStateException` for any other builder. A public setter would change `sdk-core`'s public plugin API and codegen; left for a decision |
| `MockProtocol.createRequest` records the input in the context | An `ExecutionInterceptor` (`beforeTransmission`), added with the public `overrideConfiguration` of the plugin builder, puts the request in a plugin-private execution attribute when it is a `SerializableStruct` | The transport reads `API_OPERATION` and `CLIENT_PROTOCOL` from the attributes (design D.4), so no protocol wrapper is needed |
| `CurrentRequest`, `MockService` and the server `Operation` | Not ported | They only carry server-core state for `ServerProtocol`; `ResponseGenerator` takes the `ApiOperation` |
| `MockedResult` sealed interface of records `Output(output, ServerProtocol)`, `Response`, `Error` | Abstract class with a private constructor and final nested `Output(output, ResponseGenerator)`, `Response`, `Error`, created with `MockedResult.output`, `response` and `error`; `MockedRequest`/`MatcherRequest` are final classes with accessors, `equals`, `hashCode`, `toString` | Java 8. `ResponseGenerator` replaces `ServerProtocol` (design D.4) |
| `MockQueue.enqueueError(Throwable)` throws the exception from the transport | `enqueueError(SerializableStruct)` (design D.4) requires a schema with the `error` trait and serializes it into the protocol's error response, which the client deserializes and throws (Requirement 35.5). The direct throw is `enqueueFailure(Throwable)` (`MockedResult.Error`). `enqueue(SerializableStruct)` also takes a modeled exception, as in smithy-java: a schema with the `error` trait goes to `generateError` | The two `enqueueError` overloads would be ambiguous for a modeled exception, which is both |
| No matcher result: `NoSuchElementException("No matcher matched input: …")` | `IllegalStateException("Mock queue exhausted: no queued result or matcher for the call to <operation ID>")`, thrown by the transport, so before any network activity | Design D.4 and Requirement 35.7 |
| No schema check | Before serializing an `Output`, its schema ID must be the operation's output or one of `errorSchemas()`; otherwise `IllegalStateException("Expected one of [<output>, <errors>…] but queued <id>")`. The item was already polled (Requirement 35.8) | SDK addition (design D.4) |
| `sneakyThrows` rethrows the error's cause if it has one | `MockedResult.Error` is thrown as it is | Throwing the cause instead of the queued exception looked accidental |
| `getRequests()` returns `List.copyOf` | Unmodifiable copy taken under the list's lock | Java 8 |
| HTTP client | The client builder still resolves its `SYNC_HTTP_CLIENT`/`ASYNC_HTTP_CLIENT` (plugins run after the builder finalizes the configuration), so a default HTTP client is created if the user sets none. It is never called: the pipeline sends through the configured transport | A plugin cannot run before the HTTP client is resolved |
| Retries | A retried attempt polls another item, as smithy-java's transport is called once per attempt; each attempt's request is recorded | Same behavior |
| Published "from a new `test-utils` aggregator" (OD-9) | **User decision:** top-level `test-utils/pom.xml`, artifactId `test-utils-parent` (packaging `pom`, parent `aws-sdk-java-pom`), module `client-mock-plugin`, moved with `git mv`, artifactId unchanged. Root `pom.xml` lists `test-utils` and no longer `test/client-mock-plugin`; `.brazil.json` adds `test-utils-parent` (skipImport). `bom/pom.xml` already managed `client-mock-plugin` (task 9.12); aggregators are not in the BOM | `software.amazon.awssdk:test-utils` is already the artifactId of `test/test-utils` |
| `DependencyScopeTest` | Every reactor module whose parent is `software.amazon.awssdk:services` (including the `fleet-services` profile) has no compile or runtime path to `client-mock-plugin`, with `ReactorDependencyGraph`'s rules (task 9.8); a sanity test that the seven prototypes and `client-mock-plugin` are found; a synthetic graph (direct compile and transitive runtime paths reported; `test` scope and a non-service module not reported) | Requirement 35.10 |
**Negative evidence (not committed).** With a compile dependency on `client-mock-plugin` added to `services/sqs/pom.xml` in a scratch copy, `DependencyScopeTest` fails with `software.amazon.awssdk:sqs -> software.amazon.awssdk:client-mock-plugin`.
**Tests.** `MockPluginTest` (9), on generated clients with HTTP clients that fail and count every call (all counts stay 0): CloudWatch sync, two outputs returned first in, first out, and both requests recorded with input, method and signature; a queued `DashboardNotFoundError` thrown with status 404, message and request ID; a `GetDashboardOutput` queued for `DescribeAlarms` fails with the expected message and is consumed; an empty queue fails with "Mock queue exhausted" after one recorded request. CloudWatch async: an output, then a modeled error as the cause of the `CompletionException`; an empty queue. Account (restJson1): a raw response is deserialized unchanged (body, custom header, request ID); a matcher that returns null falls through to the queue, whose `ResourceNotFound` is thrown with 404. A non-generated configuration builder is rejected. `DependencyScopeTest` (3).
## Schema-serde JMH benchmarks (task 9.16)
**Target.** `test/sdk-standard-benchmarks` (the module named by the task): `software.amazon.awssdk.benchmark.schema` with `SchemaMarshallBenchmark`, `SchemaUnmarshallBenchmark`, `TraitLookupBenchmark`, `SchemaExtensionBenchmark` and their support classes (`SchemaSerdeProtocol`, `SchemaSerdePayload`, `SchemaSerdeFixture`, `MemberCountExtension`); seven C2J benchmark models in `src/main/resources/schema-codegen-resources`, generated by a second `codegen-maven-plugin` execution; tests `SchemaSerdeFixtureTest` and `ZeroAllocationBenchmarkTest`. SDK addition, no smithy-java source.
| Task, design or requirement | This task | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| The module builds | **It did not build at task 9.14.** Fixed in a separate commit: the `rpc-v2-cbor` rule set read the optional `Endpoint` without `isSet` (now `isSet`, with a regional fallback); `rest-json` and `rest-xml` bound `ContentLength` to `Content-Length` next to a `Body` payload. **User decision:** `ContentLength` is removed from their `PutObjectRequest` and `GetObjectOutput` and from the 7 `PutObject` cases per protocol in `serde-tests/*/input` that set it | The C2J_Converter leaves reserved headers unbound (`c2j#reservedHeader` marker) and the Smithy IntermediateModel builder does not restore the header location, so Smithy reports `HttpPayload` and `ShapeModel.getUnboundMembers` throws for a member beside a payload. **Open codegen gap:** any C2J model binding `Content-Length` (or another reserved header) beside a payload fails the same way. The existing `RestJson`/`RestXml` serde benchmarks now marshall `PutObject` without a `Content-Length` member, so their numbers are not comparable with a Baseline_Build run of the same cases |
| "Per protocol" classes `SchemaMarshallBenchmark`/`SchemaUnmarshallBenchmark` | One class each, with `@Param protocol` (`RPCV2_CBOR`, `AWS_JSON_1_0`, `AWS_JSON_1_1`, `REST_JSON_1`, `REST_XML`, `AWS_QUERY`, `EC2_QUERY`) and `@Param payload` (`SMALL`, `LARGE`) | One benchmark body for all protocols; JMH reports each protocol and size as its own row |
| Design D.3: "uses the existing `serde-tests` payloads, re-expressed as generated prototype-service inputs" | **Benchmark-only services, one per protocol, with the same shapes** (`SchemaBench<Protocol>`, operations `PutPayload` with the payload as input and `GetPayload` with it as output; `stagedClientProtocols`, so every operation is routed and no `transform` package is generated) | No prototype service has nested structure, list, map, timestamp and blob members on one operation; the `serde-tests` cases are DynamoDB- and CloudWatch-shaped and mostly ≤ 10 KB. With identical shapes, the protocols differ only in their wire format, and every protocol uses its own generated classes. ec2Query has a service of its own (it is otherwise covered only by protocol tests) |
| Payload content | `PutPayloadInput`/`GetPayloadOutput`: string `Id`, timestamp `CreatedAt`, blob `Data`, nested `Inner` (string, integer, boolean, string list), list of `Item` and map of `Inner`. `Item`: string, long, double, timestamp, blob, string-to-string map, double list, nested `Inner`. `SMALL`: 1 item, 1 attribute, 1 value, 1 tag, 8-byte blobs. `LARGE`: 400 items, 4 attributes, 8 values, 4 tags, 96-byte blobs. Built once with the awsJson1_0 service's generated classes and copied into each other service's classes through `JsonCodec` | Requirement 34.1: nested structure, list, map, timestamp and blob members in every protocol |
| Marshalling and unmarshalling (Requirement 34.1, design D.3) | Marshall: `ClientProtocol.serializeRequest(PUT_PAYLOAD, input, attributes, endpoint)` to the `SdkHttpFullRequest`. Unmarshall: `ClientProtocol.deserializeResponse(GET_PAYLOAD, …)` on the response the Response_Generator (`client-mock-plugin`, task 9.12) produces for the output; each call wraps the response bytes in a new `AbortableInputStream`, as the existing `*UnmarshallBenchmark`s do. No signing, endpoint resolution or transport. Protocols are created with the `ProtocolSettings` the generated builders use (service schema, version, base exception) | |
| µs/op and B/op | `Mode.AverageTime` in µs (the existing serde benchmarks use `SampleTime` in ns for the cross-language format); B/op with `-prof gc` | Requirement 34.1 units |
| `TraitLookupBenchmark` (Requirement 4.3, 4.4) | `getTrait` on a hand-built member with `jsonName`, `xmlName` and `sensitive` traits: present, absent, and a key whose index is beyond the member's trait array (`RPCV2_CBOR_TRAIT`); and absent `timestampFormat` on the generated `Item$UpdatedAt` member | |
| `SchemaExtensionBenchmark` (Requirement 5.7) | After a `@Setup` call has computed the value: `getExtension` with a key registered by this module through `ServiceLoader` (`MemberCountExtension`, on the generated `Item` schema), and `ApiOperation.idempotencyTokenMember()`, which reads the SDK's operation-members extension | One extension through the public SPI and one SDK extension the pipeline reads per call; the codec extensions are internal API |
| "0 B/op assertions with the GC profiler" | `ZeroAllocationBenchmarkTest` runs both classes with JMH's `Runner` and `GCProfiler` (1 fork, 2 × 500 ms warmup and measurement) and fails if any benchmark's `gc.alloc.rate.norm` is 1 B/op or more. Measured about 2–6 × 10⁻⁵ B/op for all six. It runs in the module's default build (about 15 s), with no tag or profile | 1 B/op leaves room for JMH's per-iteration bookkeeping spread over about 10⁹ operations; one allocation per call is at least 16 B/op |
**Payload sizes** (serialized bodies in bytes, request / response; `SchemaSerdeFixtureTest` asserts ≤ 1,024 for `SMALL` and ≥ 102,400 for `LARGE`):
| Protocol | SMALL | LARGE |
|---|---|---|
| rpcv2Cbor | 343 / 343 | 153,479 / 153,479 |
| awsJson1_0 | 451 / 451 | 180,444 / 180,444 |
| awsJson1_1 | 451 / 451 | 180,444 / 180,444 |
| restJson1 | 451 / 451 | 180,444 / 180,444 |
| restXml | 887 / 836 | 332,537 / 332,486 |
| awsQuery (XML response) | 798 / 902 | 468,774 / 332,552 |
| ec2Query (XML response) | 686 / 863 | 373,539 / 332,513 |
**Running.** Build with `mvn clean install -pl :sdk-standard-benchmarks` (add `-P quick --am` the first time), then `java -jar test/sdk-standard-benchmarks/target/benchmarks.jar 'benchmark\.schema\.' -prof gc -rf json -rff schema-serde.json` (class defaults: 5 warmup and 5 measurement iterations, 2 forks). One case: `-p protocol=AWS_JSON_1_0 -p payload=LARGE`. The paired A/B runs, host settings and JVM options of design D.3 are tasks 9.17/9.18. The Baseline_Build arm has no counterpart of these services; design D.3's baseline arm is the existing `*MarshallBenchmark`s on Baseline_Build jars.
**Tests.** `SchemaSerdeFixtureTest` (21): the size bounds above for each protocol, and each protocol's unmarshalled output equal by fields (`equalsBySdkFields`) to the payload, twice from the same fixture. `ZeroAllocationBenchmarkTest` (1). The existing `RestJson`/`RestXml` serde benchmarks were run once with `-foe true` on all 20 of their cases (the 6 `PutObject` cases included) after the model change.

## Racecar E2E benchmark tooling (task 9.17)

Ported from racecar, `feature/poc/racecar` at `dc26e4aee93` (the local branch and `fork/feature/poc/racecar` are the same commit), as benchmark tooling only (OD-16). No racecar runtime code comes with it.

| Racecar source | Here | Change |
|---|---|---|
| `test/standalone-e2e-benchmarks/src/main/java/.../e2e/*.java` (16 classes) | `test/standalone-e2e-benchmarks/src/main/java/.../e2e/` | Copied. `AppCpuMeter`, `CpuTimeSource(s)`, `Driver`, `MockDdbServer`, `ServerStats`, `Warmup` and the scenario definitions are unchanged apart from the license header and the bind below |
| `Workloads` | same | Only `v2-sync` (Apache5) and `v2-async` (CRT) are kept. The `v1`, `smithy` and `v2-bridged` arms are removed: every arm of a paired run here is a V2 build from a git ref, so all arms run the same workload code |
| `BenchmarkItems`, `MetricsSupport`, `BuildProvenance` | same | V1 and smithy-java converters, the V1 metric collector, the OTel holder and the V1/smithy-java versions removed |
| `Pipeline`, `PipelineCheck` (`STOCK`/`BRIDGED`, required per arm) | `STOCK`/`SCHEMA`, detected and reported | A probe call records the stack under credential resolution; `SyncApiCallPipeline`/`AsyncApiCallPipeline` frames mean the schema path (task 9.2). The result is printed and written to a new `pipeline` results column instead of failing the run, because the same harness runs on stock (Baseline_Build, racecar) and schema (feature) builds |
| `BenchmarkRunner` results | same | New `alloc_bytes_per_op` column: `com.sun.management.ThreadMXBean.getTotalThreadAllocatedBytes()` over the measured window divided by the operation count, for all client-JVM threads (racecar measured B/op with async-profiler in `collect.sh`). Requirement 37.9 compares B/op against racecar |
| `MockDdbServer` bound every interface | binds `127.0.0.1` | The server is unauthenticated (including `/stats`) and the launcher always runs the client on the same host |
| `pom.xml` (V1, smithy-java, OTel, Netty BOM; release 21) | standalone `pom.xml`, release 21 | V2 `dynamodb`, `apache5-client`, `aws-crt-client` at `${aws.sdk.v2.version}` (default `2.49.7-SNAPSHOT`), Jetty `9.4.45.v20220203` (the root pom's managed `jetty.version`, also racecar's), `oshi-core` 7.6.0, `slf4j-nop` 2.0.9. Not in the root reactor and no parent, as in racecar: each arm builds this harness against a different SDK version. Java 21 source level as in racecar (records, text blocks, pattern matching); the SDK under test is still built for Java 8 |
| `scripts/benchmark.sh`, `scripts/server.sh` | same | Copied; usage text trimmed to the V2 clients |
| `scripts/paired-ab.sh` (prebuilt jars) | `scripts/schema-serde/paired-ab.sh` | Arms are git refs (`--arm LABEL=REF`), each SDK built `-P quick -am` in its own worktree with its own local repository chained in front of the shared one, as `build-size-baseline.sh` does; the current checkout's harness is built against each arm's SDK, so the harness is identical in every arm. `--null LABEL=REF` (or a prebuilt jar) runs one build as both arms. `--jar LABEL=PATH` and `--skip-build` reuse prebuilt jars. Same JDK (`JAVA_HOME`) for every build and run; default client JVM options are racecar's (`-Xms2g -Xmx2g -XX:+AlwaysPreTouch -XX:ParallelGCThreads=4 -XX:CICompilerCount=4`); offline unless `--online`. Interleaving and the even-repetition reversal are racecar's. Each run writes its own results file, so arms are identified by run, not by the jar's stamped label (racecar could not label a same-jar null experiment) |
| `pipeline_benchmark2/analysis/scripts/paired_ab_summary.py` | `scripts/schema-serde/paired_ab_summary.py` | `collect` writes `paired-ab.json` from the run files; `summarize` reports per client/scenario and metric each arm's mean, 99.9% error (Student t, as JMH) and repetitions, the paired ratio to the first arm, its spread and the floor. A null experiment's floor is max(|ratio − 1|, spread of pairs) per scenario and metric; `--null` applies it to a comparison ("no measurable difference" within the floor). It flags mixed harness commits, failed runs and a null experiment whose arms ran different pipelines. `analyze()` returns the per-case structure 9.18's report writer reads. `--self-test` runs its stdlib `unittest` tests on fixture data |

Not ported: racecar's `build-jar.sh`, `collect.sh` (profiles), `concurrency-sweep.sh`, `deploy-remote.sh`, `remote-run.sh`, `verify-pipelines.sh`, `verify-responses.sh` and its 40 KB README; `ResponseDigest` (the response diff tool) is ported.

**Running the full experiments later** (on a quiet host; design D.3 names a c6g.metal with pinned cores):

```
# null experiment on the feature build (noise floor per scenario)
scripts/schema-serde/paired-ab.sh --null feature=HEAD --pin-client 0-15 --pin-server 16-31
# Baseline_Build vs racecar vs feature; the first arm is the baseline
scripts/schema-serde/paired-ab.sh --arm baseline=fc8b74e9fe21 --arm racecar=feature/poc/racecar --arm feature=HEAD \
    --pin-client 0-15 --pin-server 16-31
python3 scripts/schema-serde/paired_ab_summary.py summarize <run>/paired-ab.json --null <null run>/paired-ab.json
```

Defaults: 7 repetitions, 50,000 measured and 10,000 warmup operations, both clients, all five scenarios. Output goes to `~/.cache/aws-sdk-java-v2/paired-ab/<run-id>/`; worktrees to `$TMPDIR/aws-sdk-paired-ab-<commit>` (and `-m2`).

**Smoke runs only (task 9.17).** These prove the tooling works end to end; the numbers are not measurements (300 operations, laptop, unpinned, JIT still compiling):

- `benchmark.sh --client v2-sync --scenario small-get --iterations 300` on the feature SDK: server `READY`, `pipeline detected: SYNC SCHEMA`, `alloc_bytes_per_op=72044`. `v2-async` `batch-get,describe-table`: `pipeline detected: ASYNC SCHEMA`.
- `paired-ab.sh --null feature=<prebuilt jar> --reps 2 --iterations 300 --clients v2-sync --scenarios small-get,batch-put`: 8 interleaved runs, all ok, `paired-ab.json` and `summary.md` written, both arms `schema`, B/op ratios 0.994 and 1.001.
- `paired-ab.sh --null feature=HEAD --reps 1 --iterations 300 --clients v2-sync --scenarios small-get` (the ref path): worktree created at `6e613352852`, SDK built `-P quick -am` (63 modules, about 2 minutes on a warm shared repository), harness built against it with the harness and SDK commits stamped, 2 runs ok, both arms `schema`, small-get about 64 KB/op.

## E2E benchmarks and the performance report tooling (task 9.18)

Tooling and correctness tests only. Per the user's decision for 9.17–9.26, the runs below are short smoke runs; the full measurements are deferred to a later run on a quiet host, and `perf-report.md` is marked **PRELIMINARY — smoke data, not measurements**.

| Task text or design | This task | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| DynamoDB `small-get`/`small-put`/`batch-get`/`batch-put` paired A/B, `MARSHALLING_DURATION` reported separately for ≥ 100 KB batch bodies (34.2) | The four scenarios run on the 9.17 harness unchanged. The 25 × ~2 KB batches are about 50 KB, so a new `batch-put-large` scenario (25 × `BenchmarkItems.largeItem`, a medium item plus 12 × 200-character strings; measured request body 104,740 bytes) carries the ≥ 100 KB case. It is not in `all` | Racecar's scenarios keep their meaning and stay comparable with the recorded numbers |
| Marshalling apart from SigV4 payload hashing | With `--metrics`, new `marshalling_us` (mean `MarshallingDuration`), `signing_us` (mean `SigningDuration`, which contains the payload SHA-256) and `request_body_bytes` (largest request body, from `Content-Length` or the body's length, via `MetricsSupport.RequestSizeInterceptor`) RESULT/CSV columns. `-1`/empty without `--metrics` | The publisher and interceptor change what is measured, so timing and B/op come from runs without `--metrics` and the split from a separate `--metrics` run |
| `ClientColdStartBenchmark` in `test/standalone-e2e-benchmarks` (D.3), ≥ 10 fresh-JVM samples (34.7) | `ClientColdStartBenchmark` main class in the harness jar: configures the builder, times only `build()`, then only the first GetItem against `MockDdbServer`; `PipelineCheck` runs after both so its probe client cannot warm them. `benchmark.sh --main ClientColdStartBenchmark --repeat N` starts one server and N client JVMs. Output: a `COLDSTART` line and a CSV row (`build_ms`, `first_call_ms`, `jvm_uptime_at_main_ms`, `pipeline`, commits) | Lives where D.3 puts it and is built against each arm's SDK like the rest of the harness |
| 34.7 "each Prototype_Service_Set service" | DynamoDB only | The harness's stub is a DynamoDB server; the other services need their own stub responses. Gap left for a later task |
| `paired_ab_summary.py` writes `perf-report.md` (34.5, 37.10) | New `report` command: `report RUN_JSON --null NULL_JSON --reference perf-reference.json --notes perf-notes.json --out perf-report.md [--roles baseline=L,racecar=L,feature=L] [--cold-start LABEL=CSV ...] [--metrics-run METRICS_JSON] [--preliminary] [--strict]`. Roles default to first arm = baseline, last arm = feature, an arm labelled `racecar` = racecar | |
| Pass/fail: time ≤ 1.05×, B/op ≤ baseline + floor, B/op ≤ racecar final | Time is `app_cpu_us_per_op`, memory `alloc_bytes_per_op`; ratios are arm means over all repetitions (34.3/34.4 say "mean across all repetitions"). Time: ratio > 1.05 is `FAIL: regression` even when the floor is wider (34.10's "exceeds the threshold"); otherwise a floor > 5% is `INCONCLUSIVE` (34.11); a difference within the floor is `PASS: no measurable difference`. B/op: floor in bytes = floor % × baseline mean. Racecar (37.9): strict `≤`, against the racecar arm measured in the same run (`NOT RUN` without one); recorded racecar numbers are context only, because they come from another host and JDK. No floor means `NO FLOOR`, never a pass | 34.10 and 34.11 overlap when the floor is above 5% and the ratio above 1.05; the stricter reading is used |
| Smithy-java and the D.6 bridged column | Recorded numbers only, in `perf-reference.json`, because `paired-ab.sh` builds V2 refs and the bridged build lives on another branch. "Vs bridged" scales the feature mean by recorded baseline / measured baseline before comparing, so a different host or JDK cancels out to first order. Each difference above 5% needs a note keyed `scenario/metric` in `perf-notes.json` (seeded from the D.6 table); a missing note is shown as **MISSING NOTE** and raised as a warning (`--strict` exits 1) | |
| `RuntimeCodegenMode` deferral (OD-6) in the report template | A fixed section of every generated report (34.8: decision **defer**, and why the Java 8 measurements could not be taken) | |
| `hot_path_check.sh` runs async-profiler on the 34.1 benchmarks | `scripts/schema-serde/hot_path_check.sh` runs `SchemaMarshallBenchmark`/`SchemaUnmarshallBenchmark` (override with `--bench`) under JMH `-prof async` with collapsed output, once with `event=cpu` and once with `event=alloc`. JMH profiles measurement iterations only, so warm-up is excluded. The matcher is `scripts/schema-serde/hot_path_frames.py` (stdlib). It looks only above the `_jmhStub` frame (JMH calls the stub through `Method.invoke`), and fails on `java.lang.reflect`/`jdk.internal.reflect` frames, `ShapeId.hashCode`/`equals` under a `HashMap`/`ConcurrentHashMap` lookup, and `ClassValue` or `Class.hashCode`/`Object.hashCode`/`System.identityHashCode` under a map lookup. A `$$Lambda` allocation is a warning only (D.3's per-member lambda rule; the task names reflect and keyed lookups as the failures). Library from `ASYNC_PROFILER_HOME`, then Homebrew, `/usr/local/opt`, `/opt/async-profiler` | Identity hashes are intrinsics, so a Class-keyed `HashMap.get` may leave no hash frame; the 0 B/op assertions of the 9.16 trait/extension benchmarks cover those paths as well |

**Reference provenance.** `perf-reference.json` copies `pipeline_benchmark3/results.md` from `feature/poc/racecar` (last changed in `7cf5006daad16a03a2622c6629066a68e1ca6b89`; branch tip `dc26e4aee93`), run 2026-09-12 on EC2 c6g.metal with JDK 25.0.4: application CPU µs/op with paired spread and repetitions (5 for small, 3 for batch and describe-table) from `pipeline_benchmark3/data/timing-tables.txt`, and B/op from results.md §4.3 (no spread or repetition count given). Arms: stock 2.54.0 v2-sync, racecar optimized v2-sync (before H11; D.6 gives about 67 µs for small-get after it), the smithy-java bridge, smithy-java 1.5.1.

**Tests.** `paired_ab_summary.py --self-test`: 9 (5 from 9.17, 4 new: the time verdict incl. threshold, floor and inconclusive; the B/op and racecar verdicts; the bridged normalization; the report's missing-note warning, preliminary banner and OD-6 section). `hot_path_frames.py --self-test`: 6 (reflection below the stub ignored, reflection above it flagged, ShapeId under `HashMap.get` flagged but not outside a lookup, `ClassValue` and identity-hash lookups flagged, non-benchmark threads ignored, lambda allocation a warning).

**Smoke runs (not measurements; Apple M4 Pro laptop, unpinned, JDK 21.0.2, JIT still compiling):**
- `benchmark.sh --client v2-sync --scenario small-get,batch-put-large --iterations 200 --metrics`: `pipeline=schema`; small-get `marshalling_us=18.7 signing_us=81.6 request_body_bytes=66`; batch-put-large `marshalling_us=147.3 signing_us=92.9 request_body_bytes=104740`.
- `benchmark.sh --main ClientColdStartBenchmark --repeat 3 --client v2-sync`: `build_ms` 207.0/206.8/209.0, `first_call_ms` 158.1/143.0/139.6, `pipeline=schema`.
- `paired-ab.sh --null feature=<prebuilt jar> --reps 2 --iterations 300 --warmup 200 --clients v2-sync --scenarios small-get,small-put,batch-get,batch-put,batch-put-large --metrics`: 20 runs ok. `perf-report.md` was generated from it with `--roles baseline=feature-a,feature=feature-b --preliminary` and the 3 cold-start samples; its warnings are the same-build roles, two inconclusive floors (13.4%, 28.9%) and fewer than 10 cold-start samples.
- `hot_path_check.sh --bench 'benchmark\.schema\.SchemaMarshallBenchmark' -- -wi 2 -i 2 -f 1 -w 1s -r 2s -p protocol=AWS_JSON_1_0 -p payload=LARGE` (benchmarks.jar built from a `/tmp` copy of the module): cpu, 403 samples, 201 of 204 stacks under the JMH stub, no violations; alloc, no violations, one lambda-allocation warning (`LowCopyListMap$ForBuildable` allocating a `$$Lambda` in its constructor, in the SDK HTTP request builder, not in the serde). PASS.

**Full run later** (quiet host, pinned cores, D.3's JVM options are `paired-ab.sh`'s defaults):
```
# 1. null experiment on the feature build, with the >= 100 KB scenario
scripts/schema-serde/paired-ab.sh --null feature=HEAD --pin-client 0-15 --pin-server 16-31 \
    --scenarios small-get,small-put,describe-table,batch-get,batch-put,batch-put-large
# 2. Baseline_Build vs racecar vs feature (timing and B/op, no metric publisher)
scripts/schema-serde/paired-ab.sh --arm baseline=fc8b74e9fe21 --arm racecar=feature/poc/racecar --arm feature=HEAD \
    --pin-client 0-15 --pin-server 16-31 \
    --scenarios small-get,small-put,describe-table,batch-get,batch-put,batch-put-large
# 3. marshalling vs signing split (34.2), a separate --metrics run
scripts/schema-serde/paired-ab.sh --arm baseline=fc8b74e9fe21 --arm feature=HEAD --metrics --reps 3 \
    --clients v2-sync --scenarios batch-put-large --pin-client 0-15 --pin-server 16-31
# 4. cold start, >= 10 fresh JVMs per arm, from each arm's harness jar (built by step 2 under <out>/jars/)
for arm in baseline feature; do
  test/standalone-e2e-benchmarks/scripts/benchmark.sh --jar ~/.cache/aws-sdk-java-v2/paired-ab/jars/$arm.jar \
      --main ClientColdStartBenchmark \
      --repeat 10 --client v2-sync --append-to-results-file /tmp/cold-$arm.csv
done
# 5. the report (drop --preliminary); --strict fails on any failed or undecidable criterion or missing note
python3 scripts/schema-serde/paired_ab_summary.py report <run2>/paired-ab.json --null <run1>/paired-ab.json \
    --reference docs/design/schema-serde/perf-reference.json --notes docs/design/schema-serde/perf-notes.json \
    --metrics-run <run3>/paired-ab.json \
    --cold-start baseline=/tmp/cold-baseline.csv --cold-start feature=/tmp/cold-feature.csv \
    --out docs/design/schema-serde/perf-report.md
# 6. hot-path check on the 34.1 benchmarks at the class defaults
mvn -o install -pl :sdk-standard-benchmarks -P quick && scripts/schema-serde/hot_path_check.sh
```
`--metrics-run` takes the marshalling/signing split from step 3 instead of the timing run.
## Optimization experiment procedure (task 9.19)
Tooling and correctness tests only. Per the user's decision for 9.17–9.26, the run below is a short smoke run, and the measurements are deferred to a later run on a quiet host. SDK addition, no racecar or smithy-java source.
**Target.** `scripts/schema-serde/optimization_experiment.sh`, `scripts/schema-serde/optimization_experiment.py` (Python 3 standard library; imports `paired_ab_summary` and `racecar_decisions` from the same directory), `docs/design/schema-serde/optimizations/targets.json`, `optimizations/README.md` (the procedure) and `optimizations/example-smoke.md` (the smoke record).
| Task text or design | This task | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| `optimization_experiment.sh <racecar-id> <candidate-ref>` runs a null experiment on the feature build, then a paired A/B | `plan` validates the id against `racecar-decisions.yaml` and `targets.json`. Then the script runs `paired-ab.sh --null feature=<feature-ref>` (default `HEAD`) and `paired-ab.sh --jar feature=<that run's harness jar> --arm candidate=<ref>` with the same pass-through settings (`--reps`, `--iterations`, `--warmup`, `--clients`, `--scenarios`, `--concurrency`, `--jvm-args`, `--pin-*`, `--port`, `--online`, `--skip-build`). Both runs write to one experiment directory (`--out`, default `~/.cache/aws-sdk-java-v2/optimization-experiments/<id>-<time>`) | The feature arm of the A/B is the build the null experiment measured, so it is not built twice |
| "the relevant 9.16 JMH benchmarks" | Per id in `targets.json` (`module`, `include`, `params`). `benchmarks.jar` is built `-P quick -am` in paired-ab's worktree for the commit (`$TMPDIR/aws-sdk-paired-ab-<sha12>`, local repository `<worktree>-m2` chained in front of the shared one). The run order is feature, candidate, feature again, each with `java -jar … -prof gc -rf json`. `--jmh-include`, `--jmh-param` (replaces the mapping's params), `--jmh-opts`, `--feature-jmh-jar`/`--candidate-jmh-jar` and `--skip-jmh` are available | F's signer benchmark is `Sigv4SignerBenchmark` in `test/sdk-benchmarks` (not 9.16's module), so the module is per id. The JMH build uses `install` without `clean`: the worktree is pinned to one commit |
| JMH noise floor | The feature jar runs twice, before and after the candidate: floor = max(\|b/a − 1\|, each run's relative 99.9% `scoreError`).
